- **An exact-pixel check that differs only on lavapipe** (an edge, a rounded corner, a blended pixel): write a ledger entry `Ruling needed: <check> on <distro>: expected <r,g,b> at <x,y>, lavapipe drew <r,g,b>` from its `.pixels` file, and stop for the controller. The check is not loosened, and no tolerance is added, until the user rules (spec § 9, "Exact-pixel checks").
- **A crash in lavapipe's JIT under ASan** (`run.sh:229-231` records that llvmpipe's JIT once crashed ASan runs at random, which is why softpipe was chosen for OpenGL; lavapipe has only the JIT): keep the `.err` files, write them to the ledger, and stop for the controller. Nothing is switched off to get past it.
- **A leak the leak pass lists from lavapipe or the Vulkan loader** with no frame in StreamFlex: the same, with its stack; any suppression is a ruling, listed with its reason (spec § 9, "Leaks").
- Anything else is a port defect, fixed here.

- [ ] **Step 1: The `.deb` must depend on SDL3's libraries, and on no SDL2 one**

In `build.yml`, replace both `Show package metadata` steps (`:178-179`, `:226-227`) with:

```yaml
      - name: Show package metadata
        run: |
          dpkg-deb -f build/*.deb Package Version Architecture Maintainer Depends Homepage
          # shlibdeps derives the dependencies from the binary: SDL3's three libraries, never SDL2's
          depends=$(dpkg-deb -f build/*.deb Depends)
          for lib in libsdl3-0 libsdl3-image libsdl3-ttf; do
            grep -q "$lib" <<< "$depends" || { echo "::error::The package does not depend on $lib: $depends"; exit 1; }
          done
          if grep -q 'libsdl2' <<< "$depends"; then echo "::error::The package still depends on SDL2: $depends"; exit 1; fi
```

(By prefix, `libsdl3-image` and `libsdl3-ttf`: Task 1.1 Step 7 recorded Debian's real binary package names, which spec § 2 gives as `libsdl3-image-0` and `libsdl3-ttf-0` without having checked them.)

- [ ] **Step 2: Measure which SDL3 symbol sets the package's floor, and rewrite the note**

```powershell
docker run --rm -v "C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex:/src:ro" streamflex-test bash -c 'export LC_ALL=C && rm -rf /work && mkdir -p /work && tar -C /src --exclude=./build --exclude=./.git --exclude=./headless-out --exclude=./.superpowers -cf - . | tar -C /work -xf - && cmake -S /work -B /work/build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Release -DCMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX=/usr -DPACKAGE=DEB > /dev/null && cmake --build /work/build --target package -j"$(nproc)" > /dev/null && dpkg-deb -f /work/build/*.deb Depends && nm -D --undefined-only /work/build/streamflex | awk "{ print \$2 }" | sed "s/@.*//" | sort -u > /tmp/used && awk "/^ / { split(\$1, s, \"@\"); print s[1], \$2 }" /var/lib/dpkg/info/libsdl3-0:amd64.symbols | sort -k1,1 > /tmp/symbols && join /tmp/used /tmp/symbols | sort -V -k2,2 | tail -3'
```

Expected: the `Depends` line with `libsdl3-0 (>= <V>)`, then three `<symbol> <version>` lines, the last of which carries `<V>`: the symbol whose version sets the floor (the `.deb`'s floor comes from Debian's symbols file, not from `MIN_SDL_VERSION`). Write both into the ledger, then replace `CMakeLists.txt:191-196` with:

```cmake
    # dpkg-shlibdeps derives every dependency and its minimum version (glibc
    # included) from the built binary. No explicit CPACK_DEBIAN_PACKAGE_DEPENDS:
    # CPack appends shlibdeps' list to it without merging, so each library
    # would appear twice. SDL3's floor comes from its symbols file:
    # <symbol> (<V>) makes the package depend on libsdl3-0 (>= <V>).
    # MIN_SDL_VERSION above is the build's floor, Debian trixie's 3.2.10; no
    # release older than trixie packages libsdl3-0 at all.
```

with `<symbol>` and `<V>` as measured.

### Task 1.3: The return-value flip

SDL3's functions that returned a negative code on failure return `bool`, true on success, and every `< 0`, `!= 0`, `== 0` or `== -1` test of one inverts silently (spec § 1 item 1). Task 1.2a flipped each site as it ported it, so that the build passes; this task audits every call in the port whose result is tested, and proves each failure branch still fires (unit test 4). A site proven by a check that runs in every pass gets that check; one no run can make fail gets two mutants: a **fault** (the call made to fail, so the failure branch must fire) and the **SDL2 form** (the old test put back, which the check must catch).

**Files:**
- Create: `tests/headless/checks/05-sdl-results.sh`, `tests/headless/fixtures/f05-init.ini`
- Modify: `tests/headless/checks/55-settings-pages.sh` :89-95 (the `f55-frame` result)
- Create (outside the repo): `$P/mutants/mutants.py`, `$P/mutants/task-1.3.json`, `$P/mutants/png.ini`
- Test: unit test 4 (the new check, the clause in 55, and the mutant runs); the full headless set

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: Task 1.2a's sites, by the exact text Steps 4-9 of Task 1.2a give them.
- Produces: `$P/mutants/mutants.py`, the mutant driver Task 1.4 and later tasks reuse: `python3 /mutants/mutants.py /mutants/<list>.json` in the `streamflex-test` image, `/src` read-only, `/out` the logs. Each entry: `name`, `file`, `find` (a fixed string; `null` for an unmutated control), `replace`, `count` (default 1), `fixture` (a name under `tests/headless/fixtures/`, or an absolute path), `env`, `keys`, `wait` (default `Begin program loop`), `seconds` (default 20), `expect` and `absent` (regular expressions over the log, multi-line), `exit` (optional), `verdict` (`holds`, `fires` or `caught`).

- [ ] **Step 1: List every SDL call whose result the port tests**

The bool-returning SDL, SDL_ttf and SDL_image functions the port calls, from SDL's own headers:

```powershell
$bool = docker run --rm -v "C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex:/src:ro" streamflex-test bash -c 'grep -hoE "extern SDL_DECLSPEC bool SDLCALL [A-Za-z0-9_]+" /usr/include/SDL3/*.h /usr/include/SDL3_ttf/*.h /usr/include/SDL3_image/*.h | awk "{ print \$5 }" | sort -u > /tmp/bool && grep -rhoE "\b(SDL|TTF|IMG)_[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9_]*\(" /src/src --include="*.[ch]" | tr -d "(" | sort -u | comm -12 - /tmp/bool'
$bool -join ' '
```

Expected (40): `SDL_BlitSurface SDL_DetachVirtualJoystick SDL_DisableScreenSaver SDL_EnableScreenSaver SDL_FillSurfaceRect SDL_GetGamepadButton SDL_GetRenderVSync SDL_HideCursor SDL_Init SDL_InitSubSystem SDL_IsGamepad SDL_LockSurface SDL_PollEvent SDL_RaiseWindow SDL_RenderClear SDL_RenderFillRect SDL_RenderLine SDL_RenderPresent SDL_RenderRect SDL_RenderTexture SDL_RenderTextureRotated SDL_SetError SDL_SetFloatProperty SDL_SetHint SDL_SetJoystickVirtualAxis SDL_SetJoystickVirtualButton SDL_SetNumberProperty SDL_SetRenderDrawBlendMode SDL_SetRenderDrawColor SDL_SetRenderTarget SDL_SetRenderVSync SDL_SetStringProperty SDL_SetTextureAlphaMod SDL_SetTextureBlendMode SDL_SetTextureScaleMode SDL_ShowSimpleMessageBox TTF_FontHasGlyph TTF_GetGlyphMetrics TTF_GetStringSize TTF_Init`. A name not in this list is a call this plan did not foresee: add it to the tables below before going on.

Where any of them is tested, and where any SDL call returning an id, a count or a pointer is:

```powershell
$names = ($bool + 'SDL_AttachVirtualJoystick', 'SDL_GetJoysticks', 'SDL_GetDesktopDisplayMode', 'SDL_GetBasePath', 'SDL_GetPointerProperty', 'SDL_AddGamepadMappingsFromFile', 'SDL_OpenGamepad', 'SDL_OpenJoystick', 'SDL_CreateWindow', 'SDL_CreateRenderer') -join '|'
git -C C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex grep -n -P "\b($names)\(" -- src | Select-String -Pattern '(if|while) \(|!(SDL|TTF)_|[!=]= |[<>]=? |\? |&& |\|\| |= \(?(SDL|TTF)_' | ForEach-Object Line
```

Every line printed is a row of one of the two tables below; a line that is in neither is a site the port did not foresee, audited the same way before going on. Lines whose call's result is not used (a `?:` in an argument, `SDL_SetHint(..., x ? "0" : "1")`) are in the second table as *unused*.

**The flipped calls (SDL2 returned a negative code or an `int`; SDL3 returns `bool`):**

| Site (Task 1.2a's text) | SDL2 (master) | SDL3 | Success proven by | Failure proven by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `init_sdl()`: `if (!SDL_Init(sdl_flags))` | `:249` `if (SDL_Init(sdl_flags) < 0)` | `!` | every check | **real**: `SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=nosuchdriver` (Step 2's check); SDL2 form `init-sdl2-form` |
| `start_gamepad()`: `if (!SDL_InitSubSystem(SDL_INIT_GAMEPAD)) {` | `:1939` `< 0` | `!` | `42-features.sh` `f42-all` (`Gamepad started`) | fault `gamepad-fault`; SDL2 form `gamepad-sdl2-form` |
| `apply_frame_timing()`: `if (!SDL_SetRenderVSync(renderer, wanted ? 1 : 0))` | `:333` `!= 0` | `!` | `55-settings-pages.sh` `f55-frame`, with Step 3's clause | fault `vsync-set-fault`; SDL2 form `vsync-set-sdl2-form` |
| `renderer_vsync()`: `if (renderer == NULL \|\| !SDL_GetRenderVSync(renderer, &vsync))` | `:280` `SDL_GetRendererInfo(...) != 0` | `!` | `42-features.sh` (`the renderer gives on`) | fault `vsync-read-fault`; SDL2 form `vsync-read-sdl2-form` |
| `init_sdl_ttf()`: `if (!TTF_Init())` | `:453` `== -1` | `!` | every check (`Title font:`) | fault `ttf-fault`; SDL2 form `ttf-sdl2-form` |
| `keep_surface_off_chroma_key()`: `if (SDL_LockSurface(surface)) {` | `image.c:275` `== 0` | no `== 0` | `70-chroma.sh` (no key pixel in an icon) | fault `lock-fault`; SDL2 form `lock-sdl2-form` (the success path then takes the else branch, which logs) |
| connect, event: `SDL_IsGamepad(joystick_at(i))`, `SDL_IsGamepad(event.jdevice.which)` | `:1927`, `:2509` `== SDL_TRUE` | the `bool` itself | `42-features.sh` (`Gamepad connected with device index 0`) | polarity mutant `isgamepad-polarity` (both negated: no pad connects) |
| `gamepad_pressed_label()`, `poll_gamepad()`: `SDL_GetGamepadButton(...)` | `:1996` `!= 0`, `:2029` truth (a `Uint8`) | the `bool` | the pad checks (`50-settings.sh`, `62-settings-bindings.sh`: a held button acts) | not a flip: `true != 0` and `1 != 0` agree; the not-held branch runs every frame no pad is pressed |
| main loop: `while (SDL_PollEvent(&event))` | `:2482` truth (an `int`, 1 or 0) | the `bool` | every check | not a flip; the loop ends every frame |
| `fonts_sample()`, `read_font_file()`: `TTF_FontHasGlyph(...)` | `settings_fonts.c:111`, `:220-221` `TTF_GlyphIsProvided` truth (an `int` glyph index) | the `bool` | `59-settings-fonts.sh` (the bundled fonts listed, by their `Aa0`) | `59-settings-fonts.sh:327` (`Dingy ... lacks common punctuation`): both branches run already |

**Calls returning an id, a count or a pointer, and calls whose `bool` is not used:**

| Site | SDL2 | SDL3 | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|
| test hooks: `test_pad_id = test_pad_attach_virtual();` then `test_pad_id != 0 ?`, and `if (id == 0)` in `test_pad_swap()`, `test_pad_plug()` | `SDL_JoystickAttachVirtual` returned a device index, `< 0` on failure | `SDL_AttachVirtualJoystick` returns an instance id, 0 on failure | success: every pad check; failure: fault `pad-attach-fault` |
| `joystick_at()`, `joystick_index()`, `joystick_count()`, `list_pads()`: `ids != NULL` / `ids == NULL` | `SDL_NumJoysticks()`, negative on error | `SDL_GetJoysticks()`, NULL on error | success: every pad check; failure: fault `joysticks-fault` (a failed list reads as no pads, and nothing crashes) |
| `start_gamepad()`: `if (added < 0)` | `SDL_GameControllerAddMappingsFromFile`, `int` | `SDL_AddGamepadMappingsFromFile`, still `int` (-1 on error, `SDL_gamepad.h`) | unchanged, so outside the flip |
| `init_sdl()`: `if (mode != NULL)` | `SDL_GetDesktopDisplayMode(0, &mode)` (its result ignored) | a pointer | interim: Task 1.6 replaces it with the window's pixel size and owns its failure branch |
| `main()`: `base_path != NULL ? strdup(base_path) : NULL` | `SDL_GetBasePath()`, a pointer to free | a pointer SDL owns | the success path, every check. The NULL branch has no mutant: with `exe_path` NULL, `find_default_font()` (`image.c:623`) passes NULL to `join_paths()`, which dereferences it, on SDL2 as on SDL3 (reported, not fixed here) |

| `create_window()`: `SDL_SetRenderVSync(renderer, vsync_wanted ? 1 : 0);`, and every other call of the first list | (various) | `bool`, unused | unused, as on SDL2: a refusal at creation is `check_vsync()`'s to report |

Create `tests/headless/checks/05-sdl-results.sh`:

```bash
# The return-value flip (spec section 1, item 1; plan Task 1.3): SDL3's functions that returned a
# negative code on failure return false, so the port tests SDL_Init with ! where SDL2's code tested
# < 0. A video driver that does not exist makes SDL_Init fail for real, and its failure branch must
# still fire: the line, SDL's reason after it, and exit 1. Every other check runs the success branch.
STREAMFLEX_TEST_NO_MESSAGE_BOX=1 SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=nosuchdriver run_quick f05-init
ok=1
grep -qx 'Could not initialize SDL' "$out/f05-init.log" && ran_clean f05-init 1 && ok=0
result "SDL that cannot start stops the launcher, with SDL's reason (exit $(cat "$out/f05-init.code"))" $ok
grep -A1 -x 'Could not initialize SDL' "$out/f05-init.log" | sed 's/^/      /'
```

In `checks/55-settings-pages.sh`, replace `:89-94` (from `# VSync off, then an FPS limit of 30` to `... ran_clean f55-frame && ok=0`) with:

```bash
# VSync off, then an FPS limit of 30: the frame timing follows live, and both are saved. SDL3's
# SDL_SetRenderVSync returns true on success, so a test left in SDL2's form would log a refusal of
# every change that worked (plan Task 1.3)
cfg=$(writable_config f55-frame)
CFG=$cfg run_keys f55-frame Menu Return Down Down Down Down Down Left Down Right BackSpace BackSpace
ok=1
grep -q 'Frame timing: FPS limit 30, 33 ms a frame' "$out/f55-frame.log" \
    && grep -qx 'VSync=false' "$cfg" && grep -qx 'FPSLimit=30' "$cfg" \
    && ! grep -q '^Could not turn VSync' "$out/f55-frame.log" && ran_clean f55-frame && ok=0
```

- [ ] **Step 4: The mutant driver**

Create `$P/mutants/mutants.py`:

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""The plan's mutant driver (outside the repo, never committed). For each entry of a JSON list it puts
one mutation into a copy of the source, builds the launcher with the harness's test hooks (no
sanitizers, for speed), runs it on a fixture under Xvfb with -d, presses the entry's keys a second
apart, ends it (TERM, then KILL 10 s later), and judges its log. It runs in the streamflex-test image:
/src the repository (read-only), /mutants this folder, /out the logs and the verdicts.

verdict "holds": an unmutated control, whose expectations all hold. "fires": a failure forced, whose
failure branch's expectations all hold. "caught": the old form put back, whose expectations (the
check's) must NOT all hold: the check catches the mutant."""
import glob
import json
import os
import re
import shutil
import subprocess
import sys
import time

SRC, WORK, OUT = "/src", "/work", "/out"
LOG = os.path.expanduser("~/.local/share/streamflex/streamflex.log")


def tree():
    shutil.rmtree(WORK, ignore_errors=True)
    os.makedirs(WORK)
    tar = subprocess.Popen(["tar", "-C", SRC, "--exclude=./build", "--exclude=./.git", "--exclude=./headless-out",
                            "--exclude=./.superpowers", "-cf", "-", "."], stdout=subprocess.PIPE)
    subprocess.run(["tar", "-C", WORK, "-xf", "-"], stdin=tar.stdout, check=True)
    tar.wait()
    subprocess.run(["cmake", "-S", WORK, "-B", f"{WORK}/build", "-DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug",
                    "-DCMAKE_C_FLAGS=-DSTREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS"], check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL)


def mutate(entry):
    if entry.get("find") is None:
        return True
    path = f"{WORK}/{entry['file']}"
    text = open(path, encoding="utf-8").read()
    found = text.count(entry["find"])
    if found != entry.get("count", 1):
        print(f"MUTANT NOT APPLIED {entry['name']}: the text is in {entry['file']} {found} times")
        return False
    open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="\n").write(text.replace(entry["find"], entry["replace"]))
    return True


def log_text():
    return open(LOG, encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").read() if os.path.exists(LOG) else ""


def launch(entry, env):
    fixture = entry["fixture"]
    source = fixture if fixture.startswith("/") else f"{WORK}/tests/headless/fixtures/{fixture}.ini"
    config = f"/tmp/{entry['name']}.ini"
    shutil.copyfile(source, config)   # A settings save writes here, never into /src
    if os.path.exists(LOG):
        os.remove(LOG)
    with open(f"{OUT}/{entry['name']}.err", "w") as err:
        process = subprocess.Popen([f"{WORK}/build/streamflex", "-c", config, "-d"], env=env,
                                   stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=err)
        end = time.time() + entry.get("seconds", 20)
        wait = entry.get("wait", "Begin program loop")
        while time.time() < end and process.poll() is None and wait not in log_text():
            time.sleep(0.2)
        for key in entry.get("keys", []):
            if process.poll() is not None:
                break
            if re.fullmatch(r"F\d+", key):
                subprocess.run(["python3", f"{WORK}/tests/headless/key.py", "key", key], env=env)
            else:
                subprocess.run(["xdotool", "key", key], env=env)
            time.sleep(1)
        time.sleep(2)
        if process.poll() is None:
            process.terminate()
            try:
                process.wait(10)
            except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
                process.kill()
                process.wait()
    shutil.copyfile(LOG, f"{OUT}/{entry['name']}.log") if os.path.exists(LOG) else open(f"{OUT}/{entry['name']}.log", "w").close()
    return process.returncode


def main():
    entries = json.load(open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8"))
    tree()
    xvfb = subprocess.Popen(["Xvfb", ":99", "-screen", "0", "1920x1080x24", "-ac"],
                            stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    env = dict(os.environ, DISPLAY=":99", VK_DRIVER_FILES=sorted(glob.glob("/usr/share/vulkan/icd.d/lvp_icd.*.json"))[0])
    for _ in range(100):
        if subprocess.run(["xdotool", "getdisplaygeometry"], env=env, capture_output=True).returncode == 0:
            break
        time.sleep(0.2)
    wrong = 0
    touched = set()
    for entry in entries:
        for path in touched:   # Every file an earlier entry mutated goes back first
            shutil.copyfile(f"{SRC}/{path}", f"{WORK}/{path}")
        if entry.get("find") is not None:
            touched.add(entry["file"])
        if not mutate(entry):
            wrong += 1
            continue
        if subprocess.run(["cmake", "--build", f"{WORK}/build", "-j", str(os.cpu_count())],
                          capture_output=True).returncode != 0:
            print(f"BUILD FAILED {entry['name']}")
            wrong += 1
            continue
        code = launch(entry, dict(env, **entry.get("env", {})))
        text = log_text() if os.path.exists(LOG) else open(f"{OUT}/{entry['name']}.log").read()
        expected = all(re.search(rx, text, re.M) for rx in entry.get("expect", []))
        clear = not any(re.search(rx, text, re.M) for rx in entry.get("absent", []))
        exited = "exit" not in entry or code == entry["exit"]
        held = expected and clear and exited
        ok = held if entry["verdict"] in ("holds", "fires") else not held
        wrong += 0 if ok else 1
        print(f"{'OK   ' if ok else 'WRONG'} {entry['name']} ({entry['verdict']}): expect {expected}, absent {clear}, exit {code}")
    xvfb.terminate()
    print(f"{len(entries)} entries, {wrong} wrong")
    sys.exit(1 if wrong else 0)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

**Smoke rows (Task 1.17 writes them):** none.

### Task 1.4: Key codes and old configs

`config.ini` stores hotkeys as hex SDL key codes, and SDL3 renumbered one block of them. Read from SDL2 2.0.18's `include/SDL_keycode.h` and SDL3 3.2.10's (`SDLK_*` defines), 211 of SDL2's 240 named codes keep their value (the letters, digits, symbols, F1-F24, the arrows, keypad, modifiers, `SDLK_APPLICATION` and `SDLK_MENU` among them), and the 29 in `#40000102`-`#4000011E` (SDL2's AudioNext through AudioFastForward) do not: 17 moved, and SDL3 reuses their old values for other keys (SDL2's `#40000105` AudioPlay is SDL3's ChannelDown), and 12 (Mail, WWW, Calculator, Computer, the brightness and keyboard-light keys, DisplaySwitch, App1, App2) SDL3 never sends. By physical key, from both versions' `scancodes_linux.h` (evdev, which X11 and Wayland map through) and Windows' scan code tables, a remote's play/pause key sent `#40000105` on SDL2 and sends `#4000010F` on SDL3, and Windows' mute key sent `#40000106` (AudioMute) and sends `#4000007F` (Mute). F1-F24, which Windows' `:exit` registers (`keycode_convert.h`), keep their values. So a translation table is needed (spec § 1 item 2).

**The approach:** every key code StreamFlex holds stays in SDL2's numbering, the one every `config.ini` already holds. A key event's SDL3 code is turned into it as it arrives (`keycode_to_stored()`), and a stored code into SDL3's only to name the key (`keycode_from_stored()`). Old configs mean what they meant, and what StreamFlex writes (a captured key) is the code an SDL2-era StreamFlex reads. A key SDL2 never had, in the renumbered block (Wake, Channel up and down, Media pause and record, AC New ... AC Properties), is stored `0x200` higher, clear of every SDL2 code (SDL2's scancodes end below 512): an SDL2-era StreamFlex never sees that key, and the code matches nothing there. SDL3's key events carry the unmodified key code by default (Task 1.2a Step 4), as SDL2's did.

Create `tests/test_keycodes.c`:

```c
// Key codes as config.ini stores them (spec section 9, test 1): every code a user can bind or a config
// can hold reads as the same key as on SDL2, from the code that key sends on SDL3, and names as that
// key. The keys that moved are read by physical key from SDL2 2.26.5's and SDL3 3.2.10's scancode
// tables (scancodes_linux.h, Windows' scan codes and SDL2's VKeytoScancode).
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "keycodes.h"

// Each physical key whose SDL3 code is not SDL2's: the code it sends on SDL3, and the one SDL2 sent
static const struct { uint32_t sdl3, sdl2; } MOVED[] = {
    { 0x4000010B, 0x40000102 },   // Next track (KEY_NEXTSONG, VK_MEDIA_NEXT_TRACK): SDL2's AudioNext
    { 0x4000010C, 0x40000103 },   // Previous track: AudioPrev
    { 0x4000010D, 0x40000104 },   // Stop (KEY_STOPCD, VK_MEDIA_STOP): AudioStop
    { 0x4000010F, 0x40000105 },   // Play/pause (KEY_PLAYPAUSE, VK_MEDIA_PLAY_PAUSE): AudioPlay
    { 0x40000106, 0x40000105 },   // Play (KEY_PLAY): AudioPlay as well, which SDL2 gave both
    { 0x40000110, 0x40000107 },   // Media select (KEY_MEDIA, VK_LAUNCH_MEDIA_SELECT): MediaSelect
    { 0x40000118, 0x4000010C },   // AC Search
    { 0x40000119, 0x4000010D },   // AC Home
    { 0x4000011A, 0x4000010E },   // AC Back
    { 0x4000011B, 0x4000010F },   // AC Forward
    { 0x4000011C, 0x40000110 },   // AC Stop
    { 0x4000011D, 0x40000111 },   // AC Refresh
    { 0x4000011E, 0x40000112 },   // AC Bookmarks
    { 0x4000010E, 0x40000119 },   // Eject (KEY_EJECTCD): Eject
    { 0x40000102, 0x4000011A },   // Sleep (KEY_SLEEP): Sleep
    { 0x4000010A, 0x4000011D },   // Rewind (KEY_REWIND): AudioRewind
    { 0x40000109, 0x4000011E },   // Fast forward (KEY_FASTFORWARD): AudioFastForward
};

// SDL3's keys SDL2 never had whose codes SDL2 used for other keys: stored 0x200 higher
static const uint32_t SDL3_ONLY[] = { 0x40000103, 0x40000104, 0x40000105, 0x40000107, 0x40000108, 0x40000111,
                                      0x40000112, 0x40000113, 0x40000114, 0x40000115, 0x40000116, 0x40000117 };

// SDL2's keys SDL3 never sends: Mail, WWW, Calculator, Computer, the brightness keys, DisplaySwitch,
// the keyboard-light keys, App1 and App2
static const uint32_t SDL2_ONLY[] = { 0x40000108, 0x40000109, 0x4000010A, 0x4000010B, 0x40000113, 0x40000114,
                                      0x40000115, 0x40000116, 0x40000117, 0x40000118, 0x4000011B, 0x4000011C };

// The F keys, F1 to F24: Windows' :exit registers F1-F11 and F13-F24 (platform/keycode_convert.h)
static const uint32_t F_KEYS[] = {
    0x4000003A, 0x4000003B, 0x4000003C, 0x4000003D, 0x4000003E, 0x4000003F, 0x40000040, 0x40000041, 0x40000042,
    0x40000043, 0x40000044, 0x40000045, 0x40000068, 0x40000069, 0x4000006A, 0x4000006B, 0x4000006C, 0x4000006D,
    0x4000006E, 0x4000006F, 0x40000070, 0x40000071, 0x40000072, 0x40000073
};

#define COUNT(a) ((int) (sizeof(a) / sizeof((a)[0])))

// A function to tell whether a code is stored and named as itself, on both platforms
static bool unmoved(uint32_t code, bool skip_windows_mute)
{
    return keycode_to_stored(code, false) == code && (skip_windows_mute || keycode_to_stored(code, true) == code)
        && keycode_from_stored(code) == code;
}

// A function to test the moved keys: the SDL3 code is stored as SDL2's, on both platforms, and names back
static void test_moved(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < COUNT(MOVED); i++) {
        CHECK(keycode_to_stored(MOVED[i].sdl3, false) == MOVED[i].sdl2);
        CHECK(keycode_to_stored(MOVED[i].sdl3, true) == MOVED[i].sdl2);
        CHECK(keycode_to_stored(keycode_from_stored(MOVED[i].sdl2), false) == MOVED[i].sdl2);
    }
    // AudioPlay's code names the play/pause key, the one a remote has
    CHECK(keycode_from_stored(0x40000105) == 0x4000010F);
    CHECK(keycode_from_stored(0x40000102) == 0x4000010B);
}

// A function to test the mute key: AudioMute on Windows under SDL2, Mute on Linux
static void test_mute(void)
{
    CHECK(keycode_to_stored(0x4000007F, true) == 0x40000106);
    CHECK(keycode_to_stored(0x4000007F, false) == 0x4000007F);
    CHECK(keycode_from_stored(0x40000106) == 0x4000007F);
    CHECK(keycode_from_stored(0x4000007F) == 0x4000007F);
}

// A function to test SDL3's own keys: stored clear of every code SDL2 could send, and named back
static void test_sdl3_only(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < COUNT(SDL3_ONLY); i++) {
        uint32_t stored = keycode_to_stored(SDL3_ONLY[i], false);
        CHECK(stored == SDL3_ONLY[i] + 0x200);
        CHECK(keycode_to_stored(SDL3_ONLY[i], true) == stored);
        CHECK(stored > 0x400001FF);
        CHECK(keycode_from_stored(stored) == SDL3_ONLY[i]);
    }
}

// A function to test SDL2's keys SDL3 never sends: no SDL3 key names them
static void test_sdl2_only(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < COUNT(SDL2_ONLY); i++)
        CHECK(keycode_from_stored(SDL2_ONLY[i]) == 0);
}

// A function to test every code no key moved: Unicode key codes, the scancode keys before and after
// the renumbered block, and SDL3's extended keys, each stored and named as itself
static void test_unmoved(void)
{
    int wrong = 0;
    for (uint32_t code = 0; code <= 0x10FFFF; code++)
        wrong += !unmoved(code, false);
    for (uint32_t code = 0x40000000; code <= 0x40000101; code++)
        wrong += !unmoved(code, code == 0x4000007F);
    for (uint32_t code = 0x4000011F; code <= 0x400001FF; code++)
        wrong += !unmoved(code, false);
    for (uint32_t code = 0x20000001; code <= 0x20000007; code++)
        wrong += !unmoved(code, false);
    CHECK_INT(wrong, 0);
}

// A function to test the F keys behind Windows' :exit
static void test_f_keys(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < COUNT(F_KEYS); i++)
        CHECK(unmoved(F_KEYS[i], false));
}

int main(void)
{
    test_moved();
    test_mute();
    test_sdl3_only();
    test_sdl2_only();
    test_unmoved();
    test_f_keys();
    return check_report();
}
```

Create `src/keycodes.h`:

```c
// Key codes as config.ini stores them: SDL2's numbering, which every config written so far holds.
// SDL3 renumbered the media and browser keys (SDL2's AudioNext ... AudioFastForward, #40000102 to
// #4000011E), so a key event's SDL3 code is turned into the stored one as it arrives, and a stored
// code into SDL3's only to name its key. A key SDL2 never had, in that block, is stored 0x200 higher,
// clear of every SDL2 code. Pure: no SDL, no globals.
#ifndef KEYCODES_H
#define KEYCODES_H

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdint.h>

#ifdef _WIN32
#define KEYCODES_WINDOWS true   // SDL2 named Windows' mute key AudioMute (VK_VOLUME_MUTE); Linux's was Mute
#else
#define KEYCODES_WINDOWS false
#endif

uint32_t keycode_to_stored(uint32_t sdl3, bool windows);   // A key event's SDL3 code, as config.ini stores it
uint32_t keycode_from_stored(uint32_t stored);              // The SDL3 code naming a stored code; 0 for a key SDL3 never sends

#endif
```

Create `src/keycodes.c`:

```c
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include "keycodes.h"

#define FIRST_MOVED 0x40000102u      // SDL2's AudioNext, where the block SDL3 renumbered starts...
#define LAST_MOVED 0x4000011Eu       // ...to SDL2's AudioFastForward, where it ends
#define SDL3_ONLY 0x200u             // How far up a key SDL2 never had is stored, clear of every SDL2 code
#define SDL3_MUTE 0x4000007Fu        // Mute: SDL_SCANCODE_MUTE's key code in both
#define SDL2_AUDIOMUTE 0x40000106u   // Windows' mute key under SDL2 (VK_VOLUME_MUTE); Linux's was Mute

// Each physical key SDL3 sends another code for than SDL2 did: SDL3's code, and SDL2's, which config.ini
// stores (SDL2 2.26.5 and SDL3 3.2.10, by scancodes_linux.h's evdev names, which X11 and Wayland map
// through, and Windows' scan codes). Where SDL2 gave two keys one code, the key a remote sends comes
// first: play/pause before play.
static const struct { uint32_t sdl3, stored; } MOVED[] = {
    { 0x4000010Bu, 0x40000102u },   // Media next track: SDL2's AudioNext
    { 0x4000010Cu, 0x40000103u },   // Media previous track: AudioPrev
    { 0x4000010Du, 0x40000104u },   // Media stop: AudioStop
    { 0x4000010Fu, 0x40000105u },   // Media play/pause: AudioPlay
    { 0x40000106u, 0x40000105u },   // Media play (KEY_PLAY): AudioPlay as well
    { 0x40000110u, 0x40000107u },   // Media select: MediaSelect
    { 0x40000118u, 0x4000010Cu },   // AC search
    { 0x40000119u, 0x4000010Du },   // AC home
    { 0x4000011Au, 0x4000010Eu },   // AC back
    { 0x4000011Bu, 0x4000010Fu },   // AC forward
    { 0x4000011Cu, 0x40000110u },   // AC stop
    { 0x4000011Du, 0x40000111u },   // AC refresh
    { 0x4000011Eu, 0x40000112u },   // AC bookmarks
    { 0x4000010Eu, 0x40000119u },   // Media eject: Eject
    { 0x40000102u, 0x4000011Au },   // Sleep
    { 0x4000010Au, 0x4000011Du },   // Media rewind: AudioRewind
    { 0x40000109u, 0x4000011Eu },   // Media fast forward: AudioFastForward
};
#define MOVED_COUNT (sizeof(MOVED) / sizeof(MOVED[0]))

// A function to give the code config.ini stores for a key event's SDL3 key code
uint32_t keycode_to_stored(uint32_t sdl3, bool windows)
{
    if (windows && sdl3 == SDL3_MUTE)
        return SDL2_AUDIOMUTE;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < MOVED_COUNT; i++) {
        if (MOVED[i].sdl3 == sdl3)
            return MOVED[i].stored;
    }
    if (sdl3 >= FIRST_MOVED && sdl3 <= LAST_MOVED)
        return sdl3 + SDL3_ONLY;     // A key SDL2 never had: Wake, Channel up, Media pause, AC New and the rest
    return sdl3;
}

// A function to give the SDL3 key code that names a stored code; 0 for a key SDL3 never sends
uint32_t keycode_from_stored(uint32_t stored)
{
    if (stored == SDL2_AUDIOMUTE)
        return SDL3_MUTE;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < MOVED_COUNT; i++) {
        if (MOVED[i].stored == stored)
            return MOVED[i].sdl3;    // The first of two: play/pause names AudioPlay's code
    }
    if (stored >= FIRST_MOVED + SDL3_ONLY && stored <= LAST_MOVED + SDL3_ONLY &&
        keycode_to_stored(stored - SDL3_ONLY, false) == stored)
        return stored - SDL3_ONLY;
    if (stored >= FIRST_MOVED && stored <= LAST_MOVED)
        return 0;                    // SDL2's Mail, WWW, Calculator and the rest: SDL3 sends none of them
    return stored;
}
```

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# Plan Task 1.4's unit-test mutants: each one-line change to src/keycodes.c must fail test_keycodes,
# and the unmutated source (control) must pass. Runs in the streamflex-test image, /src read-only.
set -u
try() {   # NAME SED-EXPRESSION (empty for the control)
    rm -rf /work && mkdir -p /work
    tar -C /src --exclude=./build --exclude=./.git --exclude=./headless-out --exclude=./.superpowers -cf - . | tar -C /work -xf -
    if [ -n "$2" ]; then
        sed -i "$2" /work/src/keycodes.c
        cmp -s /src/src/keycodes.c /work/src/keycodes.c && { echo "MUTANT NOT APPLIED $1"; return; }
    fi
    cmake -S /work -B /work/build > /dev/null && cmake --build /work/build --target test_keycodes > /tmp/build.log 2>&1 \
        || { echo "BUILD FAILED $1"; tail -5 /tmp/build.log; return; }
    if ctest --test-dir /work/build -R '^keycodes$' --output-on-failure > /tmp/ctest.log 2>&1; then
        echo "PASSED $1"
    else
        echo "FAILED $1: $(grep -m1 'CHECK failed\| is ' /tmp/ctest.log)"
    fi
}
try control ''
try play-pause-row '/{ 0x4000010Fu, 0x40000105u }/d'
try windows-mute 's/if (windows \&\& sdl3 == SDL3_MUTE)/if (false \&\& sdl3 == SDL3_MUTE)/'
try sdl3-only-offset 's/#define SDL3_ONLY 0x200u/#define SDL3_ONLY 0x0u/'
try sdl2-only-keys 's/return 0;  *\/\/ SDL2.s Mail/return stored;   \/\/ SDL2 Mail/'
```

Create `tests/test_bindings_fixtures.c`:

```c
// 3b's bindings fixtures, written for SDL2-era files, load to exactly the bindings they held (spec
// section 9, test 2): each line's key, code and command, and every keyboard code reads as the same key
// from the code that key sends on SDL3 (keycodes.h), on both platforms
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "inidoc.h"
#include "bindings.h"
#include "fileio.h"
#include "alloc.h"
#include "keycodes.h"

typedef struct { const char *key; int code; const char *command; } Expected;   // code -1: the label's index

// A function to load one device's lines of a fixture as the settings screen loads them
// (settings_screen.c's load_section()), and check them against the bindings they must give
static void check_fixture(const char *name, BindingsDevice device, const Expected *expected, int count)
{
    char path[1024];
    snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s/%s.ini", FIXTURES, name);
    size_t length = 0;
    char *text = fileio_read_all(path, &length);
    CHECK(text != NULL);
    if (text == NULL)
        return;
    IniDoc *doc = inidoc_parse(text, length);
    alloc_free(text);
    static const char *const skip[] = { "Enabled", "DeviceIndex", "ControllerMappingsFile", NULL };
    IniDocItem items[16];
    int lines = inidoc_list(doc, device == BINDINGS_KEYBOARD ? "Hotkeys" : "Gamepad",
                            device == BINDINGS_KEYBOARD ? NULL : skip, items, 16);
    Bindings *b = bindings_create(false, true);
    CHECK(bindings_load(b, device, items, lines));
    CHECK_INT(bindings_count(b, device), count);
    for (int i = 0; i < count && i < bindings_count(b, device); i++) {
        const Binding *binding = bindings_at(b, device, i);
        CHECK_STR(binding->key, expected[i].key);
        CHECK_INT(binding->code, expected[i].code >= 0 ? expected[i].code : bindings_label_index(expected[i].key));
        CHECK_STR(binding->command, expected[i].command);
        if (device == BINDINGS_KEYBOARD) {
            uint32_t code = (uint32_t) binding->code;
            CHECK(keycode_to_stored(keycode_from_stored(code), false) == code);
            CHECK(keycode_to_stored(keycode_from_stored(code), true) == code);
        }
    }
    bindings_free(b);
    inidoc_free(doc);
}

int main(void)
{
    static const Expected empty[] = { { "", 0x4000003C, ":home" } };
    static const Expected exits[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x4000003E, ":exit" }, { "Hotkey2", 0x4000003F, ":exit" },
                                      { "Hotkey3", 0x40000040, ":home" } };
    static const Expected note[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x4000003E, ":home" }, { "Hotkey2", 0x4000003F, ":home" } };
    static const Expected select[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x40000040, ":select" } };
    static const Expected up[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x4000003A, ":up" } };
    static const Expected word[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x4000003E, ":down now" } };
    static const Expected media[] = { { "Hotkey1", 0x40000105, ":quit" }, { "Hotkey2", 0x40000102, ":right" },
                                      { "Hotkey3", 0x40000103, ":left" }, { "Hotkey4", 0x40000104, ":home" },
                                      { "Hotkey5", 0x4000010E, ":back" } };
    static const Expected button_a[] = { { "ButtonA", -1, ":select" } };
    static const Expected padok[] = { { "ButtonA", -1, ":select" }, { "ButtonY", -1, ":quit" } };
    check_fixture("f62-empty", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, empty, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-exit", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, exits, 3);
    check_fixture("f62-note", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, note, 2);
    check_fixture("f62-select", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, select, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-up", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, up, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-uppad", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, up, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-word", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, word, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-media", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, media, 5);
    check_fixture("f62-keys", BINDINGS_KEYBOARD, NULL, 0);
    check_fixture("f62-keys", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, NULL, 0);
    check_fixture("f62-pad", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, NULL, 0);
    check_fixture("f62-padoff", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, NULL, 0);
    check_fixture("f62-gpoff", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, button_a, 1);
    check_fixture("f62-padok", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, padok, 2);
    check_fixture("f62-uppad", BINDINGS_GAMEPAD, button_a, 1);
    return check_report();
}
```

```c

// A label reads the button SDL2 numbered for it: SDL3 names the face buttons by position and keeps
// SDL2's numbers (SDL_CONTROLLER_BUTTON_A was 0, as SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_SOUTH is), so a ButtonA in an
// SDL2-era config is the same button (spec section 1, item 7)
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_south_is_sdl2_a, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_SOUTH == 0);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_east_is_sdl2_b, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_EAST == 1);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_west_is_sdl2_x, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_WEST == 2);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_north_is_sdl2_y, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_NORTH == 3);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_back, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_BACK == 4);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_guide, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_GUIDE == 5);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_start, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_START == 6);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_left_stick, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_LEFT_STICK == 7);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_right_stick, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_RIGHT_STICK == 8);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_left_shoulder, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_LEFT_SHOULDER == 9);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_right_shoulder, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_RIGHT_SHOULDER == 10);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_dpad_up, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_DPAD_UP == 11);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_dpad_down, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_DPAD_DOWN == 12);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_dpad_left, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_DPAD_LEFT == 13);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_dpad_right, SDL_GAMEPAD_BUTTON_DPAD_RIGHT == 14);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_left_x, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_LEFTX == 0);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_left_y, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_LEFTY == 1);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_right_x, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_RIGHTX == 2);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_right_y, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_RIGHTY == 3);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_left_trigger, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_LEFT_TRIGGER == 4);
SDL_COMPILE_TIME_ASSERT(pad_right_trigger, SDL_GAMEPAD_AXIS_RIGHT_TRIGGER == 5);
```

(Position is SDL2's meaning on Xbox and PlayStation pads. On Nintendo pads SDL2's default, `SDL_HINT_GAMECONTROLLER_USE_BUTTON_LABELS` "1", swapped A/B and X/Y to match the labels, and SDL3 has no such hint: see this group's open question. Button labels are item 26's.)

## Phase 1, Tasks 1.5 to 1.7 (draft)

**Drafted against master `ade1a12`.** Every `file:line` below is master's. Tasks 1.1 to 1.4 have landed before these run: the code is on SDL3 names, the build finds SDL3, and `create_window()` (master `src/launcher.c:356-393`) makes the renderer with the platform's Vulkan-first list. Where a step replaces code that Task 1.2 ported, it replaces the whole function or the named line range, so the exact text Task 1.2 left there does not matter; where a step must keep a line Task 1.2 wrote, it says so and names the master line.

**Split:** 1.5 is 1.5a (the pure `window_plan()`) and 1.5b (`create_renderer()` and its legs). 1.6 is 1.6a (the screen size from the window) and 1.6b (the one pending reload and the size-change rules). 1.7 is 1.7a (render resets) and 1.7b (a lost device and its new renderer). Each half can be rejected while its neighbor is approved.

**Commands every task here uses.** No task step uses `cd`.


  ```c
  typedef enum { WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT } WindowKind;
  typedef struct {
      WindowKind kind;
      const char *render_list;   // NULL: pass no list (SDL_RENDER_DRIVER chose)
      bool mark_fullscreen;      // ITaskbarList2::MarkFullscreenWindow on the HWND
      bool refused;              // An override outside the transparent list: use the opaque window
  ```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test `tests/test_winplan.c`**

```c
// Unit test 14 (spec section 9): the window a background needs and the renderers that may draw it.
// winplan.c is pure, so this links it alone, as test_layout links layout.c.
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "winplan.h"

// ModeBackground's values (launcher.h, which needs SDL): Color, Image, Slideshow, Transparent
#define BG_COLOR 0
#define BG_IMAGE 1
#define BG_SLIDESHOW 2
#define BG_TRANSPARENT WINPLAN_BACKGROUND_TRANSPARENT

// A plan is checked field by field, as a macro so a failure names the caller's line: its kind, its
// list (NULL for none), whether it marks the window, and whether it was refused
#define CHECK_PLAN(plan, kind_, list_, mark_, refused_) do { \
    WindowPlan plan_ = (plan); \
    const char *list_wanted_ = (list_); \
    CHECK_INT((int) plan_.kind, (int) (kind_)); \
    if (list_wanted_ == NULL) \
        CHECK(plan_.render_list == NULL); \
    else \
        CHECK_STR(plan_.render_list, list_wanted_); \
    CHECK(plan_.mark_fullscreen == (mark_)); \
    CHECK(plan_.refused == (refused_)); \
} while (0)

// Transparent on Windows: one row short, the transparent list, and the taskbar mark; an empty
// SDL_RENDER_DRIVER counts as unset
static void test_transparent_on_windows(void)
{
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, NULL), WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT, RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT, true, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, ""), WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT, RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT, true, false);
}

// Every opaque background on Windows: true fullscreen on the Vulkan-first list, never marked
static void test_opaque_on_windows(void)
{
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_COLOR, true, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_IMAGE, true, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_SLIDESHOW, true, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS, false, false);
}

// Every background on Linux, Transparent included (the chroma key stays there): fullscreen on
// Linux's list, never marked
static void test_linux(void)
{
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_COLOR, false, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_UNIX, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_IMAGE, false, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_UNIX, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_SLIDESHOW, false, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_UNIX, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, false, NULL), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, RENDER_LIST_UNIX, false, false);
}

// SDL_RENDER_DRIVER takes the list away and changes nothing else: not the window's kind, not the mark
static void test_override_takes_only_the_list(void)
{
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_COLOR, true, "opengl"), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, false, "opengl"), WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "direct3d11"), WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT, NULL, true, false);
    CHECK_PLAN(window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "vulkan"), WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT, NULL, true, false);
}

// An override outside the transparent list is refused by its name alone, for the opaque window,
// with the reason the Video: line gives; a name is matched whole, never as a prefix
static void test_override_outside_the_transparent_list(void)
{
    WindowPlan plan = window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "direct3d12");
    CHECK_PLAN(plan, WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, true);
    CHECK_STR(plan.reason, "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=direct3d12 is not in the transparent list");
    plan = window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "direct3d");
    CHECK_PLAN(plan, WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, true);
    CHECK_STR(plan.reason, "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=direct3d is not in the transparent list");
    plan = window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "opengl");
    CHECK_PLAN(plan, WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, true);
    CHECK_STR(plan.reason, "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=opengl is not in the transparent list");
    plan = window_plan(BG_TRANSPARENT, true, "direct3d1");
    CHECK_PLAN(plan, WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, NULL, false, true);
    CHECK_STR(plan.reason, "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=direct3d1 is not in the transparent list");
}

// The Renderer: line (spec section 3, Logging): the list's first choice plain, any other with the
// first named as unavailable, and an override's said to come from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER
static void test_renderer_line(void)
{
    char line[160];
    winplan_renderer_line(RENDER_LIST_UNIX, "vulkan", line, sizeof(line));
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: vulkan");
    winplan_renderer_line(RENDER_LIST_UNIX, "opengl", line, sizeof(line));
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: opengl (vulkan was unavailable)");
    winplan_renderer_line(RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS, "direct3d11", line, sizeof(line));
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: direct3d11 (vulkan was unavailable)");
    winplan_renderer_line(RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT, "direct3d11", line, sizeof(line));
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: direct3d11 (vulkan was unavailable)");
    winplan_renderer_line(NULL, "opengl", line, sizeof(line));
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: opengl (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)");
    winplan_renderer_line(RENDER_LIST_UNIX, "vulk", line, sizeof(line));   // A prefix of the first is not it
    CHECK_STR(line, "Renderer: vulk (vulkan was unavailable)");
}

int main(void)
{
    test_transparent_on_windows();
    test_opaque_on_windows();
    test_linux();
    test_override_takes_only_the_list();
    test_override_outside_the_transparent_list();
    test_renderer_line();
    return check_report();
}
```

```c
// The window a background needs and the renderers that may draw it (spec section 3): pure, no SDL, so
// tests/test_winplan.c builds it on its own, as layout.c is built
#ifndef WINPLAN_H
#define WINPLAN_H

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>

#define RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS "vulkan,direct3d12,direct3d11,opengl"
#define RENDER_LIST_UNIX "vulkan,opengl"
#define RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT "vulkan,direct3d11"
#define WINPLAN_BACKGROUND_TRANSPARENT 3   // ModeBackground's BACKGROUND_TRANSPARENT; launcher.c checks they agree
#ifdef _WIN32
#define WINPLAN_WINDOWS true               // What a caller passes as window_plan()'s `windows`
#else
#define WINPLAN_WINDOWS false
#endif

typedef enum { WINDOW_FULLSCREEN, WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT } WindowKind;

typedef struct {
    WindowKind kind;
    const char *render_list;   // NULL: pass no list (SDL_RENDER_DRIVER chose)
    bool mark_fullscreen;      // ITaskbarList2::MarkFullscreenWindow on the HWND
    bool refused;              // An override outside the transparent list: use the opaque window
    char reason[128];          // Set when refused: "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=<name> is not in the transparent list"
} WindowPlan;

// background: the ModeBackground wanted; windows: built for Windows; render_driver_env: getenv("SDL_RENDER_DRIVER"), NULL or "" for unset
WindowPlan window_plan(int background, bool windows, const char *render_driver_env);
// The log's Renderer: line for the renderer SDL chose from render_list (NULL: SDL_RENDER_DRIVER chose)
void winplan_renderer_line(const char *render_list, const char *chosen, char *out, size_t size);

#endif
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `src/winplan.c`**

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "winplan.h"

// A function to tell whether a name is one of a comma-separated list's entries, whole
static bool in_list(const char *list, const char *name)
{
    size_t length = strlen(name);
    for (const char *at = list; *at != '\0'; ) {
        size_t entry = strcspn(at, ",");
        if (entry == length && strncmp(at, name, length) == 0)
            return true;
        at += entry;
        if (*at == ',')
            at++;
    }
    return false;
}

// A function to work out the window for a background and the renderers that may draw it. Only
// Transparent on Windows needs a window of its own: per-pixel alpha, one row short of the display so
// Windows composes it, marked fullscreen for the taskbar, on the two renderers that can draw it.
// SDL_RENDER_DRIVER, when set, picks the renderer: it takes the list away and changes nothing else,
// unless it names one outside the transparent list, which is refused by its name alone (a renderer
// that is made can still draw no alpha), for the opaque window.
WindowPlan window_plan(int background, bool windows, const char *render_driver_env)
{
    WindowPlan plan;
    memset(&plan, 0, sizeof(plan));
    bool forced = render_driver_env != NULL && render_driver_env[0] != '\0';
    plan.kind = WINDOW_FULLSCREEN;
    plan.render_list = forced ? NULL : windows ? RENDER_LIST_WINDOWS : RENDER_LIST_UNIX;
    if (!windows || background != WINPLAN_BACKGROUND_TRANSPARENT)
        return plan;
    if (forced && !in_list(RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT, render_driver_env)) {
        plan.refused = true;
        snprintf(plan.reason, sizeof(plan.reason), "SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=%s is not in the transparent list", render_driver_env);
        return plan;
    }
    plan.kind = WINDOW_TRANSPARENT_SHORT;
    plan.render_list = forced ? NULL : RENDER_LIST_TRANSPARENT;
    plan.mark_fullscreen = true;
    return plan;
}

// A function to word the log's Renderer: line: the renderer SDL chose, and the list's first entry
// named as unavailable when it was not that one, or SDL_RENDER_DRIVER named when it chose (no list)
void winplan_renderer_line(const char *render_list, const char *chosen, char *out, size_t size)
{
    if (render_list == NULL) {
        snprintf(out, size, "Renderer: %s (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)", chosen);
        return;
    }
    size_t first = strcspn(render_list, ",");
    if (strlen(chosen) == first && strncmp(render_list, chosen, first) == 0)
        snprintf(out, size, "Renderer: %s", chosen);
    else
        snprintf(out, size, "Renderer: %s (%.*s was unavailable)", chosen, (int) first, render_list);
}
```

### Task 1.5b: The renderer: the lists, the override, the log lines, VSync, the debug dump (spec § 3 Creation to VSync; unit test 3)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/launcher.c`: the includes (`:11-23`), the globals (`:229-234`), `renderer_vsync()` (`:267-287`), `apply_frame_timing()`'s VSync block (`:330-336`), `create_window()` (`:366-388`, between the window and the HWND read), `set_draw_color()` (`:419-428`)
- Modify: `src/debug.c:378-383` (`debug_video()`'s renderer half)
- Create: `tests/test_render_drivers.c`; modify `tests/CMakeLists.txt` (append)
- Create: `tests/headless/checks/44-renderer.sh`, `tests/headless/checks/95-every-run.sh`
- Create: `tests/headless/fixtures/f44-quit.ini`, `tests/headless/fixtures/f44-draw.ini`
- Test: CTest `render_drivers`; the headless set

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `window_plan()`, `winplan_renderer_line()`, `WINPLAN_WINDOWS`, `WINPLAN_BACKGROUND_TRANSPARENT` (Task 1.5a).
- Produces, in `launcher.c`, as the skeleton gives it:
  ```c
  static bool create_renderer(const WindowPlan *plan);
  ```
- Also produces: `static WindowPlan plan_in_force;` in `launcher.c`, the plan of the window that exists (Task 1.7b makes its new renderer from it; Task 1.8 replaces it on each recreation). And the log lines `Video: SDL's <driver> driver, the <name> renderer` (text kept), `Renderer: <name>`, `Renderer: <name> (<first> was unavailable)`, `Renderer: <name> (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)`, and in `-d`'s video dump `Render driver: <name>`, `Max texture:   <n> px`, `Render VSync:  <n>`.
- Produces for every later headless task: `tests/headless/checks/95-every-run.sh`, which every shard runs, and which holds the startup assertions every run must meet. A task that adds runs on purpose on another renderer or another video driver adds its case there (Task 1.10's Weston runs: `Video: SDL's wayland driver`).

**What the VSync rule is on master, read before re-basing it.** The spec speaks of "the rule that grays the VSync row with its reason when the renderer cannot do VSync". Master has no such row rule: the only VSync gray is the *FPS limit* row while VSync is on (`settings.c:1318-1319`, `WHY_VSYNC`), which reads the config, not the renderer. What reads the renderer is `renderer_vsync()` and `check_vsync()` (`launcher.c:267-299`): they log `Frame timing: VSync wanted <on|off>, the renderer gives <on|off>` and the refusal lines, and `present_frame()` paces each frame when the renderer does not give what is wanted. That is the rule this task re-bases on SDL3's answer (`SDL_GetRenderVSync`) and checks per renderer, on Vulkan and on the OpenGL fallback. No gray rule is added. (Reported to the controller as an open question.)

```bash
# The renderer (spec section 3, Task 1.5b): SDL takes the first renderer of the platform's list that
# works, and the log says which; SDL_RENDER_DRIVER beats the list; a renderer that cannot be made
# takes the startup error path; and with the Vulkan driver hidden (VK_DRIVER_FILES at nothing) SDL
# falls back to OpenGL by itself, which then draws the background, the titles, the settings screen
# and a picker as Vulkan does. The fallback runs are named gl-*, which 95-every-run.sh knows.
# VSync is asked for on each renderer: the rule re-based on SDL_GetRenderVSync.

R44_NO_VULKAN=/nonexistent/vulkan-driver.json

# Vulkan, as every run starts: the Video: and Renderer: lines, the video dump's renderer, and VSync given
CFG=$FX/f44-quit.ini run_quick f44-vulkan
log=$out/f44-vulkan.log
ok=1
grep -qx "Video: SDL's x11 driver, the vulkan renderer" "$log" && grep -qx 'Renderer: vulkan' "$log" \
    && grep -qx 'Render driver: vulkan' "$log" && grep -qE '^Max texture:   [0-9]+ px$' "$log" \
    && grep -qx 'Frame timing: VSync wanted on, the renderer gives on' "$log" \
    && ! grep -q 'Could not turn VSync' "$log" && ran_clean f44-vulkan && ok=0
result "renderer: Vulkan is chosen and named, and gives the VSync asked for (exit $(cat "$out/f44-vulkan.code"))" $ok
grep -E '^(Video|Renderer|Render driver|Max texture|Render VSync|Frame timing):' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# SDL_RENDER_DRIVER beats the list: StreamFlex passes none, and the log says the variable chose

log=$out/f44-override.log
ok=1
grep -qx "Video: SDL's x11 driver, the opengl renderer" "$log" && grep -qx 'Renderer: opengl (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)' "$log" \
    && ! grep -qx 'Renderer: vulkan' "$log" && ran_clean f44-override && ok=0
result "renderer: SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=opengl beats the list (exit $(cat "$out/f44-override.code"))" $ok
grep -E '^(Video|Renderer):' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# No renderer at all (a name SDL does not know) takes the startup error path: SDL's reason, then exit 1
SDL_RENDER_DRIVER=nosuchdriver STREAMFLEX_TEST_NO_MESSAGE_BOX=1 CFG=$FX/f44-quit.ini run_quick f44-nodriver
log=$out/f44-nodriver.log
ok=1
grep -qx 'Could not initialize renderer' "$log" && ! grep -q "^Video: SDL's " "$log" && ran_clean f44-nodriver 1 && ok=0
result "renderer: none to be had reports SDL's reason and exits 1 (exit $(cat "$out/f44-nodriver.code"))" $ok
grep -A1 'Could not initialize renderer' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# The automatic fallback: Vulkan hidden, SDL moves down the list by itself, and the log says so.
# VSync is asked for on OpenGL too, on and then off.
VK_DRIVER_FILES=$R44_NO_VULKAN CFG=$FX/f44-quit.ini run_quick gl-quit
VK_DRIVER_FILES=$R44_NO_VULKAN CFG=$FX/f42-fps10.ini run_quick gl-fps10
ok=1
grep -qx "Video: SDL's x11 driver, the opengl renderer" "$out/gl-quit.log" \
    && grep -qx 'Renderer: opengl (vulkan was unavailable)' "$out/gl-quit.log" \
    && grep -qx 'Frame timing: VSync wanted on, the renderer gives on' "$out/gl-quit.log" \
    && grep -qx 'Frame timing: VSync wanted off, the renderer gives off' "$out/gl-fps10.log" \
    && ran_clean gl-quit && ran_clean gl-fps10 && ok=0
result "renderer: with Vulkan hidden SDL falls back to OpenGL, which gives VSync on and off (exit $(cat "$out/gl-quit.code"), $(cat "$out/gl-fps10.code"))" $ok
grep -hE '^(Video|Renderer|Frame timing):' "$out/gl-quit.log" "$out/gl-fps10.log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# The home screen on each renderer: the background color in the screen's corner, and the one title,
# in a magenta nothing else has, spanning the same box with as many exactly magenta pixels. A title
# is a texture copied 1:1, so the two renderers must agree to the pixel.
r44_home() {
    local name=$1 pid=$2 shot=/tmp/r44.xwd
    wait_line 'Begin program loop' "$pid" || return
    screen_shows "$name" corner 10,10=30,45,60 && echo 'corner yes' >> "$out/$name.seen"
    xwd -root -silent -out "$shot" && python3 "$HERE/pixels.py" "$shot" count 255,0,255 inside 255,0,255 > "$out/$name.box" 2>&1
}
CFG=$FX/f44-draw.ini run_keys f44-home +r44_home
VK_DRIVER_FILES=$R44_NO_VULKAN CFG=$FX/f44-draw.ini run_keys gl-home +r44_home
ok=1
grep -qx 'corner yes' "$out/f44-home.seen" && grep -qx 'corner yes' "$out/gl-home.seen" \
    && grep -qE '^box [0-9]+,[0-9]+ to [0-9]+,[0-9]+: [1-9][0-9]* pixels of 255,0,255$' "$out/f44-home.box" \
    && [ "$(cat "$out/f44-home.box")" = "$(cat "$out/gl-home.box")" ] \
    && ran_clean f44-home && ran_clean gl-home && ok=0
result "renderer: OpenGL draws the background and a title as Vulkan does (exit $(cat "$out/f44-home.code"), $(cat "$out/gl-home.code"))" $ok
echo "      vulkan: $(cat "$out/f44-home.box" 2> /dev/null)"
echo "      opengl: $(cat "$out/gl-home.box" 2> /dev/null)"

# The settings screen on OpenGL: its backdrop, the preview's color, then an image the browser
# highlights (the keys of 60-settings-background.sh's f60-preview)
r44_black() { look "$1" "$2" black 'Settings opened' 30,30=0,0,0 @5,1074=11,22,32; }
r44_blue() { look "$1" "$2" blue 'Settings: the preview shows /home/tester/Pictures/blue.png' 30,30=40,70,160; }
VK_DRIVER_FILES=$R44_NO_VULKAN CFG=$FX/f60-color.ini run_keys gl-settings Menu +r44_black Down Return Right Down Return +r44_blue Menu
ok=1
grep -qx 'black yes' "$out/gl-settings.seen" && grep -qx 'blue yes' "$out/gl-settings.seen" \
    && grep -q 'Settings: nothing changed' "$out/gl-settings.log" && ran_clean gl-settings && ok=0
result "renderer: OpenGL draws the settings screen and the preview's image (exit $(cat "$out/gl-settings.code"))" $ok
sed 's/^/      /' "$out/gl-settings.seen" 2> /dev/null

# A picker on OpenGL: a color typed in the color picker's hex editor shows in the preview (the keys of
# 58-settings-pickers.sh's f58-hex); the Menu key puts it back and closes, so nothing is saved
r44_hex() { look "$1" "$2" hex 'Settings: previewing #102030' 30,30=16,32,48; }
```

Create `tests/headless/checks/95-every-run.sh`:

```bash
# Every run that opened a window, in every file: it started under SDL's x11 driver on the Vulkan
# renderer (spec section 9), but for the runs that ask for another renderer on purpose: the fallback
# subset (gl-*, Vulkan hidden) and the override run (f44-override). Only a run's first Video: and
# Renderer: lines are read: a device loss's later ones are its own check's.
# Every shard runs this file (run.sh <label> [leaks] K/N): it reads the logs in its own output
# folder, so each shard checks the runs it made.
e95_bad=() e95_runs=0
e95_chosen='^Renderer: [a-z0-9]+( \([^)]*\))?$'
for e95_log in "$out"/*.log; do
    [ -f "$e95_log" ] || continue
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's " "$e95_log" || continue
    e95_name=$(basename "$e95_log" .log)
    case $e95_name in
        gl-*) e95_video="Video: SDL's x11 driver, the opengl renderer" e95_renderer='Renderer: opengl (vulkan was unavailable)' ;;
        f44-override) e95_video="Video: SDL's x11 driver, the opengl renderer" e95_renderer='Renderer: opengl (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)' ;;
        *) e95_video="Video: SDL's x11 driver, the vulkan renderer" e95_renderer='Renderer: vulkan' ;;
    esac
    e95_runs=$((e95_runs + 1))
    e95_first_video=$(grep -m1 "^Video: SDL's " "$e95_log")
    e95_first_renderer=$(grep -m1 -E "$e95_chosen" "$e95_log")
    [ "$e95_first_video" = "$e95_video" ] && [ "$e95_first_renderer" = "$e95_renderer" ] \
        || e95_bad+=("$e95_name: '$e95_first_video', '${e95_first_renderer:-no Renderer: line}'")
done
ok=1
[ "$e95_runs" -gt 0 ] && [ "${#e95_bad[@]}" = 0 ] && ok=0
result "every run started on Vulkan under x11, but the runs that ask for OpenGL" $ok
echo "      $e95_runs runs read"
for e95_line in "${e95_bad[@]}"; do echo "      $e95_line"; done
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Re-base the VSync answer on SDL3.** Replace `renderer_vsync()` (`:267-287`, as Task 1.2 left it) with:

```c
// A function to tell whether the renderer really presents with VSync, by SDL's answer
// (SDL_GetRenderVSync). SDL3 stands in for VSync a driver refuses, pacing each present itself, so a
// renderer that is asked for VSync reports it; one that cannot turn VSync off keeps reporting it.
static bool renderer_vsync()
{
#ifdef STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS
    // Only the headless harness builds this: its SDL simulates VSync, so a refusal is made up here.
    // "off" is a renderer that keeps VSync on when it is not wanted; any other value never gives it.
    const char *refused = getenv("STREAMFLEX_TEST_VSYNC_REFUSED");
    if (refused != NULL)
        return strcmp(refused, "off") == 0;
#endif
    int vsync = 0;
    return renderer != NULL && SDL_GetRenderVSync(renderer, &vsync) && vsync != 0;
}
```

```c

            log_error("Could not turn VSync %s\n%s", wanted ? "on" : "off", SDL_GetError());

```

- [ ] **Step 7: `create_renderer()`, and `create_window()` using it.** Add above `create_window()` (before `:356`):

```c
// A function to make the renderer for the window in force, the renderer half of create_window()
// (spec section 3): the plan's list, or none when SDL_RENDER_DRIVER chose; the blend mode; the
// Video: and Renderer: lines; VSync as the frame timing wants it, and whether the renderer gives it;
// the draw color. False, with SDL's reason logged, when no renderer could be made: the caller
// decides what that means.
static bool create_renderer(const WindowPlan *plan)
{
    renderer = SDL_CreateRenderer(window, plan->render_list);
    if (renderer == NULL) {
        log_error("Could not initialize renderer\n%s", SDL_GetError());
        return false;
    }
    SDL_SetRenderDrawBlendMode(renderer, SDL_BLENDMODE_BLEND);

    // Which video driver and renderer SDL chose, and whether the list's first choice was not it
    const char *name = SDL_GetRendererName(renderer);
    char line[160];
    winplan_renderer_line(plan->render_list, name, line, sizeof(line));
    log_debug("Video: SDL's %s driver, the %s renderer", SDL_GetCurrentVideoDriver(), name);
    log_debug("%s", line);

    if (!SDL_SetRenderVSync(renderer, vsync_wanted ? 1 : 0))
        log_error("Could not turn VSync %s\n%s", vsync_wanted ? "on" : "off", SDL_GetError());
    check_vsync();
    set_draw_color();
    return true;
}
```

**Files:**

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `create_renderer()`, `plan_in_force` (Task 1.5b).
- Produces, in `src/derive.c` / `.h` (pure), as the skeleton gives it: `ScreenGeometry geometry_from_pixels(int width, int height)` returning `screen_width`, `screen_height`, `screen_margin`, `title_min_size`.
- Also produces (additions): `int derive_refresh_rate(float reported)` in `derive.c`; in `launcher.c` the statics `display_rate` (Hz) and `display_scale`, and `static void window_pixel_size(int *width, int *height)`, `static bool read_screen_geometry(void)`, `static void read_refresh_rate(void)`, which Task 1.6b's reload calls; in `win32.c` `const char *dpi_awareness(void)`; the log lines `Screen: <w>x<h> px, pixel density <d.dd>, display scale <s.ss>` and `DPI awareness: per-monitor v2`; `debug_video(SDL_Renderer *renderer, int width, int height, int refresh_rate)`.
- Produces for the harness: `$out/<run>.display`, the size of the X display a run used when it is not the main 1920x1080 one; `95-every-run.sh` reads it.

**The order in `main()`: `create_window()` moves up, to straight after `init_sdl()`.** Today `init_sdl()` sets `geo` from the desktop mode (`:252-264`), then `init_sdl_image()`, `init_sdl_ttf()` (which copies `geo.title_min_size` into `title_info.min_size`, `:461`) and `refresh_effective()` (whose `derive_input()` reads `geo.screen_width` and `geo.screen_height`, `config_fields.c:294-295`) run, and only then `init_svg()` and `create_window()` (`:2419-2426`). `create_window()` reads no `geo`, no font, no `eff` and nothing nanosvg sets up: it reads the config (`set_draw_color()`, `apply_frame_timing()`) and makes the window, the renderer and, now, `geo` itself. Moving it up is one move, against moving two steps down; it keeps `init_sdl_ttf()` and `refresh_effective()` together as they are; and a fatal error in the font now comes with a window open, which `quit()` already tears down, as for any later fatal error (it also spares the Fedora leak pass the "fatal stop before any window exists" leak `CONTRIBUTING.md:27` describes). `raise_restarted_window()` stays straight after `create_window()`.

```c
// Unit test 9 (spec section 9): the screen's geometry from the window's size in pixels, with the
// arithmetic and rounding init_sdl() always used: the margin truncated, the title minimum rounded
static void test_geometry_from_pixels(void)
{

    CHECK_INT(uhd.screen_margin, 108);

// A function to test the refresh rate taken from the display: rounded to whole hertz, and 60 when the
// display reports none (Xvfb, some VMs and remote desktops report 0), since every timing divides by it
static void test_refresh_rate(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(derive_refresh_rate(0.0f), 60);
    CHECK_INT(derive_refresh_rate(-1.0f), 60);

    CHECK_INT(derive_refresh_rate(60.0f), 60);
    CHECK_INT(derive_refresh_rate(143.98f), 144);
    CHECK_INT(derive_refresh_rate(29.97f), 30);
}
```

```c
#define DERIVE_DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE 60        // Hz, when the display reports none
#define DERIVE_SCREEN_MARGIN 0.05F            // The screen's margin: 5% of its height
#define DERIVE_TITLE_MIN_SIZE 0.02F           // The readable minimum title size: 2% of the screen height

// The screen geometry every menu shares, from the window's size in pixels (spec section 6)

    int screen_width;
```

and after `bool derive_exit_retry_due(...)` (`:86`):

```c
int derive_refresh_rate(float reported);
```

Append to `derive.c`:

```c
// A function to work out the screen's geometry from the window's size in pixels, with the arithmetic
// and rounding the launcher has always used: the margin truncated, the title minimum rounded
ScreenGeometry geometry_from_pixels(int width, int height)
{
    ScreenGeometry screen;
    screen.screen_width = width;
    screen.screen_height = height;
    screen.screen_margin = (int) (DERIVE_SCREEN_MARGIN * (float) height);
    screen.title_min_size = (int) (DERIVE_TITLE_MIN_SIZE * (float) height + 0.5F);
    return screen;
}

// A function to take the display's refresh rate in whole hertz, rounded (SDL3 gives a float: 59.94);
// a display that reports less than 1 Hz gets the default, since every frame time divides by it
int derive_refresh_rate(float reported)
{
    return reported >= 1.0F ? (int) (reported + 0.5F) : DERIVE_DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE;
}
```

In `launcher.h`, `#define DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE 60` (`:19`) becomes `#define DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE DERIVE_DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE`. (`SCREEN_MARGIN` and `TITLE_MIN_SIZE` at `:26` and `:29` go in Step 9, with their only reader.)

- [ ] **Step 7: The headless checks: every run's screen, and the Scale leg.** Append to `tests/headless/checks/95-every-run.sh`:

```bash

# Every run that opened a window: its first Screen: line is its display's size (1920x1080, or what
# NAME.display says for a run on another X display) at pixel density 1.00 and display scale 1.00, but
# the Scale leg's run at 2 (f47-scale2); no run logs a size-change reload before its program loop
# begins, so the event SDL sends as it makes the window changes nothing; and no run's renderer draws
# at a size other than its window's (spec section 6). The runs pin GDK_SCALE, Xft.dpi and
# SDL_VIDEO_X11_SCALING_FACTOR unset (run.sh) except where a check sets one.
e95_bad=() e95_runs=0
for e95_log in "$out"/*.log; do
    [ -f "$e95_log" ] || continue
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's " "$e95_log" || continue
    e95_name=$(basename "$e95_log" .log)
    e95_size=$(cat "$out/$e95_name.display" 2> /dev/null || echo 1920x1080)
    e95_scale=1.00
    [ "$e95_name" = f47-scale2 ] && e95_scale=2.00
    e95_runs=$((e95_runs + 1))
    e95_first=$(grep -m1 '^Screen: ' "$e95_log")
    [ "$e95_first" = "Screen: $e95_size px, pixel density 1.00, display scale $e95_scale" ] \
        || e95_bad+=("$e95_name: first Screen line '${e95_first:-none}', wanted $e95_size at scale $e95_scale")
    awk '/^Begin program loop$/ { exit } /^Screen: .*; reloading/ { found = 1; exit } END { exit !found }' "$e95_log" \
        && e95_bad+=("$e95_name: a size-change reload before the program loop")
    grep -q '^Screen: the renderer draws ' "$e95_log" && e95_bad+=("$e95_name: $(grep -m1 '^Screen: the renderer draws ' "$e95_log")")
done
ok=1
[ "$e95_runs" -gt 0 ] && [ "${#e95_bad[@]}" = 0 ] && ok=0
result "every run's screen is its display's size at scale 1.00, and none reloads as it starts" $ok
echo "      $e95_runs runs read"
for e95_line in "${e95_bad[@]}"; do echo "      $e95_line"; done
```

```c
// A function to read the window's size in pixels, the screen's one source (spec section 6): never the
// desktop mode, which Wayland gives in scaled units
static void window_pixel_size(int *width, int *height)
{
    *width = 0;
    *height = 0;
    SDL_GetWindowSizeInPixels(window, width, height);
}

// A function to set the screen geometry from the window once the window system has carried out what
// was asked of it (the fullscreen request, a resize); true when the size changed
static bool read_screen_geometry(void)
{
    SDL_SyncWindow(window);   // At worst it times out, and the size read is the one in force
    int width, height;
    window_pixel_size(&width, &height);
    if (width == geo.screen_width && height == geo.screen_height)
        return false;
    ScreenGeometry screen = geometry_from_pixels(width, height);
    geo.screen_width = screen.screen_width;
    geo.screen_height = screen.screen_height;
    geo.screen_margin = screen.screen_margin;
    geo.title_min_size = screen.title_min_size;
    title_info.min_size = screen.title_min_size;
    return true;
}

// A function to read the refresh rate of the display the window is on, the one thing the desktop mode
// still gives. No mode, or a rate of 0 (Xvfb, some VMs and remote desktops), keeps 60 Hz.
static void read_refresh_rate(void)
{
    const SDL_DisplayMode *mode = SDL_GetDesktopDisplayMode(SDL_GetDisplayForWindow(window));
    display_rate = derive_refresh_rate(mode != NULL ? mode->refresh_rate : 0.0F);
    if (mode == NULL || mode->refresh_rate < 1.0F)
        log_debug("The display reports no refresh rate, using %i Hz", DEFAULT_REFRESH_RATE);
}
```

(The fallback line keeps its text: `20-refresh.sh` greps it.)

- [ ] **Step 11: `dpi_awareness()` on Windows.** In `src/platform/platform.h`'s Windows block, after `void hide_cursor(Entry* entry);` (`:45`) add `const char *dpi_awareness(void);   // How Windows scales this process's windows, as the manifest set it`. Append to `src/platform/win32.c`:

```c
// A function to say how Windows scales this process's windows, as the manifest set it before SDL
// started: per-monitor v2 (spec section 6), per-monitor v1, system or unaware
const char *dpi_awareness(void)
{
    DPI_AWARENESS_CONTEXT context = GetThreadDpiAwarenessContext();
    if (AreDpiAwarenessContextsEqual(context, DPI_AWARENESS_CONTEXT_PER_MONITOR_AWARE_V2))
        return "per-monitor v2";
    switch (GetAwarenessFromDpiAwarenessContext(context)) {
        case DPI_AWARENESS_PER_MONITOR_AWARE:
            return "per-monitor v1";
        case DPI_AWARENESS_SYSTEM_AWARE:
            return "system";
        default:
            return "unaware";
    }
}
```

These are Windows 10 1607 calls, declared when `WINVER` is `0x0605` or more; the build defines no `WINVER`, so the SDK's newest applies. If MSVC reports `DPI_AWARENESS_CONTEXT` undeclared, the target is missing `_WIN32_WINNT=0x0A00`: stop and report it rather than redefining it in this file.

### Task 1.6b: One pending reload, geometry first; the size-change rules (spec § 6 and § 3 "One reload, geometry first"; unit test 10)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/derive.h`, `src/derive.c` (append: `derive_size_decision()`)
- Modify: `src/launcher.h` (after `:344`: `remake_screensaver_texture()`; after `:355`: the reload interface)
- Modify: `src/launcher.c`: the includes (`:11-23`), the test-hook prototypes (`:51-57`), `start_screensaver()` (`:787-809`) and a new function after `reload_screensaver()` (`:830-834`), new functions before `pre_launch()` (before `:2245`), `post_launch()` (`:2257-2278`), the main loop (`:2479-2481`, the event switch `:2483-2555`, and `:2563-2594`), the test hooks (after `test_pad_stop()`, `:1894`)
- Modify: `src/settings_screen.c`: `free_screen()` (`:768-776`), `settings_open()` (`:1658-1681`), and new functions after `apply_all()` (`:390`) and `settings_close_now()` (`:1690`); `src/settings_screen.h`
- Modify: `src/settings_pickers.c` (after `pickers_command()`, `:867`), `src/settings_pickers.h`
- Modify: `tests/test_derive.c`
- Modify: `tests/headless/checks/47-screen.sh` (append); create `tests/headless/fixtures/f47-launch.ini`
- Modify: `CONTRIBUTING.md:45-66` (the test hooks)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `read_screen_geometry()`, `window_pixel_size()`, `read_refresh_rate()`, `display_rate`, `display_scale` (Task 1.6a); `REFRESH_ORDER`, `run_refresh()` (`settings_screen.c:263-349`).
- Produces, in `launcher.c` (declared in `launcher.h`), as the skeleton gives them:
  ```c
  typedef enum { RELOAD_NONE = 0, RELOAD_TARGETS = 1, RELOAD_DEVICE = 2, RELOAD_SIZE = 4, RELOAD_RECREATE = 8 } ReloadReason;
  void reload_mark(ReloadReason why);   // Event handlers only mark; nothing reloads inside the drain
  void reload_run_pending(void);        // After the drain, before drawing: geometry first, then the textures
  bool app_in_front(void);              // state.application_launching || state.application_running
  ```
- Produces, in `settings_screen.c` (declared in `settings_screen.h`), as the skeleton gives them: `void settings_refresh_all(void)` and `void settings_reflow(void)`.
- Also produces, for the reload (controller decision, 2026-10-11: the reload restarts no gamepad and lifts no screensaver dim, spec § 3 "the current menu and screen state are kept"): in `settings_screen.c`, `void settings_refresh_for_reload(void);` (REFRESH_ORDER's walk, with the gamepad group skipped and the screensaver's group replaced, see Step 8); in `launcher.c` (declared in `launcher.h`), `void remake_screensaver_texture(void);`. `settings_refresh_all()` stays as the contract gives it, for Discard's callers.
- Also produces (additions): in `derive.c`, `typedef enum { SIZE_NOTHING, SIZE_RELOAD, SIZE_WAIT } SizeDecision;` and `SizeDecision derive_size_decision(int laid_out_width, int laid_out_height, int width, int height, bool app_in_front, bool recreated);`; in `settings_pickers.c`, `void pickers_cancel(void);`; in `settings_screen.c`, the static `make_preview()` (Task 1.7a calls it); in `launcher.c`, the static `reload_pending` bitmask and `size_deferred` flag; the log line `Reload: done at <w>x<h> px, for <reasons>`; the test hooks `STREAMFLEX_TEST_APP`, `STREAMFLEX_TEST_PIXEL_SIZE` and `STREAMFLEX_TEST_DISPLAY_EVENT`.

**The rules, as built here.** An event handler only marks a reason; `reload_run_pending()` runs once per pass of the main loop, after the events are drained and before anything is drawn. It reads the geometry first (`SDL_SyncWindow`, then the window's pixel size), unless an app is launching or running: then `geo` stays as it is and any size change waits for `post_launch()` (or the launch's timeout), so a game's temporary resolution never becomes the layout. A size reload that finds the size back where it was logs `Screen: <w>x<h> px again; nothing to reload` and does nothing more. Otherwise it re-derives `eff` and the layout area from the new size, runs the refresh groups in `REFRESH_ORDER` the way a reload needs them (`settings_refresh_for_reload()`: every group, but the gamepad, which keeps running, and the screensaver, whose dim and transition are kept and only its screen-sized layer made again), and, with settings open and the size changed, re-measures the settings screen (`settings_reflow()`), keeping its page, cursor and unsaved edits and closing an open picker or folder browser as Back would.

- [ ] **Step 3: `derive_size_decision()`.** In `derive.h`, after the `ScreenGeometry` typedef add:

```c
// What a change of the window's pixel size does (spec section 6)
typedef enum { SIZE_NOTHING, SIZE_RELOAD, SIZE_WAIT } SizeDecision;
```

and after `int derive_refresh_rate(float reported);`:

```c
SizeDecision derive_size_decision(int laid_out_width, int laid_out_height, int width, int height, bool app_in_front, bool recreated);
```

Append to `derive.c`:

```c
// A function to decide what a change of the window's pixel size does (spec section 6): a recreated
// window always reloads, with its new renderer; the size laid out already does nothing; a new size
// reloads, or waits while an app is launching or running, so a game's temporary resolution never
// becomes the layout
SizeDecision derive_size_decision(int laid_out_width, int laid_out_height, int width, int height, bool app_in_front, bool recreated)
{
    if (recreated)
        return SIZE_RELOAD;
    if (width == laid_out_width && height == laid_out_height)
        return SIZE_NOTHING;
    return app_in_front ? SIZE_WAIT : SIZE_RELOAD;
}
```

```bash
# A change of the window's pixel size runs the one reload, geometry first (spec section 6). Whether
# SDL3's X11 driver reports a resize of a fullscreen window with no window manager is unverified, so
# s47_resize tries SDL's own event first; when no Screen: line comes within 5 s it puts the window back
# and has the test hook (STREAMFLEX_TEST_PIXEL_SIZE) force the size, which proves the reload but not
# SDL's event. The first run decides, and s47.path keeps which path every later run takes; each
# run's own NAME.path says which it took.
```
