# makes every pixel of the window that is exactly that color see-through, so an icon's own pixels of
# that color were holes in it (the Plex icon's diagonal), and so were pixels the renderer's scaling
# blended onto it from dark neighbors next to it. (Windows' Transparent mode is per-pixel alpha, with
# no key.) Every icon is now kept two steps off the key as it loads.
```

### Commands these tasks share

These four tasks run their tests the same way. The controller may fold this block into Phase 1's Global Constraints.

- **Linux unit tests (CTest)** run in the harness's Debian image as `tester`, with ASan, UBSan and `-DEXTRA_WARNINGS=ON`, as CI's `Unit tests (ASan, UBSan)` job does (`.github/workflows/build.yml:348-379`). The script lives outside the repo, at `C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-port/unit.sh`. If it is missing, write it with LF line endings, exactly:

  ```bash
  #!/bin/bash
  # Linux unit tests with ASan+UBSan and the extra warnings, run as the non-root user "tester"
  # usage: unit.sh <label> [ctest -R regex]
  set -u
  label=$1
  filter=${2:-}
  out=/out/$label; rm -rf "$out"; mkdir -p "$out"
  rm -rf /work; mkdir -p /work
  tar -C /src --exclude=./build --exclude=./.git --exclude=./headless-out -cf - . | tar -C /work -xf -
  chown -R tester /work
  flags="-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-omit-frame-pointer"
  su tester -c "cmake -S /work -B /work/build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS='$flags' -DCMAKE_EXE_LINKER_FLAGS='-fsanitize=address,undefined' -DEXTRA_WARNINGS=ON" > "$out/configure.log" 2>&1 || { echo CONFIGURE FAILED; tail -20 "$out/configure.log"; exit 2; }
  su tester -c "cmake --build /work/build -j$(nproc)" > "$out/build.log" 2>&1 || { echo BUILD FAILED; tail -30 "$out/build.log"; exit 2; }
  echo "warnings outside src/external: $(grep -iE 'warning:' "$out/build.log" | grep -v '/work/src/external/' | grep -vcE '^g?make')"
  su tester -c "UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1:halt_on_error=1 setarch $(uname -m) -R ctest --test-dir /work/build --output-on-failure ${filter:+-R '$filter'}" > "$out/ctest.log" 2>&1
  code=$?
  grep -E 'tests passed|Failed|CHECK failed|expected|checks,' "$out/ctest.log" | head -40
  exit $code
  ```

  Run it with the harness image (`docker build -t streamflex-test C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex/tests/headless` first):

  ```powershell
  docker run --rm --name sfdev-<label> --security-opt seccomp=unconfined -v C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex:/src:ro -v C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-port:/tools:ro -v C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-headless-out:/out streamflex-test bash /tools/unit.sh <label> [<regex>]
  ```

  A pass reads `warnings outside src/external: 0` and `100% tests passed`.

  pwsh C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex/tests/headless/run-shards.ps1 -Prefix <label> -OutDir C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-headless-out

The set ends `SET PASSED: 2 passes ...` or `SET PASSED: 4 passes in 2 shards each, every one complete with 0 failed`. ### Task 1.10: Native Wayland

The spec is § 5, with unit test 8 and § 9's "A Wayland leg". StreamFlex sets no video driver. It names itself to SDL before `SDL_Init`, and it logs where the driver list came from, or why SDL kept to X11 while a Wayland compositor runs. A Weston headless leg runs both branches of SDL's rule, one per image.

**What was read in Weston's source and documents (2026-10-11, read-only; the `wayland-mirror/weston` GitHub mirror at tags `14.0.2` and `15.0.1`, and Debian's weston(1) pages for trixie 14.0.2-1 and testing 15.0.1-1).** The spec calls Weston's flags unverified. These were confirmed:
- **Options both versions accept:** `--backend=headless`, `--renderer=pixman`, `--shell=kiosk`, `--socket=NAME`, `--width=W`, `--height=H`, `--scale=N`, `--idle-time=0` and `--no-config`.
  - `frontend/main.c` adds `-shell.so` to a shell name that lacks it (14.0.2: lines 986-989).
  - `--renderer` takes `pixman`; `--use-pixman` is its deprecated alias.
  - The headless backend's default renderer (`WESTON_RENDERER_AUTO`) is the no-op renderer, so the leg names `pixman`.
- **The headless output's mode is `--width` × `--scale` by `--height` × `--scale`.** `headless_output_set_size()` multiplies (15.0.1 `headless.c:507-508`). So the man page's "WxH pixels" means logical pixels, and a 3840x2160 output at scale 2 is `--width=1920 --height=1080 --scale=2`.
- **fifo-v1 is in Weston 15 and not in Weston 14.**
  - 15.0.1's `protocol/meson.build` lists `fifo` and `libweston/meson.build` builds `fifo.c`.
  - `weston_compositor_create()` calls `fifo_setup(ec)` for every backend (15.0.1 `compositor.c:10001`), so the headless compositor advertises `wp_fifo_manager_v1`.
  - 14.0.2's protocol list has no `fifo`.
- **Keyboard focus exists only on Weston 15 with `--fake-seat`.**
  - Weston 14.0.2's headless backend creates no seat at all, so no client there ever gets keyboard focus.
  - Weston 15.0.1's headless backend creates a seat with a keyboard only under `--fake-seat` (`headless_input_create()`, `headless.c:604-610`; option at `frontend/main.c:4336`).
  - The checks that read focus (the pad walk, the launch, the restart) therefore run on Weston 15 only. The spec puts them under Fedora anyway.
- **The kiosk shell** makes each toplevel fullscreen and activates it as it maps. When a toplevel closes, it activates a successor (`find_focus_successor()`, 15.0.1 `kiosk-shell.c:861`).
- **`weston --version` prints `weston 14.0.2`** (`PACKAGE_STRING`, `meson.build:116`). Weston needs `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, and warns unless it is mode 0700 and owned by Weston's user (`main.c:648-667`).

**Left to be settled when the check is written** (the spec allows this for Weston's flags):
- whether SDL 3.2.10's Wayland driver draws through lavapipe onto Weston's pixman renderer with `wl_shm`. The leg prints the `Renderer:` line but does not assert it;
- whether Weston 15's `--fake-seat` keyboard needs `xkeyboard-config` in the Fedora image (it builds a keymap from default names);
- whether SDL's virtual pad events reach a Wayland window that holds the fake seat's keyboard.

A failure in any of these is reported to the controller with the Weston log (`<out>/weston-<socket>.log`). Nothing is loosened.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing unit test**

Create `tests/test_videodriver.c`:

```c
#include <string.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "videodriver.h"

#define FIFO_NOTE "Video: x11, though a Wayland compositor is running: it lacks fifo-v1, which SDL needs before it prefers Wayland"

// A function to test the drivers that need no note: SDL's wayland, x11 with no compositor running,
// and any other platform's driver
static void test_no_note(void)
{
    char out[160] = "stale";
    CHECK(!video_driver_note("wayland", NULL, NULL, "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "");
    CHECK(!video_driver_note("x11", NULL, NULL, NULL, out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK(!video_driver_note("x11", NULL, NULL, "", out, sizeof(out)));     // Set but empty is unset
    CHECK(!video_driver_note("windows", NULL, NULL, NULL, out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "");
}

// A function to test SDL keeping to X11 while a compositor runs, with nothing overriding it
static void test_no_fifo(void)
{
    char out[160];
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", NULL, NULL, "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, FIFO_NOTE);
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", "", "", "wayland-1", out, sizeof(out)));   // Empty overrides are unset
    CHECK_STR(out, FIFO_NOTE);
}

// A function to test an override, named by the variable that set it: SDL3's own name first, as SDL
// reads it, then SDL2's, which SDL3 still reads; an override is the reason whatever SDL chose
static void test_override(void)
{
    char out[160];
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", "x11", NULL, "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=x11");
    CHECK(video_driver_note("wayland", NULL, "wayland", NULL, out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEODRIVER=wayland");
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", "x11,wayland", "wayland", "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=x11,wayland");
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", "", "x11", "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEODRIVER=x11");
}

// A function to test that a short buffer is cut, never overrun
static void test_short_buffer(void)
{
    char out[8];
    CHECK(video_driver_note("x11", NULL, NULL, "wayland-0", out, sizeof(out)));
    CHECK_STR(out, "Video: ");
}

int main(void)
{
    test_no_note();
    test_no_fifo();
    test_override();
    test_short_buffer();
    return check_report();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the pure module**

Create `src/videodriver.h`:

```c
// The note StreamFlex logs once SDL has chosen its video driver (spec section 5): where the driver
// list came from when the environment set it, or why SDL chose X11 while a Wayland compositor runs
// (SDL 3.2.10 and later prefer Wayland only when the compositor offers fifo-v1). Pure: no SDL, so
// tests/test_videodriver.c builds it on its own; launcher.c hands it SDL's choice and the environment.
#ifndef VIDEODRIVER_H
#define VIDEODRIVER_H

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>

// driver: SDL_GetCurrentVideoDriver(); the others the environment's SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER, SDL_VIDEODRIVER
// and WAYLAND_DISPLAY, NULL or "" when unset. Writes the line into out, cut to fit; false, with out
// empty, when there is nothing to say.
bool video_driver_note(const char *driver, const char *sdl_video_driver, const char *sdl_videodriver,
                       const char *wayland_display, char *out, size_t size);

#endif
```

Create `src/videodriver.c`:

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "videodriver.h"

// A function to tell whether an environment value is set: NULL and "" are not, as SDL reads them
static bool is_set(const char *value)
{
    return value != NULL && value[0] != '\0';
}

// A function to write the note for SDL's choice of video driver: an override first, by the variable
// SDL reads first (SDL3's name, then SDL2's), else X11 chosen with a compositor running
bool video_driver_note(const char *driver, const char *sdl_video_driver, const char *sdl_videodriver,
                       const char *wayland_display, char *out, size_t size)
{
    if (size > 0)
        out[0] = '\0';
    if (is_set(sdl_video_driver))
        snprintf(out, size, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=%s", sdl_video_driver);
    else if (is_set(sdl_videodriver))
        snprintf(out, size, "Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEODRIVER=%s", sdl_videodriver);
    else if (strcmp(driver, "x11") == 0 && is_set(wayland_display))
        snprintf(out, size, "Video: x11, though a Wayland compositor is running: it lacks fifo-v1, "
                            "which SDL needs before it prefers Wayland");
    else
        return false;
    return true;
}
```

In `src/CMakeLists.txt`, after `chroma.h` (`:21`), add the two lines `  videodriver.c` and `  videodriver.h`.

Create `tests/headless/checks/67-wayland.sh`:

```bash
# Native Wayland (spec section 5). StreamFlex sets no video driver: SDL 3.2.10 and later prefer
# Wayland only when the compositor offers wp_fifo_manager_v1, and otherwise use X11. A Weston
# headless compositor runs beside Xvfb so that the choice is a real one. Weston 15 (Fedora 44)
# advertises fifo-v1 with every backend, and Weston 14 (Debian trixie) has none, so each image runs
# one branch of SDL's rule; the Weston version is read, never the distribution. Read in Weston's
# source:
# - the headless output's mode is --width and --height times --scale;
# - Weston 14's headless backend has no seat, and Weston 15's has one with a keyboard only under
#   --fake-seat, so focus (which a launched app's detection reads) is checked on Weston 15 only;
# - the kiosk shell makes each window fullscreen, activates it as it maps, and activates the one
#   below when it closes.
# Weston has no keys to press, so the key-driven checks stay on Xvfb; the harness's virtual pad
# drives these runs once the window holds the keyboard.
W67_XDG=/tmp/w67-xdg
rm -rf "$W67_XDG" /tmp/w67-child /tmp/w67-app /tmp/w67-pad /tmp/w67-client.pid
mkdir -p "$W67_XDG" /tmp/w67-child
chown tester:tester "$W67_XDG" /tmp/w67-child
chmod 700 "$W67_XDG"
w67_major=$(weston --version 2> /dev/null | sed -n 's/^weston \([0-9][0-9]*\)\..*/\1/p')
w67_weston=

# A function to start a headless Weston as the test user on the socket NAME, with an output of
# WIDTH x HEIGHT logical pixels at SCALE (its mode is WIDTH*SCALE x HEIGHT*SCALE), and the fake
# seat from Weston 15 on; its PID goes in w67_weston, its log in weston-NAME.log. Fails when its
# socket never appears.
w67_start() {
    local socket=$1 width=$2 height=$3 scale=$4 seat=() i
    [ "${w67_major:-0}" -ge 15 ] && seat=(--fake-seat)
    rm -f "$W67_XDG/$socket" "$W67_XDG/$socket.lock"
    setpriv --reuid=tester --regid=tester --init-groups -- env HOME="$TESTER_HOME" XDG_RUNTIME_DIR="$W67_XDG" \
        weston --backend=headless --renderer=pixman --shell=kiosk --socket="$socket" --width="$width" \
        --height="$height" --scale="$scale" --idle-time=0 --no-config "${seat[@]}" \
        > "$out/weston-$socket.log" 2>&1 &
    w67_weston=$!
    for i in $(seq 100); do
        [ -S "$W67_XDG/$socket" ] && return 0
        running "$w67_weston" || break
        sleep 0.1
    done
    [ -S "$W67_XDG/$socket" ]
}

# A function to end the Weston that w67_start started
w67_stop() {
    [ -n "$w67_weston" ] || return 0
    kill "$w67_weston" 2> /dev/null
    wait "$w67_weston" 2> /dev/null
    w67_weston=
}

# A function to run one of run.sh's run functions (its name and arguments follow SOCKET) with the
# launcher on the Wayland socket SOCKET beside Xvfb: XDG_RUNTIME_DIR and WAYLAND_DISPLAY join its
# environment after DISPLAY, in a subshell, as run_keys_at swaps the display
w67_on() {
    local socket=$1 word at=()
    shift
    for word in "${TESTER[@]}"; do
        at+=("$word")
        [ "$word" = DISPLAY=:99 ] && at+=("XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=$W67_XDG" "WAYLAND_DISPLAY=$socket")
    done
    ( TESTER=("${at[@]}"); "$@" )
}

# A function to start the launcher as a restart's fresh copy starts (--restarted), wait for it to
# gain the keyboard, and end it; its files are kept under NAME as run_keys keeps them
w67_restarted() {
    local name=$1 pid code
    rm -f "$LOG"
    "${TESTER[@]}" "$exe" --restarted -c "$FX/f67-wayland.ini" -d > "$out/$name.out" 2> "$out/$name.err" &
    pid=$!
    wait_line 'Gained keyboard focus' "$pid"
    stop_run "$pid"; code=$?
    echo "$code" > "$out/$name.code"
    cp "$LOG" "$out/$name.log" 2> /dev/null || : > "$out/$name.log"
}

# Functions for +keys (see run_keys), called with the run's NAME and PID: hold the D-pad's right
# until the launcher has read it; press A, then wait for the launched client to take the keyboard;
# kill that client by its PID and give the launcher 2 s to log "Application finished" (written to
# NAME.seen as "finished yes" or "finished no")
w67_walk() { : > /tmp/w67-pad; wait_line 'Gamepad ButtonDPadRight detected' "$2"; rm -f /tmp/w67-pad; sleep 1; }
w67_launch() {
    rm -f /tmp/w67-client.pid
    : > /tmp/w67-pad
    wait_line 'Gamepad ButtonA detected' "$2"
    rm -f /tmp/w67-pad
    wait_line 'Application detected' "$2"
    sleep 1
}
w67_kill() {
    local name=$1 pid i
    pid=$(cat /tmp/w67-client.pid 2> /dev/null)
    if [ -z "$pid" ] || ! kill -TERM "$pid" 2> /dev/null; then
        echo "finished no: no client to kill (PID '$pid')" >> "$out/$name.seen"
        return
    fi
    for i in $(seq 20); do
        grep -qx 'Application finished' "$LOG" && { echo "finished yes" >> "$out/$name.seen"; return; }
        sleep 0.1
    done
    echo "finished no" >> "$out/$name.seen"
}

ok=1
command -v weston > /dev/null && w67_start wayland-sf 1920 1080 1 && ok=0
result "wayland: Weston starts headless beside Xvfb (weston ${w67_major:-not found})" $ok
[ $ok = 0 ] || tail -20 "$out/weston-wayland-sf.log" 2> /dev/null | sed 's/^/      /'
if [ $ok = 0 ] && [ "$w67_major" -lt 15 ]; then
    # Weston 14: no fifo-v1, so SDL keeps to X11 and the log says why; the variable still wins
    w67_on wayland-sf run_quick f67-default
    log=$out/f67-default.log
    ok=1
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's x11 driver, the " "$log" \
        && grep -qx 'Video: x11, though a Wayland compositor is running: it lacks fifo-v1, which SDL needs before it prefers Wayland' "$log" \
        && ran_clean f67-default && ok=0
    result "wayland: with no fifo-v1 SDL keeps to x11, and the log says why (exit $(cat "$out/f67-default.code"))" $ok
    grep -E '^(Video|Renderer|Screen):' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

    SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=wayland CFG=$FX/f67-quick.ini w67_on wayland-sf run_quick f67-forced
    log=$out/f67-forced.log
    ok=1
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's wayland driver, the " "$log" \
        && grep -qx 'Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=wayland' "$log" \
        && ! grep -q 'it lacks fifo-v1' "$log" && ran_clean f67-forced && ok=0
    result "wayland: SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=wayland gives wayland, and the log names the variable (exit $(cat "$out/f67-forced.code"))" $ok
    grep -E '^(Video|Renderer|Screen):' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
elif [ $ok = 0 ]; then
    # Weston 15: fifo-v1, so SDL chooses Wayland with no override, at the compositor's size
    CFG=$FX/f67-quick.ini w67_on wayland-sf run_quick f67-default
    log=$out/f67-default.log
    ok=1
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's wayland driver, the " "$log" && ! grep -q '^Video: x11, though' "$log" \
        && ! grep -q '^Video: the driver list comes from' "$log" \
        && grep -qx 'Screen: 1920x1080 px, pixel density 1.00, display scale 1.00' "$log" \
        && ran_clean f67-default && ok=0
    result "wayland: with fifo-v1 and no override SDL chooses wayland, at the compositor's size (exit $(cat "$out/f67-default.code"))" $ok
    grep -E '^(Video|Renderer|Screen):' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

    SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=x11 CFG=$FX/f67-quick.ini w67_on wayland-sf run_quick f67-x11
    log=$out/f67-x11.log
    ok=1
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's x11 driver, the " "$log" \
        && grep -qx 'Video: the driver list comes from SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=x11' "$log" && ran_clean f67-x11 && ok=0
    result "wayland: SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=x11 gives x11, and the log names the variable (exit $(cat "$out/f67-x11.code"))" $ok

    # A pad drives the menu: SDL drops pad events for a window without the keyboard
    rm -f /tmp/w67-pad
    STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/w67-pad STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_BUTTON=dpright STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_FRAMES=5 \
        WAIT_FOR='Gained keyboard focus' CFG=$FX/f67-wayland.ini w67_on wayland-sf run_keys f67-walk +w67_walk
    ok=1
    grep -q "^Video: SDL's wayland driver, the " "$out/f67-walk.log" \
        && grep -qx 'Gamepad ButtonDPadRight detected' "$out/f67-walk.log" && ran_clean f67-walk && ok=0
    result "wayland: a pad drives the menu on Wayland (exit $(cat "$out/f67-walk.code"))" $ok

    # A launch: the client takes the keyboard as it maps, and the launcher gets it back when the
    # client is killed, within 2 s
    rm -f /tmp/w67-pad
    STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/w67-pad STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_BUTTON=a STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_FRAMES=5 \
        WAIT_FOR='Gained keyboard focus' CFG=$FX/f67-wayland.ini w67_on wayland-sf run_keys f67-launch +w67_launch +w67_kill
    log=$out/f67-launch.log
    sed -n '/^Application detected$/,$p' "$log" > "$out/f67-launch.after"
    ok=1
    precedes "$log" 'Lost keyboard focus' 'Application detected' \
        && precedes "$out/f67-launch.after" 'Gained keyboard focus' 'Application finished' \
        && grep -qx 'finished yes' "$out/f67-launch.seen" && ran_clean f67-launch && ok=0
    result "wayland: a launched Wayland client takes the keyboard, and its end gives it back within 2 s (exit $(cat "$out/f67-launch.code"))" $ok
    grep -E '^(Lost|Gained) keyboard focus$|^Application (detected|finished)$' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
    sed 's/^/      /' "$out/f67-launch.seen" 2> /dev/null

    # A restart's fresh copy takes the keyboard as its window maps
    w67_on wayland-sf w67_restarted f67-restart
    ok=1
    precedes "$out/f67-restart.log" 'Restarted, so the window is brought to the front' 'Gained keyboard focus' \
        && ran_clean f67-restart && ok=0
    result "wayland: a restart's fresh copy takes the keyboard on Wayland (exit $(cat "$out/f67-restart.code"))" $ok

    # Scale 2: the window asks for native pixels, so it gets the output's mode, not half of it
    w67_stop
    ok=1
    if w67_start wayland-sf2 1920 1080 2; then
        CFG=$FX/f67-quick.ini w67_on wayland-sf2 run_quick f67-scale
        grep -qx 'Screen: 3840x2160 px, pixel density 2.00, display scale 2.00' "$out/f67-scale.log" \
            && grep -qx 'Resolution:    3840x2160' "$out/f67-scale.log" && ran_clean f67-scale && ok=0
        grep -E '^(Video|Screen|Resolution):' "$out/f67-scale.log" | sed 's/^/      /'
    fi
    result "wayland: at scale 2 the window gets the output's native pixels (exit $(cat "$out/f67-scale.code" 2> /dev/null))" $ok
    w67_stop
    w67_start wayland-sf 1920 1080 1
fi

# The app id is streamflex whatever the program file is called (SDL_SetAppMetadata), not SDL's
# fallback, the file's own name: a copy named sf-renamed, with the assets beside it as the build
# has them, on Wayland on both images, with libwayland's protocol trace in its stderr
if [ -n "$w67_weston" ]; then
    mkdir -p /tmp/w67-app
    cp "$exe" /tmp/w67-app/sf-renamed
    cp -r /work/build/assets /tmp/w67-app/
    chown -R tester:tester /tmp/w67-app
    ( exe=/tmp/w67-app/sf-renamed; SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER=wayland WAYLAND_DEBUG=1 CFG=$FX/f67-quick.ini \
        w67_on wayland-sf run_quick f67-appid )
    ok=1
    grep -qF 'set_app_id("streamflex")' "$out/f67-appid.err" && ! grep -qF 'set_app_id("sf-renamed")' "$out/f67-appid.err" \
        && ran_clean f67-appid && ok=0
    result "wayland: the app id is streamflex whatever the program file is called (exit $(cat "$out/f67-appid.code"))" $ok
    grep -oF 'set_app_id("' "$out/f67-appid.err" > /dev/null || echo "      no set_app_id in the protocol trace"
fi
w67_stop
```

### Task 1.11a: Gamepad battery, the pure rules

The spec is § 7 ("Where in `src/`", the 40/45/15 rule) and unit test 12. `src/padpower.c` holds StreamFlex's power and connection enums, with SDL's values. It also holds the level rule and the pick, plus three helpers the launcher's log and draw use:
- the state's and the connection's names;
- the glyph's color, nudged off a chroma key by `chroma.h`'s rule;
- a pad nothing has been read from.

No SDL is involved, as in `layout.c` and `chroma.c`.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/padpower.h`, `src/padpower.c`
- Create: `tests/test_padpower.c`
- Modify: `src/CMakeLists.txt` (`SOURCES`, after `chroma.h` at `:21`)
- Modify: `tests/CMakeLists.txt` (after the `chroma` test, `:11-14`)
- Test: CTest `padpower`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `chroma_keep_off()` (`src/chroma.h:10-11`).
- Produces, in `src/padpower.h`. The skeleton's two signatures are exact; the rest of the module is this task's own:

                 PADPOWER_CHARGING, PADPOWER_CHARGED } PadPowerState;                 // SDL_PowerState's values
  typedef enum { PADCONN_INVALID = -1, PADCONN_UNKNOWN, PADCONN_WIRED, PADCONN_WIRELESS } PadConnection;   // SDL_JoystickConnectionState's
  typedef enum { PADPOWER_HIDDEN, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_CRITICAL } PadPowerLevel;
  typedef struct { PadPowerState state; int percent; PadConnection connection; PadPowerLevel level; } PadPower;

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing unit test**

Create `tests/test_padpower.c`:

```c
#include "check.h"
#include "padpower.h"

// A function to make one pad's power for padpower_pick()
static PadPower pad(PadPowerState state, int percent, PadPowerLevel level)
{
    PadPower power = { state, percent, PADCONN_WIRELESS, level };
    return power;
}

// A function to test a pad on its battery from no glyph: amber at or below 40 %, red at or below
// 15 %, nothing above 40 %; a percent SDL did not give (-1) or one past 100 shows nothing
static void test_on_battery(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 100, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 46, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 45, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 41, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 40, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 16, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 15, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_CRITICAL);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 0, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_CRITICAL);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, -1, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, -1, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 101, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
}

// A function to test the steps pads report in: an Xbox pad's four (10, 40, 70, 100), and a pad
// that reports in tens, walked down and back up
static void test_steps(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 100, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 70, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 40, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 10, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_CRITICAL);
    PadPowerLevel level = PADPOWER_HIDDEN;
    static const int tens[] = { 50, 40, 30, 20, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50 };
    static const PadPowerLevel shown[] = { PADPOWER_HIDDEN, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_LOW,
        PADPOWER_CRITICAL, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_LOW, PADPOWER_HIDDEN };
    for (int i = 0; i < (int) (sizeof(tens) / sizeof(tens[0])); i++) {
        level = padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, tens[i], level);
        CHECK_INT(level, shown[i]);
    }
}

// A function to test the hysteresis: once shown, the glyph hides only above 45 %, so a pad that
// reports 40 and 44 in turn does not flicker; from nothing, 44 shows nothing
static void test_hysteresis(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 44, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 45, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 46, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 44, PADPOWER_CRITICAL), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 20, PADPOWER_CRITICAL), PADPOWER_LOW);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 44, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
}

// A function to test the reports that look like a level and are not: only ON_BATTERY shows, so a
// wired Xbox pad's CHARGING 100, a charging pad at 20 %, and a driver's UNKNOWN with a percent show
// nothing, and a pad plugged in while its glyph shows hides it at once
static void test_not_on_battery(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_CHARGING, 100, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_CHARGING, 20, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_CHARGING, 20, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_CHARGED, 100, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_UNKNOWN, 40, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_UNKNOWN, 10, PADPOWER_LOW), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_UNKNOWN, -1, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_NO_BATTERY, -1, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_INT(padpower_level(PADPOWER_ERROR, -1, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
}

// A function to test the pick: the lowest pad whose glyph shows, the first of two at the same
// percent; none when no pad's glyph shows, a charging pad at a lower percent included
static void test_pick(void)
{
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(NULL, 0), -1);
    PadPower none[] = { pad(PADPOWER_CHARGING, 20, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), pad(PADPOWER_UNKNOWN, -1, PADPOWER_HIDDEN) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(none, 2), -1);
    PadPower two[] = { pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 40, PADPOWER_LOW), pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 10, PADPOWER_CRITICAL) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(two, 2), 1);
    PadPower reversed[] = { pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 10, PADPOWER_CRITICAL), pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 40, PADPOWER_LOW) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(reversed, 2), 0);
    PadPower charging[] = { pad(PADPOWER_CHARGING, 5, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 30, PADPOWER_LOW) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(charging, 2), 1);
    PadPower held[] = { pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 70, PADPOWER_HIDDEN), pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 44, PADPOWER_LOW) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(held, 2), 1);
    PadPower tie[] = { pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 30, PADPOWER_LOW), pad(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY, 30, PADPOWER_LOW) };
    CHECK_INT(padpower_pick(tie, 2), 0);
}

// A function to test a pad nothing has been read from, and the names the log uses
static void test_unknown_and_names(void)
{
    PadPower power = padpower_unknown();
    CHECK_INT(power.state, PADPOWER_UNKNOWN);
    CHECK_INT(power.percent, -1);
    CHECK_INT(power.connection, PADCONN_UNKNOWN);
    CHECK_INT(power.level, PADPOWER_HIDDEN);
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_ERROR), "error");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_UNKNOWN), "unknown");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY), "on battery");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_NO_BATTERY), "no battery");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_CHARGING), "charging");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_state_name(PADPOWER_CHARGED), "charged");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_connection_name(PADCONN_INVALID), "unknown");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_connection_name(PADCONN_UNKNOWN), "unknown");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_connection_name(PADCONN_WIRED), "wired");
    CHECK_STR(padpower_connection_name(PADCONN_WIRELESS), "wireless");
}

// A function to test the glyph's colors: amber and red, left alone by the shipped key (#010101),
// and moved two steps off a key set on them or one step off them, by chroma.h's rule
static void test_color(void)
{
    unsigned char rgb[3];
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_LOW, NULL, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xFF && rgb[1] == 0xB0 && rgb[2] == 0x00);
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_CRITICAL, NULL, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xE8 && rgb[1] == 0x20 && rgb[2] == 0x20);
    const unsigned char shipped[3] = { 0x01, 0x01, 0x01 };
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_LOW, shipped, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xFF && rgb[1] == 0xB0 && rgb[2] == 0x00);
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_CRITICAL, shipped, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xE8 && rgb[1] == 0x20 && rgb[2] == 0x20);
    const unsigned char amber[3] = { 0xFF, 0xB0, 0x00 };
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_LOW, amber, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xFD && rgb[1] == 0xAE && rgb[2] == 0x02);
    const unsigned char near_red[3] = { 0xE9, 0x21, 0x1F };
    padpower_color(PADPOWER_CRITICAL, near_red, rgb);
    CHECK(rgb[0] == 0xE7 && rgb[1] == 0x23 && rgb[2] == 0x21);
}

int main(void)
{
    test_on_battery();
    test_steps();
    test_hysteresis();
    test_not_on_battery();
    test_pick();
    test_unknown_and_names();
    test_color();
    return check_report();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the module**

Create `src/padpower.h`:

```c
// The gamepad battery glyph's rules (spec section 7): the level a pad's power report shows, and the
// pad the one glyph speaks for. Pure: no SDL, so tests/test_padpower.c builds it on its own (with
// chroma.c, whose rule keeps the glyph's color off a chroma key). The state and connection enums
// hold SDL's own values, which launcher.c checks when it compiles.
#ifndef PADPOWER_H
#define PADPOWER_H

#define PADPOWER_LOW_PERCENT 40        // On its battery at or below this, a pad shows the glyph, amber...
#define PADPOWER_CRITICAL_PERCENT 15   // ...and red at or below this
#define PADPOWER_HIDE_PERCENT 45       // Once shown, it hides only above this, so a pad that reports in
                                       // steps of ten does not flicker at the edge

typedef enum {   // SDL_PowerState's values
    PADPOWER_ERROR = -1,
    PADPOWER_UNKNOWN,
    PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY,
    PADPOWER_NO_BATTERY,
    PADPOWER_CHARGING,
    PADPOWER_CHARGED
} PadPowerState;

typedef enum {   // SDL_JoystickConnectionState's values
    PADCONN_INVALID = -1,
    PADCONN_UNKNOWN,
    PADCONN_WIRED,
    PADCONN_WIRELESS
} PadConnection;

typedef enum {
    PADPOWER_HIDDEN,
    PADPOWER_LOW,
    PADPOWER_CRITICAL
} PadPowerLevel;

// One pad's power, as StreamFlex holds it
typedef struct {
    PadPowerState state;
    int percent;               // -1: the pad gave none
    PadConnection connection;
    PadPowerLevel level;       // What padpower_level() last gave this pad
} PadPower;

PadPowerLevel padpower_level(PadPowerState state, int percent, PadPowerLevel shown);   // shown: the pad's level until now
int padpower_pick(const PadPower *pads, int count);   // The lowest pad whose glyph shows; -1 for none
PadPower padpower_unknown(void);                      // A pad nothing has been read from
const char *padpower_state_name(PadPowerState state);             // For the log: "on battery", "charging", ...
const char *padpower_connection_name(PadConnection connection);   // "wired", "wireless" or "unknown"
void padpower_color(PadPowerLevel level, const unsigned char *key, unsigned char rgb[3]);   // key: an RGB to keep off, or NULL

#endif
```

Create `src/padpower.c`:

```c
#include <string.h>
#include "padpower.h"
#include "chroma.h"

// The glyph's colors, both far from the shipped chroma key (#010101)
static const unsigned char AMBER[3] = { 0xFF, 0xB0, 0x00 };
static const unsigned char RED[3] = { 0xE8, 0x20, 0x20 };

// A function to work out the glyph a pad's report shows: only a pad on its battery with a percent
// shows one, from 40 % down (red from 15 %), and once shown it stays until the pad is above 45 %
PadPowerLevel padpower_level(PadPowerState state, int percent, PadPowerLevel shown)
{
    if (state != PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY || percent < 0 || percent > 100)
        return PADPOWER_HIDDEN;
    if (percent <= PADPOWER_CRITICAL_PERCENT)
        return PADPOWER_CRITICAL;
    if (percent <= PADPOWER_LOW_PERCENT || (shown != PADPOWER_HIDDEN && percent <= PADPOWER_HIDE_PERCENT))
        return PADPOWER_LOW;
    return PADPOWER_HIDDEN;
}

// A function to pick the pad the one glyph speaks for: the lowest of those whose glyph shows, the
// first of two at the same percent; -1 when none shows
int padpower_pick(const PadPower *pads, int count)
{
    int lowest = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) {
        if (pads[i].level != PADPOWER_HIDDEN && (lowest < 0 || pads[i].percent < pads[lowest].percent))
            lowest = i;
    }
    return lowest;
}

// A function to give a pad nothing has been read from: unknown, with no percent and no glyph
PadPower padpower_unknown(void)
{
    PadPower power = { PADPOWER_UNKNOWN, -1, PADCONN_UNKNOWN, PADPOWER_HIDDEN };
    return power;
}

// A function to name a power state for the log
const char *padpower_state_name(PadPowerState state)
{
    switch (state) {
        case PADPOWER_ERROR:
            return "error";
        case PADPOWER_ON_BATTERY:
            return "on battery";
        case PADPOWER_NO_BATTERY:
            return "no battery";
        case PADPOWER_CHARGING:
            return "charging";
        case PADPOWER_CHARGED:
            return "charged";
        default:
            return "unknown";
    }
}

// A function to name a connection for the log; SDL's invalid one reads as unknown
const char *padpower_connection_name(PadConnection connection)
{
    if (connection == PADCONN_WIRED)
        return "wired";
    return connection == PADCONN_WIRELESS ? "wireless" : "unknown";
}

// A function to give the glyph's color for its level, moved off the chroma key KEY (RGB) by
// chroma.h's rule when it lands within a step of it; NULL: no key to keep off
void padpower_color(PadPowerLevel level, const unsigned char *key, unsigned char rgb[3])
{
    const unsigned char *color = level == PADPOWER_CRITICAL ? RED : AMBER;
    unsigned char rgba[4] = { color[0], color[1], color[2], 0xFF };
    if (key != NULL)
        chroma_keep_off(rgba, 1, 1, 4, key[0], key[1], key[2]);
    memcpy(rgb, rgba, 3);
}
```

In `src/CMakeLists.txt`, after `chroma.h` (`:21`), add the two lines `  padpower.c` and `  padpower.h`. If Task 1.10's `videodriver.c` / `.h` are already there, add them after those.

### Task 1.11b: Gamepad battery, the pads, the glyph and its check

This task covers spec § 7 ("What the user sees", "Where in `src/`", "Log lines", "Test hook") and § 9's `66-pad-power.sh`:
- Each pad reads and logs its power when it opens. A battery event updates it from the event's own fields. Closing the pads for a launch resets them to unknown.
- `draw_pad_battery()` draws the glyph from filled rectangles in the top corner opposite the clock, at the clock's margin, after the clock and never in the preview.
- The harness pushes battery events through `STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER`.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/launcher.h`:
  - its includes (`:1-2`);
  - its launcher parameters (`:18-30`);
  - the `Gamepad` struct (`:156-161`).
- Modify: `src/util.h` (after `UNUSED`, `:18`). `COMPILE_CHECK` moves here, so `launcher.c` can use it.
- Modify: `src/settings_screen.c` (delete the macro and its comment, `:254-256`)
- Modify: `src/launcher.c`:
  - the includes, `:11-23`, and the prototypes, `:25-86`;
  - the enum checks, after the globals at `:194-234`;
  - `init_gamepad()`, `:1625-1634`;
  - `open_controller()`, `:1648-1668`;
  - `disconnect_gamepad()`, `:1728-1731`;
  - `draw_scene()`, `:1445-1450`;
  - the hooks, `test_pad_plug()` `:1814-1837` and `test_pad_update()` `:1845-1872`;
  - `main()`'s event switch, after the joystick-removed case at `:2515-2520`.
- Modify: `CONTRIBUTING.md` (hook list `:57-61`, and a new line after `:61`)
- Create: `tests/headless/checks/66-pad-power.sh`, `tests/headless/fixtures/f66-power.ini`, `tests/headless/fixtures/f66-right.ini`
- Test: `66-pad-power.sh` (Debian and Fedora legs), and a compile check proven by a mutant

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes:
  - Task 1.11a's `padpower.h`;
  - `Gamepad` as Task 1.2 left it: `SDL_Gamepad *controller` and the instance id in `id`;
  - SDL3's `SDL_GetGamepadPowerInfo`, `SDL_GetGamepadConnectionState`, `SDL_EVENT_JOYSTICK_BATTERY_UPDATED` and `event.jbattery` (`which`, `state`, `percent`, all at 3.2.10);
  - `config.clock_alignment`, `eff.clock_margin` and `config.chroma_key_color`.
- Produces:
  - `Gamepad.power` (`PadPower`);
  - `static void draw_pad_battery(void)`, `static void log_pad_power(const char *what, const Gamepad *gamepad)` and `static void gamepad_power_changed(SDL_JoystickID id, SDL_PowerState state, int percent)` in `launcher.c`;
  - `#define COMPILE_CHECK(name, condition)` in `util.h`;
  - the hook `STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER=<file>`;
  - these five log lines, byte-exact (debug):
    - `Gamepad power: instance id %i, <connection>, <state>, <percent>`
    - `Gamepad power changed: instance id %i, <connection>, <state>, <percent>`
    - `Battery indicator: shown, low, %i%% at %i,%i %ix%i`
    - `Battery indicator: shown, critical, %i%% at %i,%i %ix%i`
    - `Battery indicator: hidden`
  - In those lines, `<connection>` is `unknown`, `wired` or `wireless`, and `<state>` is `unknown`, `on battery`, `no battery`, `charging`, `charged` or `error`. `<percent>` is `40%`, or `no percent` for -1.
  - `Gamepad opened at device index %i, instance id %i` stays as it is (smoke rows 1.26 and 1.27).

**The glyph's geometry.** It is `h = max(screen_height / 60, 9)` tall and `2h` wide, at the clock's margin from the top. Its side is the one the clock is not aligned to: top right for a left clock, top left for a right one. It is built from filled rectangles:
- four edges, `t = max(h / 9, 1)` thick, round a body `2h - nub` wide;
- a terminal `nub = max(h / 6, 2)` wide and `h - 2 * (h / 4)` tall, at `h / 4` from the top;
- a charge bar inside, a gap of `t` from the edges, half the inside width for low and a quarter for critical.

On a 1920x1080 screen with the default 5 % margin, it is `36x18` at `1830,54` for a left clock and at `54,54` for a right one. Its left edge's middle is at `(x, y + 9)`, and its charge bar holds `(x + 5, y + 9)`.

Create `tests/headless/checks/66-pad-power.sh`:

```bash
# The low-battery glyph (spec section 7). The harness build's STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER file holds
# one line, "<state> <percent>", with an optional pad letter in front, and each new line is pushed
# as the battery event a real pad sends, for the first pad StreamFlex holds (A) or the second (B).
# The rules:
# - the glyph shows only for a pad on its battery at or below 40 % (amber), red at or below 15 %;
# - once shown, it hides again only above 45 %;
# - it takes the top corner opposite the clock's alignment, at the clock's margin, on or off;
# - it is never drawn under the settings screen.
# This proves StreamFlex's handling and the glyph's levels, colors and corner, not that SDL's drivers
# read a real pad: only hands-on hardware can.
F66_AMBER=255,176,0
F66_RED=232,32,32
F66_BLACK=0,0,0
f66_steps=0
f66_at=1830

# A function to write the power file's line whole: the launcher reads the file once a frame
f66_write() { printf '%s\n' "$1" > /tmp/f66-power.tmp && mv -f /tmp/f66-power.tmp /tmp/f66-power; }

# A function to wait up to 20 s for the log to hold N lines holding LINE (PID: the launcher)
f66_wait_count() {
    local line=$1 n=$2 pid=$3 i c
    for i in $(seq 100); do
        c=$(grep -cF -- "$line" "$LOG" 2> /dev/null)
        [ "${c:-0}" -ge "$n" ] && return 0
        running "$pid" || break
        sleep 0.2
    done
    c=$(grep -cF -- "$line" "$LOG" 2> /dev/null)
    [ "${c:-0}" -ge "$n" ]
}

# A function to read the screen at the glyph's left edge and inside its charge bar, for the glyph at
# X,54 18 px tall, and write "TAG yes" to NAME.seen when both show COLOR, else "TAG no"
f66_look() {
    local name=$1 tag=$2 x=$3 color=$4 t=2 mid=63
    if screen_shows "$name" "$tag" "$x,$mid=$color" "$((x + 2 * t + 1)),$mid=$color"; then
        echo "$tag yes" >> "$out/$name.seen"
    else
        echo "$tag no" >> "$out/$name.seen"
    fi
}

# A function for one step of a run (NAME, PID, TAG, the file's LINE, the COLOR then expected at the
# glyph's place): write the line, wait for the launcher's next "Gamepad power changed" line and a
# second more for the frame that draws it, then look
f66_step() {
    local name=$1 pid=$2 tag=$3 line=$4 color=$5
    f66_steps=$((f66_steps + 1))
    f66_write "$line"
    if ! f66_wait_count 'Gamepad power changed: ' "$f66_steps" "$pid"; then
        echo "$tag: the launcher never took '$line'" >> "$out/$name.pixels"
        echo "$tag no" >> "$out/$name.seen"
        return
    fi
    sleep 1
    f66_look "$name" "$tag" "$f66_at" "$color"
}

# The steps, as +keys (see run_keys)
f66_none()     { f66_look "$1" none "$f66_at" "$F66_BLACK"; }
f66_low()      { f66_step "$1" "$2" low 'battery 40' "$F66_AMBER"; }
f66_critical() { f66_step "$1" "$2" critical 'battery 10' "$F66_RED"; }
f66_full()     { f66_step "$1" "$2" full 'battery 100' "$F66_BLACK"; }
f66_charging() { f66_step "$1" "$2" charging 'charging 20' "$F66_BLACK"; }
f66_charged()  { f66_step "$1" "$2" charged 'charged 100' "$F66_BLACK"; }
f66_unknown()  { f66_step "$1" "$2" unknown 'unknown -1' "$F66_BLACK"; }
f66_again()    { f66_step "$1" "$2" again 'battery 40' "$F66_AMBER"; }
f66_hold()     { f66_step "$1" "$2" hold 'battery 44' "$F66_AMBER"; }
f66_hide()     { f66_step "$1" "$2" hide 'battery 46' "$F66_BLACK"; }
# The same report again, for pad A by its letter: the hook pushes it, and nothing changes or is logged
f66_same() {
    f66_write 'A battery 46'
    f66_wait_count "Test hook: pad A's power is battery 46" 2 "$2" && sleep 1
    if [ "$(grep -c '^Gamepad power changed: ' "$LOG")" = "$f66_steps" ]; then
        echo "same yes" >> "$out/$1.seen"
    else
        echo "same no" >> "$out/$1.seen"
    fi
}

# One pad, the clock off and aligned left: the glyph's levels, colors and edges, top right
rm -f /tmp/f66-power /tmp/f66-power.tmp /tmp/pad-none
f66_steps=0 f66_at=1830
STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/pad-none STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER=/tmp/f66-power WAIT_FOR='Gamepad power: ' \
    run_keys f66-power +f66_none +f66_low +f66_critical +f66_full +f66_charging +f66_charged +f66_unknown \
    +f66_again +f66_hold +f66_hide +f66_same
log=$out/f66-power.log
seen=$out/f66-power.seen
indicator=$(grep '^Battery indicator: ' "$log" | tr '\n' '|')
ok=1
grep -qxE 'Gamepad power: instance id [0-9]+, unknown, unknown, no percent' "$log" \
    && grep -qx 'none yes' "$seen" && ran_clean f66-power && ok=0
result "battery: a pad that reports nothing logs unknown power and draws no glyph (exit $(cat "$out/f66-power.code"))" $ok
ok=1
case $indicator in
    'Battery indicator: shown, low, 40% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: shown, critical, 10% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: hidden|'*) ok=0 ;;
esac
[ $ok = 0 ] && grep -qx 'low yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'critical yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'full yes' "$seen" || ok=1
result "battery: on battery at 40% the glyph is amber, at 10% red, at 100% gone, top right at the clock's margin" $ok
echo "      ${indicator:-no indicator lines}"
ok=1
grep -qx 'charging yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'charged yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'unknown yes' "$seen" && ok=0
result "battery: a charging, charged or unknown pad draws nothing" $ok
ok=1
[ "$indicator" = 'Battery indicator: shown, low, 40% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: shown, critical, 10% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: hidden|Battery indicator: shown, low, 40% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: hidden|' ] \
    && grep -qx 'again yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'hold yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'hide yes' "$seen" && ok=0
result "battery: 44% after 40% keeps the glyph, and 46% hides it" $ok
ok=0
for want in 'on battery, 40%' 'on battery, 10%' 'on battery, 100%' 'charging, 20%' 'charged, 100%' \
            'unknown, no percent' 'on battery, 44%' 'on battery, 46%'; do
    grep -qE "^Gamepad power changed: instance id [0-9]+, unknown, $want\$" "$log" || { ok=1; echo "      no change to '$want' logged"; }
done
grep -qx 'same yes' "$seen" || ok=1
result "battery: each report is logged with the pad's state and percent, and one that changes nothing is not" $ok
sed 's/^/      /' "$seen" 2> /dev/null

# Two pads: the lower is drawn, and pulling it out hides the glyph when the other is charging
f66_plugged()  { f66_wait_count 'Gamepad power: ' 2 "$2" || echo "plug: the second pad never opened" >> "$out/$1.pixels"; }
f66_a_low()    { f66_step "$1" "$2" a-low 'A battery 40' "$F66_AMBER"; }
f66_b_low()    { f66_step "$1" "$2" b-critical 'B battery 10' "$F66_RED"; }
f66_a_charge() { f66_step "$1" "$2" a-charging 'A charging 50' "$F66_RED"; }
f66_unplug()   { rm -f /tmp/pad-plug; wait_line 'Battery indicator: hidden' "$2"; sleep 1; f66_look "$1" unplugged "$f66_at" "$F66_BLACK"; }
rm -f /tmp/f66-power /tmp/pad-none
: > /tmp/pad-plug
chmod 666 /tmp/pad-plug
f66_steps=0 f66_at=1830
STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/pad-none STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_PLUG=/tmp/pad-plug STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER=/tmp/f66-power \
    CFG=$FX/f66-power.ini WAIT_FOR='Test hook: pad plugged in' \
    run_keys f66-two +f66_plugged +f66_a_low +f66_b_low +f66_a_charge +f66_unplug
seen=$out/f66-two.seen
ok=1
[ "$(grep '^Battery indicator: ' "$out/f66-two.log" | tr '\n' '|')" = 'Battery indicator: shown, low, 40% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: shown, critical, 10% at 1830,54 36x18|Battery indicator: hidden|' ] \
    && grep -qx 'a-low yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'b-critical yes' "$seen" && grep -qx 'a-charging yes' "$seen" \
    && grep -qx 'unplugged yes' "$seen" && ran_clean f66-two && ok=0
result "battery: with two pads the lower is drawn, and pulling it out hides the glyph (exit $(cat "$out/f66-two.code"))" $ok
sed 's/^/      /' "$seen" 2> /dev/null
rm -f /tmp/pad-plug

# The clock on and aligned right: the glyph takes the top left corner
f66_right() { f66_step "$1" "$2" right-low 'battery 40' "$F66_AMBER"; }
rm -f /tmp/f66-power
f66_steps=0 f66_at=54
STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/pad-none STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER=/tmp/f66-power CFG=$FX/f66-right.ini WAIT_FOR='Gamepad power: ' \
    run_keys f66-right +f66_right
ok=1
grep -qx 'Battery indicator: shown, low, 40% at 54,54 36x18' "$out/f66-right.log" \
    && grep -qx 'right-low yes' "$out/f66-right.seen" && ran_clean f66-right && ok=0
result "battery: with the clock on the right the glyph takes the top left corner (exit $(cat "$out/f66-right.code"))" $ok

# Settings open over a shown glyph: with no render targets the scene fills the screen behind the
# settings (a test hook), drawn as the preview is, and the glyph's place there stays black
f66_preview() { wait_line 'Settings opened over menu' "$2"; sleep 1; f66_look "$1" preview "$f66_at" "$F66_BLACK"; }
rm -f /tmp/f66-power
f66_steps=0 f66_at=1830
STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD=/tmp/pad-none STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER=/tmp/f66-power STREAMFLEX_TEST_NO_RENDER_TARGETS=1 \
    CFG=$FX/f66-power.ini WAIT_FOR='Gamepad power: ' run_keys f66-preview +f66_critical Menu +f66_preview
ok=1
grep -qx 'critical yes' "$out/f66-preview.seen" && grep -qx 'preview yes' "$out/f66-preview.seen" \
    && ran_clean f66-preview && ok=0
result "battery: the glyph is never drawn under the settings screen (exit $(cat "$out/f66-preview.code"))" $ok
rm -f /tmp/f66-power /tmp/f66-power.tmp
```

```c
// A function to log a pad's power as StreamFlex holds it: WHAT is "Gamepad power" as it opens and
// "Gamepad power changed" when a battery event changes it
static void log_pad_power(const char *what, const Gamepad *gamepad)
{
    char percent[16];
    if (gamepad->power.percent < 0)
        snprintf(percent, sizeof(percent), "no percent");
    else
        snprintf(percent, sizeof(percent), "%i%%", gamepad->power.percent);
    log_debug("%s: instance id %i, %s, %s, %s", what, (int) gamepad->id,
        padpower_connection_name(gamepad->power.connection), padpower_state_name(gamepad->power.state), percent);
}

// A function to take a battery event for the pad its instance id names, with the state and percent
// the event itself carries, never asked of SDL again (the harness's pushed event would prove nothing
// otherwise, since its virtual pad reads unknown). A report that changes nothing is not logged.
static void gamepad_power_changed(SDL_JoystickID id, SDL_PowerState state, int percent)
{
    for (Gamepad *pad = gamepads; pad != NULL; pad = pad->next) {
        if ((SDL_JoystickID) pad->id != id)
            continue;
        if (pad->power.state == (PadPowerState) state && pad->power.percent == percent)
            return;
        pad->power.state = (PadPowerState) state;
        pad->power.percent = percent;
        pad->power.level = padpower_level(pad->power.state, percent, pad->power.level);
        log_pad_power("Gamepad power changed", pad);
        return;
    }
}
```

In `disconnect_gamepad()`'s `else` branch (`:1728-1731`), the branch that closes a pad for a launch and keeps it in the list, add `i->power = padpower_unknown();` after `i->controller = NULL;`. The glyph returns with the first report after `post_launch()`, whose `open_controller()` reads it again.

In `main()`'s event switch, after the joystick-removed case (`:2515-2520`), add:

```c

                    gamepad_power_changed(event.jbattery.which, event.jbattery.state, event.jbattery.percent);
                    break;
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Draw the glyph**

After `draw_checkerboard()` (which ends at `launcher.c:1401`), before `draw_scene()`'s comment, add:

```c
// A function to draw the low-battery glyph for the lowest pad on its battery, from filled
// rectangles (no texture: nothing to rebuild after a device loss): in the top corner opposite the
// clock's alignment, at the clock's margin whether or not the clock is on, amber when low and red
// when critical, nudged off a chroma key on Linux. The log says when it shows, changes level or
// place, or hides.
static void draw_pad_battery()
{
    PadPower powers[MAX_BATTERY_PADS];
    int count = 0;
    for (Gamepad *pad = gamepads; pad != NULL && count < MAX_BATTERY_PADS; pad = pad->next) {
        if (pad->controller != NULL)
            powers[count++] = pad->power;
    }
    int lowest = padpower_pick(powers, count);
    PadPowerLevel level = lowest >= 0 ? powers[lowest].level : PADPOWER_HIDDEN;
    if (level == PADPOWER_HIDDEN) {
        if (battery_shown != PADPOWER_HIDDEN)
            log_debug("Battery indicator: hidden");
        battery_shown = PADPOWER_HIDDEN;
        return;
    }
    int h = geo.screen_height / BATTERY_GLYPH_DIVISOR > BATTERY_GLYPH_MIN ? geo.screen_height / BATTERY_GLYPH_DIVISOR : BATTERY_GLYPH_MIN;
    int w = 2 * h;
    SDL_Rect rect = {
        config.clock_alignment == ALIGNMENT_RIGHT ? eff.clock_margin : geo.screen_width - eff.clock_margin - w,
        eff.clock_margin, w, h
    };
    if (level != battery_shown || rect.x != battery_rect.x || rect.y != battery_rect.y || rect.h != battery_rect.h)
        log_debug("Battery indicator: shown, %s, %i%% at %i,%i %ix%i", level == PADPOWER_CRITICAL ? "critical" : "low",
            powers[lowest].percent, rect.x, rect.y, rect.w, rect.h);
    battery_shown = level;
    battery_rect = rect;

    unsigned char rgb[3];
#ifdef _WIN32
    padpower_color(level, NULL, rgb);   // Windows has no chroma key
#else
    const unsigned char key[3] = { config.chroma_key_color.r, config.chroma_key_color.g, config.chroma_key_color.b };
    padpower_color(level, key, rgb);
#endif
    int t = h / 9 > 1 ? h / 9 : 1;
    int nub = h / 6 > 2 ? h / 6 : 2;
    int body = w - nub;
    int inside = body - 4 * t;
    int charge = level == PADPOWER_LOW ? inside / 2 : inside / 4;
    SDL_Rect parts[6] = {
        { rect.x, rect.y, body, t },                         // The edges
        { rect.x, rect.y + h - t, body, t },
        { rect.x, rect.y, t, h },
        { rect.x + body - t, rect.y, t, h },
        { rect.x + body, rect.y + h / 4, nub, h - 2 * (h / 4) },   // The terminal
        { rect.x + 2 * t, rect.y + 2 * t, charge, h - 4 * t }      // The charge left
    };
    SDL_FRect drawn[6];
    for (int i = 0; i < 6; i++)
        drawn[i] = (SDL_FRect) { (float) parts[i].x, (float) parts[i].y, (float) parts[i].w, (float) parts[i].h };
    Uint8 r, g, b, a;
    SDL_GetRenderDrawColor(renderer, &r, &g, &b, &a);
    SDL_SetRenderDrawColor(renderer, rgb[0], rgb[1], rgb[2], 0xFF);
    SDL_RenderFillRects(renderer, drawn, 6);
    SDL_SetRenderDrawColor(renderer, r, g, b, a);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: The test hook**

After `test_pad_plug()` (ends at `launcher.c:1837`), add:

```c
// A function only the headless harness builds: with STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER set, it reads the file
// that names once a frame and, each time its line changes, pushes the battery event a real pad sends,
// with the line's state and percent: "<state> <percent>" (battery 40, charging 80, unknown -1), the
// state one of unknown, battery, none, charging, charged or error, for the first pad StreamFlex holds,
// or with A or B in front for the first or the second. The handler reads the event as it reads SDL's.
static void test_pad_power()
{
    static char last[64] = "";
    static const struct { const char *name; SDL_PowerState state; } states[] = {
        { "unknown", SDL_POWERSTATE_UNKNOWN }, { "battery", SDL_POWERSTATE_ON_BATTERY },
        { "none", SDL_POWERSTATE_NO_BATTERY }, { "charging", SDL_POWERSTATE_CHARGING },
        { "charged", SDL_POWERSTATE_CHARGED }, { "error", SDL_POWERSTATE_ERROR }
    };
    const char *path = getenv("STREAMFLEX_TEST_PAD_POWER");
    if (path == NULL || !gamepad_on)
        return;
    char *text = fileio_read_all(path, NULL);
    char line[64];
    snprintf(line, sizeof(line), "%s", text != NULL ? text : "");
    alloc_free(text);
    line[strcspn(line, "\r\n")] = '\0';
    if (strcmp(line, last) == 0)
        return;
    snprintf(last, sizeof(last), "%s", line);
    if (line[0] == '\0')
        return;
    char letter = 'A';
    const char *rest = line;
    if ((line[0] == 'A' || line[0] == 'B') && line[1] == ' ') {
        letter = line[0];
        rest = line + 2;
    }
    const char *space = strchr(rest, ' ');
    size_t length = space != NULL ? (size_t) (space - rest) : 0;
    char *end = NULL;
    long percent = space != NULL ? strtol(space + 1, &end, 10) : 0;
    size_t s = 0;
    while (s < SDL_arraysize(states) && !(strlen(states[s].name) == length && strncmp(states[s].name, rest, length) == 0))
        s++;
    if (space == NULL || end == space + 1 || *end != '\0' || s == SDL_arraysize(states)) {
        log_error("Test hook: the power file's line '%s' is not '[A|B] <state> <percent>'", line);
        return;
    }
    Gamepad *pad = gamepads;
    if (letter == 'B' && pad != NULL)
        pad = pad->next;
    if (pad == NULL) {
        log_error("Test hook: no pad %c for the power file's line '%s'", letter, line);
        return;
    }
    SDL_Event battery;
    SDL_zero(battery);
    battery.type = SDL_EVENT_JOYSTICK_BATTERY_UPDATED;
    battery.jbattery.which = (SDL_JoystickID) pad->id;
    battery.jbattery.state = states[s].state;
    battery.jbattery.percent = (int) percent;
    log_debug("Test hook: pad %c's power is %s %i", letter, states[s].name, (int) percent);
    if (!SDL_PushEvent(&battery))
        log_error("Test hook: the battery event was not pushed\n%s", SDL_GetError());
}
```

### Task 1.12: Startup timing, the shipped half

The spec is § 8 ("What ships", "Image formats are primed") and § 9's "Timing".
- `load_icon()` and `load_texture_measured()` read with `SDL_LoadFile` and decode with `IMG_LoadTyped_IO`, from memory, with the file's extension as the type, as `IMG_Load` passes it. `IMG_Load_IO` passes none and is never used. The slideshow's loader does the same, so its line splits the read from the decode.
- The `fileio_not_a_file` guard stays in front of every read.
- Three `Timing:` debug lines report in whole milliseconds. `Menu '%s': rendered its buttons at %i px` stays byte-identical.
- PNG, JPEG and WebP are primed on the main thread before any decoding thread starts.

The measurement points are Phase 0's throwaway patch's (`ef32c93`, branch `spike/sdl2-timing`), ported to SDL3 calls. Two changes are made on the way:
- the slideshow's read and decode are timed apart, which the patch could not do without changing the loader;
- only the three lines the spec ships are kept. The patch's `opened in`, `slideshow folder scanned` and `ms after SDL started` lines are not.

**The single image's line.** The spec gives `Timing: background: Slideshow image in 31 ms (read 1, decode 24, luminance 4, texture 2)` "and the same shape for a single image". This plan writes the single image as `Timing: background: image in 31 ms (read 1, decode 24, luminance 4, texture 2)`. It is logged from `load_texture_measured()`, which also serves a slideshow folder with one image (`launcher.c:775`).

Create `tests/headless/checks/60-timing.sh`:

```bash
# Startup timing (spec section 8). With -d:
# - each menu render logs one Timing: line beside its "rendered its buttons" line, which stays as it
#   was;
# - a background image, or the slideshow's first image, logs one;
# - the program loop's start logs how long after the window opened the first menu was shown.
# Every figure is a whole number of milliseconds. A line's parts add up to no more than its total
# plus 2 ms, the rounding of up to four parts and the total. No absolute time is asserted: ASan and
# lavapipe distort it. The icons are one 512 px PNG drawn 48 times in each of two menus, so decoding
# takes long enough for a part counted twice to show in a sum.
# The image formats are primed on the main thread before anything decodes (SDL_image sets each
# format up on its first load, unsynchronized), and a priming that fails says so.
rm -rf "$TESTER_HOME/t60"
mkdir -p "$TESTER_HOME/t60"
python3 "$HERE/make_images.py" --keyed "$TESTER_HOME/t60/icon.png" 0,200,200 rgba
printf 'not a picture\n' > "$TESTER_HOME/t60/broken.png"
chown -R tester:tester "$TESTER_HOME/t60"

T60_PARTS='\(read [0-9]+, decode [0-9]+, texture [0-9]+\)'
T60_BACKGROUND_PARTS='\(read [0-9]+, decode [0-9]+, luminance [0-9]+, texture [0-9]+\)'

# A function to tell whether a Timing: line's parts (inside its brackets) add up to no more than the
# total before them plus 2 ms
t60_adds_up() {
    local line=$1 total sum=0 n
    total=$(sed -nE 's/.* in ([0-9]+) ms \(.*/\1/p' <<< "$line")
    for n in $(sed -nE 's/.*\(([^)]*)\).*/\1/p' <<< "$line" | grep -oE '[0-9]+'); do sum=$((sum + n)); done
    [ -n "$total" ] && [ "$sum" -le $((total + 2)) ]
}

# A function to check a menu's render in the log LOG: one "rendered its buttons" line, as it always
# read, one Timing: line of the shipped shape with 48 icons and 48 titles, and its parts adding up
t60_menu() {
    local log=$1 menu=$2 lines
    lines=$(grep -E "^Timing: menu '$menu': " "$log")
    [ "$(grep -cE "^Menu '$menu': rendered its buttons at [0-9]+ px\$" "$log")" = 1 ] \
        && [ "$(grep -c . <<< "$lines")" = 1 ] \
        && grep -qE "^Timing: menu '$menu': 48 icons at [0-9]+ px in [0-9]+ ms $T60_PARTS, 48 titles in [0-9]+ ms\$" <<< "$lines" \
        && t60_adds_up "$lines"
}

UNTIL="Timing: menu 'More': " run_keys f60-icons Return
log=$out/f60-icons.log
ok=1
t60_menu "$log" Main && t60_menu "$log" More && ran_clean f60-icons && ok=0
result "timing: each menu render logs one Timing: line beside its rendered line, its figures adding up (exit $(cat "$out/f60-icons.code"))" $ok
grep -E "^Timing: |^Menu '(Main|More)': rendered" "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
ok=1
background=$(grep -E '^Timing: background: ' "$log")
[ "$(grep -c . <<< "$background")" = 1 ] \
    && grep -qE "^Timing: background: image in [0-9]+ ms $T60_BACKGROUND_PARTS\$" <<< "$background" \
    && t60_adds_up "$background" && ok=0
result "timing: a background image logs its read, decode, luminance and texture" $ok
ok=1
[ "$(grep -cE '^Timing: startup: first menu shown [0-9]+ ms after the window opened$' "$log")" = 1 ] && ok=0
result "timing: the program loop's start logs when the first menu was shown" $ok
ok=1
[ "$(grep -cx 'Images: primed the PNG, JPEG and WebP loaders' "$log")" = 1 ] \
    && precedes "$log" 'Images: primed the PNG, JPEG and WebP loaders' 'Timing: background: ' \
    && ! grep -q 'Could not prime' "$log" && ok=0
result "timing: the PNG, JPEG and WebP loaders are primed before anything decodes" $ok
ok=1
reason=$(grep -A1 -xF 'Could not load image /home/tester/t60/broken.png' "$log" | sed -n 2p)
[ -n "$reason" ] && [ "$reason" != 'not a regular file' ] \
    && grep -qE "^Menu 'More': rendered its buttons at [0-9]+ px\$" "$log" && ok=0
result "timing: an icon that is not an image logs SDL_image's reason, and the menu still renders" $ok
echo "      ${reason:-no reason logged}"

# The slideshow's first image, loaded on the main thread
CFG=$FX/f30-slideshow.ini run_quick f60-slides
ok=1
slide=$(grep -E '^Timing: background: ' "$out/f60-slides.log")
[ "$(grep -c . <<< "$slide")" = 1 ] \
    && grep -qE "^Timing: background: Slideshow image in [0-9]+ ms $T60_BACKGROUND_PARTS\$" <<< "$slide" \
    && t60_adds_up "$slide" && ran_clean f60-slides && ok=0
result "timing: the slideshow's first image logs its read, decode, luminance and texture (exit $(cat "$out/f60-slides.code"))" $ok
echo "      ${slide:-no Timing: background: line}"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The timed loader, the counters and the priming (`image.c`, `image.h`)**

After `NSVGrasterizer *rasterizer = NULL;` (`image.c:37`), add:

```c
// What render_buttons' icons cost in its current render, in performance-counter counts: their reads,
// their decodes (with the chroma pass, which a decode worker would run too) and their textures. Main
// thread only: render_buttons zeroes them, and the slideshow's thread never touches them.
Uint64 timing_read = 0, timing_decode = 0, timing_texture = 0;

// A function to turn performance-counter counts into whole milliseconds, rounded
unsigned timing_ms(Uint64 counts)
{
    Uint64 frequency = SDL_GetPerformanceFrequency();
    return (unsigned) ((counts * 1000 + frequency / 2) / frequency);
}

// A function to load an image file as IMG_Load does, in two timed parts: the read (SDL_LoadFile)
// and the decode (IMG_LoadTyped_IO from memory, with the file's extension as the type, as IMG_Load
// passes it; IMG_Load_IO would pass none), adding each part's counts to *read and *decode. NULL,
// with SDL's reason in SDL_GetError(), when either fails. It touches nothing else, so the
// slideshow's thread can use it.
static SDL_Surface *load_surface_timed(const char *path, Uint64 *read, Uint64 *decode)
{
    Uint64 start = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
    size_t size = 0;
    void *data = SDL_LoadFile(path, &size);
    Uint64 loaded = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
    *read += loaded - start;
    if (data == NULL)
        return NULL;
    const char *dot = SDL_strrchr(path, '.');
    SDL_Surface *surface = IMG_LoadTyped_IO(SDL_IOFromConstMem(data, size), true, dot != NULL ? dot + 1 : NULL);
    SDL_free(data);
    *decode += SDL_GetPerformanceCounter() - loaded;
    return surface;
}
```

After `quit_svg()` (`image.c:51-54`), add:

```c
// Three 1 x 1 images, one per format SDL_image decodes for StreamFlex: a mid-gray PNG (RGB), a
// baseline grayscale JPEG with the standard Huffman tables, and a lossless WebP. Made with
// ImageMagick 7.1.2 and decoded back by Pillow before they were pasted here.
static const unsigned char PRIME_PNG[] = {
    0x89, 0x50, 0x4E, 0x47, 0x0D, 0x0A, 0x1A, 0x0A, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x0D, 0x49, 0x48, 0x44, 0x52,

// A function to set SDL_image's PNG, JPEG and WebP loaders up on the main thread, before any thread
// that decodes starts. SDL_image sets each format up on its first load, with nothing to stop two
// threads doing it at once, and the slideshow's loader and the settings preview's decode load beside
// the main thread. Each format decodes its tiny image once; one that cannot says why.
void prime_image_formats(void)
{
    static const struct { const char *name; const char *type; const unsigned char *data; size_t size; } formats[] = {
        { "PNG", "png", PRIME_PNG, sizeof(PRIME_PNG) },
        { "JPEG", "jpg", PRIME_JPEG, sizeof(PRIME_JPEG) },
        { "WebP", "webp", PRIME_WEBP, sizeof(PRIME_WEBP) }
    };
    size_t primed = 0;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(formats) / sizeof(formats[0]); i++) {
        SDL_Surface *surface = IMG_LoadTyped_IO(SDL_IOFromConstMem(formats[i].data, formats[i].size), true, formats[i].type);
        if (surface == NULL)
            log_error("Could not prime SDL_image's %s loader\n%s", formats[i].name, SDL_GetError());
        else {
            SDL_DestroySurface(surface);
            primed++;
        }
    }
    if (primed == sizeof(formats) / sizeof(formats[0]))
        log_debug("Images: primed the PNG, JPEG and WebP loaders");
}
```

```c

    if (surface == NULL) {
        log_error("Could not load image %s\n%s", path, SDL_GetError());
        return NULL;
    }
    // The chroma pass counts as decode: a decode worker would run it too (spec section 8)
    Uint64 decoded = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
    surface = keep_surface_off_chroma_key(surface, path);
    Uint64 kept = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
    timing_decode += kept - decoded;
    SDL_Texture *texture = load_texture(surface);
    timing_texture += SDL_GetPerformanceCounter() - kept;
    return texture;
```

### Task 1.13: Parallel icon decode (the timing gate tripped)

**Why this task exists.** Task 0.5's verdict (ledger, 2026-10-11): threshold 2 tripped. The heavy configs' 24-icon Main took 61-84 ms to open at startup on the Windows guest (6 of 6 runs over 50 ms) and 41-52 ms on the Ubuntu guest (1 of 6), with decode 41-54 ms of it. Spec § 8 "If it trips" applies: `render_buttons` hands its icons' read, decode and chroma pass to a small pool of SDL threads; the main thread joins them, makes every texture and renders the titles, so the grid still appears all at once, only sooner.

**Runs after Task 1.12** (the shipped `Timing:` lines and the format priming), which it builds on: `load_surface_timed()`, the `timing_*` counters and `prime_image_formats()` exist, and the priming is what makes concurrent first loads safe (spec § 8, the SDL_image hazard).

**One design call, stated for the reviewer.** The batch is synchronous: `render_buttons` starts the workers and waits for every job before it makes a texture, all on the main thread. A reload or a menu change also runs on the main thread, so it can never meet a batch in flight, and no result can go stale. The spec's "a reload or a menu change waits for a batch in flight, and a stale result is freed" therefore holds by construction. Its third job-table test (a stale result discarded) has nothing to exercise; it is replaced by the ASan proof below that every surface a batch makes is consumed or freed. **Approved by the user, 2026-10-11.**

**Files:**
- Create: `src/iconload.c`, `src/iconload.h` (the job table and the worker pool; SDL threads, no image code)
- Create: `tests/test_iconload.c` (CTest `iconload`)
- Modify: `src/image.c`, `src/image.h` (a worker-safe decode, `decode_icon_job()`, and the SVG rasterizer split)
- Modify: `src/launcher.c` (`render_buttons()`, `:1230-1269` on master, as Tasks 1.12 and 1.16 left it)
- Modify: `CMakeLists.txt` (the new source; the new test, beside `test_layout`)
- Modify: `tests/headless/run.sh` (a `tsan` mode), `tests/headless/checks/60-timing.sh` (the menu sums under parallel decode)
- Create: `tests/headless/tsan.supp`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes (Task 1.12): `static SDL_Surface *load_surface_timed(const char *path, Uint64 *read, Uint64 *decode)` in `image.c`; `timing_read`, `timing_decode`, `timing_texture`, `timing_ms()`; `prime_image_formats()` called in `main()` before any decoding thread.
- Consumes (Task 1.9): the chroma pass is Linux-only (`#ifndef _WIN32`).
- Produces, in `src/iconload.h`:
  ```c
  typedef struct {
      const char *path;      // In: the icon file, already past the main thread's fileio_not_a_file() guard
      int size;              // In: the button size, which an SVG is rasterized to
      bool svg;              // In: rasterize instead of decode
      SDL_Surface *surface;  // Out: NULL when it failed
      char error[256];       // Out: why it failed, copied on the worker (SDL's error is per thread)
      int moved;             // Out: pixels the chroma pass moved off the key (Linux); logged by the main thread
      Uint64 read, decode;   // Out: performance-counter counts
      int claims;            // Out: how many workers claimed it; exactly 1 (the job table's tests read it)
      bool done;             // Out: posted
  } IconJob;

  typedef void (*IconWork)(IconJob *job, void *worker_state);
  typedef void *(*IconWorkerStart)(void);           // A worker's own state (its SVG rasterizer); may return NULL
  typedef void (*IconWorkerEnd)(void *worker_state);

  int iconload_worker_count(int logical_cores);      // clamp(cores - 1, 1, 4); a core count below 1 gives 1
  int iconload_workers(void);                        // iconload_worker_count(SDL_GetNumLogicalCPUCores()), worked out once
  // Runs every job once on `workers` SDL threads, each claiming the next unclaimed job, and returns only
  // when every job has posted. A thread that cannot start leaves its share to the others, and when none
  // starts the main thread runs every job itself, logged once.
  void iconload_batch(IconJob *jobs, int count, int workers, IconWork work, IconWorkerStart start, IconWorkerEnd end);
  ```
- Produces, in `src/image.h`:
  ```c
  void decode_icon_job(IconJob *job, void *rasterizer);   // The IconWork for icons: read, decode or rasterize, chroma pass
  void *icon_worker_start(void);                          // nsvgCreateRasterizer()
  void icon_worker_end(void *rasterizer);                 // nsvgDeleteRasterizer()
  ```
- Produces these debug lines, byte-exact:
  - `Icons: decoding on %i threads` (once, the first time `iconload_workers()` is called)
  - `Icons: no decode thread started, decoding on the main thread\n%s` (an error, once per batch it happens in)

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing job-table test**

Create `tests/test_iconload.c`:

```c
// The job table behind parallel icon decode (spec section 8): the worker count, every job claimed
// exactly once, the batch returning only when every job has posted, and the main thread running the
// batch when no thread starts. It links SDL for its threads, as the vulkan check does.
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include "../src/iconload.h"

static int failures = 0;
#define CHECK(cond, ...) do { if (!(cond)) { failures++; printf("FAIL  " __VA_ARGS__); printf("\n"); } } while (0)

static SDL_AtomicInt started;   // Worker states made
static SDL_AtomicInt ended;     // Worker states ended

static void *fake_start(void) { SDL_AddAtomicInt(&started, 1); return &started; }
static void fake_end(void *state) { (void) state; SDL_AddAtomicInt(&ended, 1); }

// A job that takes a little time, so the workers overlap and a double claim would show
static void fake_work(IconJob *job, void *state)
{
    CHECK(state == &started, "the worker's own state reaches its jobs");
    job->claims++;
    SDL_Delay((Uint32) (1 + job->size % 3));
    job->done = true;
}

static void test_worker_count(void)
{
    static const int cores[]   = { -1, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 16 };
    static const int workers[] = {  1, 1, 1, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4,  4 };
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(cores) / sizeof(cores[0]); i++)
        CHECK(iconload_worker_count(cores[i]) == workers[i], "worker count for %i cores: %i, wanted %i",
              cores[i], iconload_worker_count(cores[i]), workers[i]);
}

static void test_batch(int count, int threads)
{
    IconJob jobs[200] = { 0 };
    for (int i = 0; i < count; i++)
        jobs[i].size = i;
    SDL_SetAtomicInt(&started, 0);
    SDL_SetAtomicInt(&ended, 0);
    iconload_batch(jobs, count, threads, fake_work, fake_start, fake_end);
    int once = 0, posted = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < count; i++) {
        once += jobs[i].claims == 1;
        posted += jobs[i].done;
    }
    CHECK(once == count, "%i jobs on %i threads: %i claimed exactly once", count, threads, once);
    CHECK(posted == count, "%i jobs on %i threads: %i posted when the batch returned", count, threads, posted);
    CHECK(SDL_GetAtomicInt(&started) == SDL_GetAtomicInt(&ended), "every worker state made is ended");
}

int main(void)
{
    if (!SDL_Init(0)) {
        printf("FAIL  SDL_Init: %s\n", SDL_GetError());
        return 1;
    }
    test_worker_count();
    test_batch(200, 4);
    test_batch(3, 4);     // Fewer jobs than threads
    test_batch(1, 1);
    test_batch(0, 2);     // An empty batch returns at once
    // No thread starts: the main thread runs the whole batch
    SDL_SetHint("STREAMFLEX_TEST_ICONLOAD_NO_THREADS", "1");
    test_batch(50, 4);
    SDL_ResetHint("STREAMFLEX_TEST_ICONLOAD_NO_THREADS");
    SDL_Quit();
    printf("%s: %i failed\n", failures ? "FAILED" : "PASSED", failures);
    return failures != 0;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: The job table and the pool**

Create `src/iconload.h` with the Interfaces block above (include guard `ICONLOAD_H`, `#include <stdbool.h>` and `<SDL3/SDL.h>`).

Create `src/iconload.c`:

```c
// Parallel icon decode (spec section 8): a batch of jobs run on a few SDL threads, each claiming the
// next unclaimed job, and the batch returns only when every job has posted. The jobs touch nothing
// but themselves; the caller makes the textures afterward, on the main thread.
#include <stdlib.h>
#include "iconload.h"
#include "debug.h"

#define ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS 4

typedef struct {
    IconJob *jobs;
    int count;
    SDL_AtomicInt next;   // The next unclaimed job
    IconWork work;
    IconWorkerStart start;
    IconWorkerEnd end;
} Batch;

// A function to work out how many decode threads a machine with this many logical cores gets: one
// fewer than its cores, so the main thread keeps one, and at least 1 and at most 4
int iconload_worker_count(int logical_cores)
{
    int workers = logical_cores - 1;
    if (workers < 1)
        workers = 1;
    if (workers > ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS)
        workers = ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS;
    return workers;
}

// A function to say how many decode threads this machine gets, worked out and logged once
int iconload_workers(void)
{
    static int workers = 0;
    if (workers == 0) {
        workers = iconload_worker_count(SDL_GetNumLogicalCPUCores());
        log_debug("Icons: decoding on %i threads", workers);
    }
    return workers;
}

// A function to run jobs until none is left unclaimed, with one worker state for the run
static void run_jobs(Batch *batch)
{
    void *state = batch->start != NULL ? batch->start() : NULL;
    for (;;) {
        int i = SDL_AddAtomicInt(&batch->next, 1);   // Returns the value before the add
        if (i >= batch->count)
            break;
        batch->work(&batch->jobs[i], state);
        batch->jobs[i].done = true;
    }
    if (batch->end != NULL)
        batch->end(state);
}

static int worker_main(void *data)
{
    run_jobs((Batch *) data);
    return 0;
}

void iconload_batch(IconJob *jobs, int count, int workers, IconWork work, IconWorkerStart start, IconWorkerEnd end)
{
    if (count <= 0)
        return;
    Batch batch = { .jobs = jobs, .count = count, .work = work, .start = start, .end = end };
    SDL_SetAtomicInt(&batch.next, 0);
    if (workers > ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS)
        workers = ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS;
    if (workers > count)
        workers = count;
    SDL_Thread *threads[ICONLOAD_MAX_WORKERS] = { NULL };
    int running = 0;
    bool no_threads = false;
#ifdef STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS
    // Only test builds have this: no decode thread starts, as when the system refuses one
    no_threads = SDL_GetHintBoolean("STREAMFLEX_TEST_ICONLOAD_NO_THREADS", false);
#endif
    for (int i = 0; i < workers && !no_threads; i++) {
        threads[i] = SDL_CreateThread(worker_main, "Icon decode", &batch);
        if (threads[i] != NULL)
            running++;
    }
    if (running == 0) {
        log_error("Icons: no decode thread started, decoding on the main thread\n%s",
                  no_threads ? "Test hook: no thread starts" : SDL_GetError());
        run_jobs(&batch);
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < workers; i++) {
        if (threads[i] != NULL)
            SDL_WaitThread(threads[i], NULL);
    }
}
```

In CMake, define `STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS` for the `test_iconload` target only (`target_compile_definitions(test_iconload PRIVATE STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS)`), so the hint reaches it.
