- [ ] **Step 6: The worker-safe decode (`image.c`, `image.h`)**

Split `rasterize()`'s parse and rasterize into a function a worker can call with its own rasterizer. Directly above `rasterize()`, add:

```c
// A function to parse and rasterize an SVG text buffer with the rasterizer given (each decode thread
// has its own: nanosvg's keeps scratch buffers inside it), into a new RGBA buffer the caller frees;
// w or h -1 keeps the aspect ratio. NULL when it cannot, with why in `error`.
static unsigned char *rasterize_pixels(NSVGrasterizer *with, char *buffer, int w, int h,
                                       int *width_out, int *height_out, char *error, size_t error_size)
```

Its body is `rasterize()`'s, moved: from `image = nsvgParse(buffer, "px", 96.0f);` through `nsvgRasterize(...)`, with `rasterizer` replaced by `with`, the two `log_error(...)` calls replaced by `SDL_snprintf(error, error_size, "could not open SVG image.")` and `SDL_snprintf(error, error_size, "Could not alloc SVG pixel buffer.")`, `nsvgDelete(image)` called before it returns (on the success path and after a failed malloc), and `*width_out` / `*height_out` set. `rasterize()` then calls `rasterize_pixels(rasterizer, ...)`, logs `error` with `log_error("%s", error)` when it returns NULL, and keeps the rest of its body (the chroma pass, the timing counters of Task 1.12, the surface, the texture, `rect`, `free(pixel_buffer)`) unchanged. The highlight, the scroll arrows and `rasterize_svg()` keep the global rasterizer, on the main thread.

Then add, after `load_icon()`:

```c
// The decode thread's own SVG rasterizer, made when it starts and deleted when it ends
void *icon_worker_start(void)
{
    return nsvgCreateRasterizer();
}

void icon_worker_end(void *with)
{
    if (with != NULL)
        nsvgDeleteRasterizer((NSVGrasterizer *) with);
}

// A function to do a menu icon's work off the main thread (iconload's IconWork): read and decode it,
// or rasterize an SVG at the button size, then move its pixels off the chroma key on Linux. It logs
// nothing and makes no texture; the main thread does both from what it posts. The fileio guard ran
// on the main thread before the job was made.
void decode_icon_job(IconJob *job, void *with)
{
    if (job->svg) {
        Uint64 start = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
        size_t size = 0;
        char *buffer = SDL_LoadFile(job->path, &size);
        Uint64 loaded = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
        job->read += loaded - start;
        if (buffer == NULL) {
            SDL_snprintf(job->error, sizeof(job->error), "%s", SDL_GetError());
            return;
        }
        int width = 0, height = 0;
        unsigned char *pixels = with != NULL
            ? rasterize_pixels((NSVGrasterizer *) with, buffer, job->size, -1, &width, &height, job->error, sizeof(job->error))
            : NULL;
        if (with == NULL)
            SDL_snprintf(job->error, sizeof(job->error), "Could not initialize SVG rasterizer.");
        SDL_free(buffer);
        if (pixels == NULL) {
            job->decode += SDL_GetPerformanceCounter() - loaded;
            return;
        }
#ifndef _WIN32
        job->moved = chroma_keep_off(pixels, width, height, 4 * width,
                         config.chroma_key_color.r, config.chroma_key_color.g, config.chroma_key_color.b);
#endif
        // nanosvg writes R, G, B, A bytes in memory order: SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32. The surface owns
        // its own copy, so the buffer is freed here.
        job->surface = SDL_CreateSurface(width, height, SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32);
        if (job->surface != NULL) {
            for (int y = 0; y < height; y++)
                SDL_memcpy((Uint8 *) job->surface->pixels + y * job->surface->pitch, pixels + y * 4 * width, (size_t) (4 * width));
        }
        else
            SDL_snprintf(job->error, sizeof(job->error), "%s", SDL_GetError());
        free(pixels);
        job->decode += SDL_GetPerformanceCounter() - loaded;
        return;
    }
    job->surface = load_surface_timed(job->path, &job->read, &job->decode);
    if (job->surface == NULL) {
        SDL_snprintf(job->error, sizeof(job->error), "%s", SDL_GetError());
        return;
    }
#ifndef _WIN32
    Uint64 decoded = SDL_GetPerformanceCounter();
    if (job->surface->format != SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32) {
        SDL_Surface *converted = SDL_ConvertSurface(job->surface, SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32);
        if (converted != NULL) {
            SDL_DestroySurface(job->surface);
            job->surface = converted;
        }
    }
    if (job->surface->format == SDL_PIXELFORMAT_RGBA32 && SDL_LockSurface(job->surface)) {
        job->moved = chroma_keep_off(job->surface->pixels, job->surface->w, job->surface->h, job->surface->pitch,
                         config.chroma_key_color.r, config.chroma_key_color.g, config.chroma_key_color.b);
        SDL_UnlockSurface(job->surface);
    }
    job->decode += SDL_GetPerformanceCounter() - decoded;   // The chroma pass counts as decode (spec section 8)
#endif
}
```

`config.chroma_key_color` is read while the main thread waits in `iconload_batch()`, and nothing writes config then, so the reads cannot race. In `image.h`, add `#include "iconload.h"` and the three declarations of the Interfaces block. If Task 1.2 named the converted-surface call or the surface's format field differently (SDL3's `SDL_ConvertSurface(surface, format)` and `surface->format` are the 3.2.10 names), use Task 1.2's form; the logic stays.

In `render_buttons()` (as Tasks 1.12 and 1.16 left it), replace the two lines that load the icons:

```c

```

and the loop around them, with two passes. Before the loop, after Task 1.12's counter reset and the title font set-up:

```c
    // The icons are read and decoded on the decode threads first, two jobs an entry (the icon and its
    // selected icon); the textures are made below, on this thread, in the entries' order
    int job_count = (int) menu->num_entries * 2;
    IconJob *jobs = calloc((size_t) (job_count > 0 ? job_count : 1), sizeof(IconJob));
    if (jobs == NULL)
        log_fatal("Could not allocate the icon jobs for menu '%s'", menu->name);
    for (unsigned int i = 0; i < menu->num_entries; i++) {
        const char *paths[2] = { menu->items[i]->icon_path, menu->items[i]->icon_selected_path };
        for (int k = 0; k < 2; k++) {
            IconJob *job = &jobs[i * 2 + k];
            job->path = paths[k];
            job->size = size;
            if (paths[k] == NULL)
                job->done = true;   // No icon: nothing to do
            else if (fileio_not_a_file(paths[k])) {
                SDL_snprintf(job->error, sizeof(job->error), "%s", fileio_last_error());
                job->done = true;
            }
            else {
                size_t length = strlen(paths[k]);
                job->svg = length > 4 && SDL_strcasecmp(paths[k] + length - 4, ".svg") == 0;
            }
        }
    }
    // The jobs with work left go to the pool as one packed array (iconload_batch() takes a
    // contiguous one), each remembering where it came from, and come back to their entries after
    IconJob *work = calloc((size_t) (job_count > 0 ? job_count : 1), sizeof(IconJob));
    int *origin = calloc((size_t) (job_count > 0 ? job_count : 1), sizeof(int));
    if (work == NULL || origin == NULL)
        log_fatal("Could not allocate the icon jobs for menu '%s'", menu->name);
    int work_count = 0;
    for (int j = 0; j < job_count; j++) {
        if (!jobs[j].done) {
            work[work_count] = jobs[j];
            origin[work_count++] = j;
        }
    }
    iconload_batch(work, work_count, iconload_workers(), decode_icon_job, icon_worker_start, icon_worker_end);
    for (int j = 0; j < work_count; j++)
        jobs[origin[j]] = work[j];
    free(work);
    free(origin);
```

- [ ] **Step 8: The Timing line's sums under parallel decode (`60-timing.sh`)**

With decode on N threads, a menu line's read and decode are the work done, summed over the threads, and can exceed its wall-clock total; only the texture is still a share of it. In `60-timing.sh`, replace `t60_menu`'s `&& t60_adds_up "$lines"` with `&& t60_menu_adds_up "$lines" "$log"`, and add above `t60_menu`:

```bash
# A function to tell whether a menu's Timing: line adds up under parallel decode: its texture is at
# most its total plus 2 ms, and its read and decode, summed over the decode threads the log names, at
# most the total times that many threads plus 2 ms
t60_menu_adds_up() {
    local line=$1 log=$2 total read decode texture threads
    total=$(sed -nE 's/.* in ([0-9]+) ms \(.*/\1/p' <<< "$line")
    read=$(sed -nE 's/.*\(read ([0-9]+),.*/\1/p' <<< "$line")
    decode=$(sed -nE 's/.*, decode ([0-9]+),.*/\1/p' <<< "$line")
    texture=$(sed -nE 's/.*, texture ([0-9]+)\).*/\1/p' <<< "$line")
    threads=$(sed -nE 's/^Icons: decoding on ([0-9]+) threads$/\1/p' "$log" | head -1)
    [ -n "$total" ] && [ -n "$threads" ] && [ "$texture" -le $((total + 2)) ] \
        && [ $((read + decode)) -le $((total * threads + 2)) ]
}
```

and one result after the menu result:

```bash
ok=1
[ "$(grep -cE '^Icons: decoding on [1-4] threads$' "$log")" = 1 ] && ok=0
result "timing: the icons decode on 1 to 4 threads, logged once" $ok
```

The background and slideshow lines keep `t60_adds_up`: they decode on one thread.

# Phase 1 drafts: Tasks 1.14a to 1.16b, fallback fonts (spec § 4)

These five tasks assume Tasks 1.1 to 1.13 have landed: SDL3 3.2.10+, SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 with HarfBuzz on every build, `Fontconfig::Fontconfig` linked into the executable on Linux, the harness images carrying `fonts-noto-cjk` / `fonts-noto-color-emoji` (Debian) and `google-noto-sans-cjk-vf-fonts` / `google-noto-color-emoji-fonts` (Fedora), and `make_fonts.py box DST FAMILY CHARS` (Task 1.1), which writes a TrueType font whose glyph for each character of CHARS is a filled square. Line numbers are master `ade1a12`; where Task 1.2's port changed a line, the step says "as Task 1.2 left it" and shows the SDL3 form.

**What SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 does, read in its source** (`src/SDL_ttf.c` at tag `release-3.2.2`, read-only):
- `TTF_AddFallbackFont` (`:2274-2311`) and `TTF_RemoveFallbackFont` (`:2313-2346`) only append to or unlink from the font's list and call `UpdateFontText`, which marks `TTF_Text` objects (`:2239-2255`). Neither calls `Flush_Cache` (`:2457-2478`), so the font's eight cached glyph-position runs (`GetCachedGlyphPositions`, `:3625-3672`) keep `pos->glyph` pointers into the fallback's glyph table.
- `TTF_CloseFont` (`:6098-6135`) flushes the font's own cache first, then clears its fallbacks, then unlinks it from every font it is a fallback for, then destroys its glyph table, whose values are freed (`SDL_DestroyHashValue`, `:2058`). So closing a fallback while its font lives, then measuring the same text on the font, reads `glyph->sz_left` from freed memory in `TTF_Size_Internal` (`:3727-3741`): upstream #549 (reported at SDL 3.2.10 / SDL3_ttf 3.2.2, closed 2026-01-23).
- `TTF_FontHasGlyph` (`:3153-3158`) calls `get_char_index_fallback` (`:3112-3135`), which walks the fallbacks: once one is attached it cannot say what the font itself lacks.
- Fallback spans are shaped by the fallback's own font (`CollectGlyphsWithFallbacks`, `:3532-3575`), and every glyph sits on its own font's ascent (`:3604`, upstream #629, Task 1.1's overlay on Windows).
- `TTF_SetFontSizeDPI` (`:5537-5602`) returns early at the same size and otherwise flushes only the font it is given.
- `TTF_RenderGlyph_Blended` (`:3971-3978`) renders the code point as a one-character text; `TTF_FontIsScalable` is `FT_IS_SCALABLE` (`:5923-5928`).

**Measured in the bundled fonts** (their `cmap` and `hmtx`, read with a script; advances in em): Open Sans lacks ★ U+2605, → U+2192, ZWJ U+200D, U+202F and every CJK and emoji code point, and has › U+203A, × U+00D7 and … U+2026; its `.notdef` is a hollow rectangle (two contours, an outline 104/2048 em thick, 1.8 px at 36 pt) with an advance of 0.600. DejaVu Sans has ★ (0.896), → (0.838), 😀 U+1F600 (1.042), ZWJ (no ink), U+202F (0.200, no ink), and no CJK. FreeSans has → (0.987) and no ★. No bundled font is variable or color.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the local sanitized unit runner**

Create `C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-port/unit-asan.sh` with the Write tool, LF line endings:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
# The CI job "Unit tests (ASan, UBSan)" (.github/workflows/build.yml, unit_sanitizers), run locally in
# the Debian headless image: a copy of /src built with ASan, UBSan and the extra warnings, as tester,
# then ctest. Its one argument, when given, is ctest's -R filter. MUTANT_FILE and MUTANT_SED, when
# set, apply one sed program to that file in the copy only, so the repo is never touched.
set -u
rm -rf /work; mkdir -p /work
tar -C /src --exclude=./build --exclude=./.git --exclude=./headless-out -cf - . | tar -C /work -xf -
if [ -n "${MUTANT_FILE:-}" ]; then
    sed -i -e "$MUTANT_SED" "/work/$MUTANT_FILE"
    cmp -s "/src/$MUTANT_FILE" "/work/$MUTANT_FILE" && { echo "MUTANT NOT APPLIED to $MUTANT_FILE"; exit 2; }
    echo "MUTANT applied to $MUTANT_FILE:"; diff "/src/$MUTANT_FILE" "/work/$MUTANT_FILE"
fi
chown -R tester /work
flags="-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-omit-frame-pointer"
su tester -c "cmake -S /work -B /work/build -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug -DCMAKE_C_FLAGS='$flags' -DCMAKE_EXE_LINKER_FLAGS='-fsanitize=address,undefined' -DEXTRA_WARNINGS=ON" > /tmp/configure.log 2>&1 \
    || { echo "CONFIGURE FAILED"; tail -30 /tmp/configure.log; exit 2; }
su tester -c "cmake --build /work/build -j$(nproc)" > /tmp/build.log 2>&1 \
    || { echo "BUILD FAILED"; tail -40 /tmp/build.log; exit 2; }
ours=$(grep -iE 'warning:' /tmp/build.log | grep -vE '^g?make(\[[0-9]+\])?: ' | grep -v '^/work/src/external/')
[ -z "$ours" ] || { echo "WARNINGS OUTSIDE src/external"; echo "$ours"; exit 1; }
filter=
[ -z "${1:-}" ] || filter="-R '$1'"
su tester -c "UBSAN_OPTIONS=print_stacktrace=1:halt_on_error=1 setarch $(uname -m) -R ctest --test-dir /work/build --output-on-failure $filter"
```

Every unit run in Tasks 1.14a to 1.15 is this command, with the filter named in the step (`$t` for short below):

```powershell
docker build -t streamflex-test C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex/tests/headless
docker run --rm --security-opt seccomp=unconfined -v "C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex:/src:ro" -v "C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-port/unit-asan.sh:/unit-asan.sh:ro" streamflex-test bash /unit-asan.sh fontrules
```

A mutant run adds `-e MUTANT_FILE=<path> -e 'MUTANT_SED=<program>'` before the image name; the single quotes keep PowerShell from reading `$` or `\`. In a `MUTANT_SED` pattern `&` is literal and `[`, `]` are escaped; in its replacement `&` is written `\&`. A mutant run that ends `MUTANT NOT APPLIED`, `CONFIGURE FAILED`, `BUILD FAILED` or `WARNINGS OUTSIDE src/external` killed nothing: fix the mutant and run it again. Only the named check failing counts. The same holds for every headless mutant in Tasks 1.16a and 1.16b: a shard whose console says `BUILD FAILED` is not a kill, and the named `FAIL` must be the only new one.

```c
#include <stdint.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "fontrules.h"

// Unit test 7's pure half (spec section 9): the code points a font set never looks up, the spaces whose
// fallback is not asked for ink, where a provider goes in a chain, and the face as the log writes it

// A function to test the code points never looked up: Unicode's default-ignorables and the controls
static void test_skip(void)
{
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0x200D));    // ZWJ
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0xFE0F));    // VS16, emoji presentation
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0xE0100));   // VS17, in the variation selectors supplement
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0x200C));    // ZWNJ
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0x00AD));    // The soft hyphen
    CHECK(fontrules_skip(0x000A));    // A wrapped note's line break
    CHECK(!fontrules_skip(0x0041));   // A
    CHECK(!fontrules_skip(0x1F600));  // An emoji
    CHECK(!fontrules_skip(0x4E2D));   // A Han character
    CHECK(!fontrules_skip(0x0020));   // A space is drawn, as nothing
}

// A function to test the spaces a fallback draws as nothing on purpose
static void test_blank(void)
{
    CHECK(fontrules_blank(0x0020));
    CHECK(fontrules_blank(0x00A0));   // The no-break space
    CHECK(fontrules_blank(0x202F));   // The narrow no-break space
    CHECK(fontrules_blank(0x3000));   // The ideographic space, which a CJK font draws as nothing
    CHECK(!fontrules_blank(0x0041));
    CHECK(!fontrules_blank(0x2605));
}

// A function to test where a provider goes among a set's fallbacks, kept in candidate order
static void test_insert_order(void)
{
    int ranks[] = { 2, 5 };
    CHECK_INT(fontrules_insert_at(ranks, 2, 7), 2);   // After both: appended
    CHECK_INT(fontrules_insert_at(ranks, 2, 3), 1);   // Between them: the chain is rebuilt
    CHECK_INT(fontrules_insert_at(ranks, 2, 0), 0);   // Before both
    CHECK_INT(fontrules_insert_at(NULL, 0, 4), 0);    // The first: appended
    // The spec's case: a star attached Segoe UI Symbol (rank 2), and an emoji's provider, Segoe UI
    // Emoji (rank 1), goes in front of it, or the emoji would draw from Symbol in monochrome
    int symbol[] = { 2 };
    CHECK_INT(fontrules_insert_at(symbol, 1, 1), 0);
}

// A function to test the face as the log writes it: fontconfig's index is (instance << 16) | face
static void test_face_text(void)
{
    char text[48];
    fontrules_face_text(0, text, sizeof(text));
    CHECK_STR(text, "face 0");
    fontrules_face_text(2, text, sizeof(text));
    CHECK_STR(text, "face 2");
    fontrules_face_text(4 << 16, text, sizeof(text));
    CHECK_STR(text, "face 0, named instance 4");
    fontrules_face_text((4 << 16) | 1, text, sizeof(text));
    CHECK_STR(text, "face 1, named instance 4");
}

int main(void)
{
    test_skip();
    test_blank();
    test_insert_order();
    test_face_text();
    return check_report();
}
```

Create `src/fontrules.h`:

```c
// Pure rules for fallback fonts (spec section 4), with no SDL so tests/test_fontrules.c links them
// alone: the code points a font set never looks up, the spaces a fallback draws as nothing on
// purpose, where a provider goes in a set's chain, and the face as the log writes it
#ifndef FONTRULES_H
#define FONTRULES_H

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

bool fontrules_skip(uint32_t code_point);
bool fontrules_blank(uint32_t code_point);
int fontrules_insert_at(const int *ranks, int count, int rank);
void fontrules_face_text(int index, char *out, size_t size);

#endif
```

Create `src/fontrules.c`:

```c
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include "fontrules.h"

// Unicode's Default_Ignorable_Code_Point ranges (DerivedCoreProperties.txt): no font is expected to
// draw them, ZWJ, ZWNJ, the variation selectors and the soft hyphen among them
static const uint32_t IGNORABLE[][2] = {
    { 0x00AD, 0x00AD }, { 0x034F, 0x034F }, { 0x061C, 0x061C }, { 0x115F, 0x1160 }, { 0x17B4, 0x17B5 },
    { 0x180B, 0x180F }, { 0x200B, 0x200F }, { 0x202A, 0x202E }, { 0x2060, 0x206F }, { 0x3164, 0x3164 },
    { 0xFE00, 0xFE0F }, { 0xFEFF, 0xFEFF }, { 0xFFA0, 0xFFA0 }, { 0xFFF0, 0xFFF8 }, { 0x1BCA0, 0x1BCA3 },
    { 0x1D173, 0x1D17A }, { 0xE0000, 0xE0FFF }
};

// The spaces (Unicode's Zs, less the Ogham mark, which has ink, and the two separators), which a font
// draws as nothing by design
static const uint32_t BLANK[][2] = {
    { 0x0020, 0x0020 }, { 0x00A0, 0x00A0 }, { 0x2000, 0x200A }, { 0x2028, 0x2029 }, { 0x202F, 0x202F },
    { 0x205F, 0x205F }, { 0x3000, 0x3000 }
};

// A function to tell whether a code point lies in one of a table's ranges
static bool in_ranges(const uint32_t (*ranges)[2], size_t count, uint32_t code_point)
{
    for (size_t i = 0; i < count; i++) {
        if (code_point >= ranges[i][0] && code_point <= ranges[i][1])
            return true;
    }
    return false;
}

// A function to tell whether a font set never looks a code point up: a default-ignorable, or a
// control character (a wrapped note's line breaks, which SDL_ttf breaks on and never draws)
bool fontrules_skip(uint32_t code_point)
{
    if (code_point < 0x20 || (code_point >= 0x7F && code_point < 0xA0))
        return true;
    return in_ranges(IGNORABLE, sizeof(IGNORABLE) / sizeof(IGNORABLE[0]), code_point);
}

// A function to tell whether a code point is a space: a fallback that has it is not asked for ink,
// or a CJK font would be skipped for the session over its ideographic space
bool fontrules_blank(uint32_t code_point)
{
    return in_ranges(BLANK, sizeof(BLANK) / sizeof(BLANK[0]), code_point);
}

// A function to find where a provider of candidate rank `rank` goes among a set's fallbacks, whose
// ranks rise: `count` when it ranks after every one (it is appended), else the index it takes (the
// chain is rebuilt)
int fontrules_insert_at(const int *ranks, int count, int rank)
{
    int at = count;
    while (at > 0 && ranks[at - 1] > rank)
        at--;
    return at;
}

// A function to write a face as the log names it. fontconfig's index for a variable font's named
// instance is (instance << 16) | face, which SDL3_ttf hands to FreeType unchanged.
void fontrules_face_text(int index, char *out, size_t size)
{
    int instance = index >> 16;
    if (instance > 0)
        snprintf(out, size, "face %i, named instance %i", index & 0xFFFF, instance);
    else
        snprintf(out, size, "face %i", index & 0xFFFF);
}
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `tests/test_fontset.c`:

```c
// Unit test 7, its real half (spec section 9): font sets over SDL_ttf and the bundled fonts. This file
// stands in for fontcand.c (fontset_candidates() below), so no system font is read: the candidates are
// the bundled DejaVu Sans, and FreeSans where a chain's order must show. It stands in for the launcher's
// log too, and reads what fontset.c wrote there.
#include <stdarg.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include <SDL3_ttf/SDL_ttf.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "launcher.h"
#include "debug.h"
#include "fontset.h"

#define OPEN_SANS FONTSET_TEST_FONTS "/OpenSans-Regular.ttf"
#define DEJAVU FONTSET_TEST_FONTS "/DejaVuSans.ttf"
#define FREESANS FONTSET_TEST_FONTS "/FreeSans.ttf"
#define STAR "\xE2\x98\x85"     // U+2605: DejaVu Sans has it; Open Sans and FreeSans do not
#define ARROW "\xE2\x86\x92"    // U+2192: DejaVu Sans (0.838 em) and FreeSans (0.987 em) have it; Open Sans does not
#define ZHONG "\xE4\xB8\xAD"    // U+4E2D: none of the three has it
#define NNBSP "\xE2\x80\xAF"    // U+202F, the narrow no-break space: DejaVu Sans has it, with no ink
#define LINES 64

static char logged[LINES][512];
static int logged_count = 0;
static FontCandidate list[2];
static int list_count = 0;
static int lists_built = 0;    // Calls to fontset_candidates()
static int covers_calls = 0;   // Calls to never_covers()

// The launcher's log, kept for the checks to read
void output_log(LogLevel log_level, const char *format, ...)
{
    (void) log_level;
    if (logged_count == LINES)
        return;
    va_list args;
    va_start(args, format);
    vsnprintf(logged[logged_count], sizeof(logged[0]), format, args);
    va_end(args);
    logged[logged_count][strcspn(logged[logged_count], "\r\n")] = '\0';
    logged_count++;
}

// A function to count the lines logged that hold a text
static int lines_with(const char *text)
{
    int n = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < logged_count; i++)
        n += strstr(logged[i], text) != NULL;
    return n;
}

// The candidates, in place of fontcand.c's
const FontCandidate *fontset_candidates(int *count)
{
    lists_built++;
    *count = list_count;
    return list_count > 0 ? list : NULL;
}

// Nothing to let go of: the list is this file's
void fontset_candidates_free(void)
{
}

// A coverage that answers no to everything, counting the questions
static bool never_covers(const FontCandidate *candidate, Uint32 ch)
{
    (void) candidate;
    (void) ch;
    covers_calls++;
    return false;
}

// A function to begin a case: these candidates (NULL for no second), nothing logged or counted yet
static void begin(const char *first, const char *second)
{
    list[0] = (FontCandidate) { .path = (char *) first, .face = 0 };
    list[1] = (FontCandidate) { .path = (char *) second, .face = 0 };
    list_count = second != NULL ? 2 : 1;
    logged_count = 0;
    lists_built = 0;
    covers_calls = 0;
}

// A function to measure a text in a font
static int width(TTF_Font *font, const char *text)
{
    int w = 0;
    int h = 0;
    CHECK(TTF_GetStringSize(font, text, 0, &w, &h));
    return w;
}

// A function to measure a text in a file's own font at a size, with no fallbacks
static int plain_width(const char *path, float size, const char *text)
{
    TTF_Font *font = TTF_OpenFont(path, size);
    CHECK(font != NULL);
    int w = font != NULL ? width(font, text) : -1;
    TTF_CloseFont(font);
    return w;
}

// A function to test that a text the font covers builds no candidate list, opens nothing, logs nothing
static void test_covered_text(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    CHECK(set != NULL);
    fontset_prepare(set, "Kodi Media Center \xE2\x80\xBA 12 \xC3\x97 10");   // Open Sans has the › and the ×
    CHECK_INT(lists_built, 0);
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 0);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 0);   // No list, so nothing to let go of
}

// A function to test that a star attaches DejaVu Sans once, measures wider than Open Sans's own box
// for it, and that a second character DejaVu Sans has attaches nothing more
static void test_star(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    int boxed = plain_width(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, "A" STAR);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "A" STAR);
    CHECK(width(fontset_font(set), "A" STAR) > boxed);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to " DEJAVU " (face 0), first for U+2605"), 1);
    fontset_prepare(set, STAR STAR ARROW);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with(" fall back to "), 1);
    CHECK_INT(lists_built, 1);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
    // DejaVu Sans's probe, opened to learn its coverage, and Open Sans's own, which said it lacks the arrow
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: let go of 1 fallback candidates and 2 probe fonts at quit"), 1);
}

// A function to test that order is kept. FreeSans ranks first and DejaVu Sans second. A star (FreeSans
// lacks it) attaches DejaVu Sans; an arrow then attaches FreeSans in front of it, rebuilding the chain,
// so the arrow is FreeSans's and DejaVu Sans still draws stars. Only the primary's own probe can say
// Open Sans lacks the arrow: Open Sans would answer through DejaVu Sans, which has it.
static void test_chain_order(void)
{
    begin(FREESANS, DEJAVU);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, STAR);
    fontset_prepare(set, ARROW);
    int free_arrow = plain_width(FREESANS, 36.0f, ARROW);
    CHECK(free_arrow != plain_width(DEJAVU, 36.0f, ARROW));
    CHECK_INT(width(fontset_font(set), ARROW), free_arrow);
    CHECK_INT(width(fontset_font(set), STAR STAR), plain_width(DEJAVU, 36.0f, STAR STAR));
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to " DEJAVU " (face 0), first for U+2605"), 1);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to " FREESANS " (face 0), first for U+2192"), 1);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

// A function to test that a resized set resizes its fallbacks: a text measured after the resize
// matches the same text in a set opened at that size
static void test_resize(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "A" STAR);
    CHECK(fontset_resize(set, 18.0f));
    FontSet *fresh = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 18.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(fresh, "A" STAR STAR);
    CHECK_INT(width(fontset_font(set), "A" STAR STAR), width(fontset_font(fresh), "A" STAR STAR));
    fontset_close(fresh);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

// A function to test that a set looks at each code point once: the same character again asks no candidate
static void test_seen_once(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, DEJAVU);
    list[0].covers = never_covers;
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, ZHONG);
    CHECK_INT(covers_calls, 1);
    fontset_prepare(set, ZHONG ZHONG);
    CHECK_INT(covers_calls, 1);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

// A function to test that a character no font has is logged once a session, not once a set
static void test_missing_once(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *first = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    FontSet *second = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 24.0f, 0, "settings rows");
    fontset_prepare(first, "A" ZHONG);
    fontset_prepare(second, ZHONG);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: no installed font draws U+4E2D; it shows as a box"), 1);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with(" fall back to "), 0);
    fontset_close(second);
    fontset_close(first);
    fontset_quit();
}

// A function to test that default-ignorables are never looked up. DejaVu Sans maps ZWJ to a glyph with
// no ink, which would have it skipped for the session, and the star after it would find no font.
static void test_ignorable(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "A\xE2\x80\x8D\xEF\xB8\x8F\xF3\xA0\x84\x80");   // ZWJ, VS16, VS17
    fontset_prepare(set, STAR);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: skipped "), 0);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: no installed font draws "), 0);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to " DEJAVU " (face 0), first for U+2605"), 1);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

// A function to test that a space the font lacks attaches the font that has it, though it draws no
// ink: DejaVu Sans's narrow no-break space does not get it taken for a font that draws nothing
static void test_blank_space(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "12" NNBSP "h");
    fontset_prepare(set, STAR);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: skipped "), 0);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to " DEJAVU " (face 0), first for U+202F"), 1);
    CHECK_INT(lines_with("Fonts: no installed font draws "), 0);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

static TTF_Font *primary = NULL;   // The set's font, which record_close() measures again while it lives
static bool primary_open = false;
static int closes = 0;
static bool primary_first = false; // The first font the set closed was its own

// The close fontset.c is pointed at: it records the order, and after each close it measures the
// primary again while that lives. A fallback closed before its font leaves the font's cached
// positions for the text pointing into the fallback's freed glyphs, and that measure reads them
// (upstream #549): ASan reports it in the "Unit tests (ASan, UBSan)" job, and the order check fails
// it everywhere.
static void SDLCALL record_close(TTF_Font *font)
{
    if (closes++ == 0)
        primary_first = font == primary;
    if (font == primary)
        primary_open = false;
    TTF_CloseFont(font);
    if (primary_open) {
        int w = 0;
        int h = 0;
        TTF_GetStringSize(primary, "A" STAR, 0, &w, &h);
    }
}

// A function to test the closing order of upstream #549: the font first, then its fallbacks; opened
// again, the same text measures the same
static void test_close_order(void)
{
    begin(DEJAVU, NULL);
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "A" STAR);
    int first = width(fontset_font(set), "A" STAR);   // The font now caches the text's positions, in DejaVu Sans
    primary = fontset_font(set);
    primary_open = true;
    closes = 0;
    primary_first = false;
    fontset_close_font = record_close;
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_close_font = NULL;
    CHECK_INT(closes, 2);
    CHECK(primary_first);
    set = fontset_open(OPEN_SANS, 36.0f, 0, "titles");
    fontset_prepare(set, "A" STAR);
    CHECK_INT(width(fontset_font(set), "A" STAR), first);
    fontset_close(set);
    fontset_quit();
}

int main(void)
{
    if (!TTF_Init()) {
        fprintf(stderr, "TTF_Init failed: %s\n", SDL_GetError());
        return 1;
    }
    test_covered_text();
    test_star();
    test_chain_order();
    test_resize();
    test_seen_once();
    test_missing_once();
    test_ignorable();
    test_blank_space();
    test_close_order();
    TTF_Quit();
    SDL_Quit();
    return check_report();
}
```

Append to `tests/CMakeLists.txt`, after the `fontrules` test:

```cmake
# Unit test 7's real half: font sets over SDL_ttf and the bundled fonts. The test stands in for
# fontcand.c's candidates and for the launcher's log, so no system font is read
add_executable(test_fontset test_fontset.c "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src/fontset.c" "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src/fontrules.c"
  "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src/fileio.c" "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src/alloc.c")
target_include_directories(test_fontset PRIVATE "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src")
target_compile_definitions(test_fontset PRIVATE FONTSET_TEST_FONTS="${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/assets/fonts")
target_link_libraries(test_fontset SDL3_ttf::SDL3_ttf SDL3::SDL3)
link_fileio(test_fontset)
```

The two targets are the ones Task 1.1 links the executable with; if Task 1.1 linked another spelling (`SDL3_ttf::SDL3_ttf-static`), use Task 1.1's.

- [ ] **Step 2: Run it and see it fail**

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the header**

Create `src/fontset.h`:

```c
// Fallback fonts (spec section 4). Each font StreamFlex opens for titles or the settings screen is a
// set: the font itself and the fallbacks attached to it at its size, all owned by the set. Before a
// text is first measured, fontset_prepare() gives each character the font lacks its provider, the
// first usable candidate that has it, attached in candidate order; SDL_ttf then measures, wraps and
// draws through the fallbacks. A text the font covers costs one lookup per character and opens
// nothing. Main thread only, as SDL_ttf's one FreeType library is.
#ifndef FONTSET_H
#define FONTSET_H

#include <stdbool.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include <SDL3_ttf/SDL_ttf.h>

#define FILENAME_FALLBACK_FONT "DejaVuSans.ttf"   // The bundled font every candidate list ends with
#define FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE 12.0f                  // The size a font's own coverage is learned at, with no fallbacks

typedef struct FontSet FontSet;

// A font a character the primary lacks may come from; fontcand.c lists them in the order they are tried
typedef struct FontCandidate {
    char *path;            // The font file
    int face;              // TTF_PROP_FONT_CREATE_FACE_NUMBER: fontconfig's (instance << 16) | face
    const char *family;    // Windows: the family it was found as; NULL elsewhere
    const void *charset;   // Linux: fontconfig's character set for it, which covers() reads
    bool (*covers)(const struct FontCandidate *candidate, Uint32 ch);   // NULL: a probe learns its coverage
} FontCandidate;

FontSet *fontset_open(const char *path, float size, int face, const char *role);   // role: "titles", "settings rows", "settings headers", "settings notes"
void fontset_prepare(FontSet *set, const char *text);   // Attach a provider for each character the font lacks, before the first measure
TTF_Font *fontset_font(FontSet *set);                    // The primary, fallbacks attached; NULL for no set
bool fontset_resize(FontSet *set, float size);          // Fallbacks first, then the primary; a set that fails is closed by its caller
void fontset_close(FontSet *set);                       // The font first, then the fallbacks (#549)
void fontset_quit(void);                                // Before TTF_Quit: the probes, the candidate list, FcFini()

const FontCandidate *fontset_candidates(int *count);   // fontcand.c: built once, logged once
void fontset_candidates_free(void);                    // fontcand.c: the list, and FcFini() on Linux

// The close every font of a set goes through: TTF_CloseFont, unless tests/test_fontset.c records the order
extern void (SDLCALL *fontset_close_font)(TTF_Font *font);

#endif
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the sets**

Create `src/fontset.c`:

```c
// Fallback fonts (spec section 4): font sets, their fallbacks and the order they are kept in. See
// fontset.h. What SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 does that this file relies on is read in its source: adding or
// removing a fallback flushes nothing (TTF_AddFallbackFont, TTF_RemoveFallbackFont), TTF_FontHasGlyph
// follows a font's fallbacks (get_char_index_fallback), and TTF_SetFontSize resizes only its font.
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include <SDL3_ttf/SDL_ttf.h>
#include "launcher.h"
#include "fontset.h"
#include "fontrules.h"
#include "fileio.h"
#include "debug.h"

#define PRIMARY_PROBES 4   // Primaries whose own coverage is kept: the titles' file and the settings', with room for a reload

struct FontSet {
    TTF_Font *font;        // The primary: what the set's users measure and draw with
    char *path;            // Its file and face, for its probe
    int face;
    float size;
    const char *role;      // For the log: "titles", "settings rows", "settings headers" or "settings notes"
    TTF_Font **fallbacks;  // Attached to the primary in candidate order...
    int *ranks;            // ...with each one's place in the candidate list
    int count;
    int capacity;
    Uint32 *seen;          // The code points looked at already: open addressing, 0 a free slot
    int seen_count;
    int seen_capacity;     // A power of two; 0 before the first
};

// What the session has learned of a candidate, beside the list fontcand.c built
typedef struct {
    TTF_Font *probe;       // At FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE with no fallbacks: the coverage of a candidate with no charset
    bool probed;           // Its probe was tried
    bool vetted;           // It draws the character it was first picked for
    bool rejected;         // Skipped for the session
} CandidateState;

void (SDLCALL *fontset_close_font)(TTF_Font *font) = NULL;

static const FontCandidate *candidates = NULL;   // NULL until a character first needs one
static CandidateState *states = NULL;
static int candidate_count = 0;
static struct {
    char *path;
    int face;
    TTF_Font *probe;       // NULL when it would not open: the primary is then taken to have everything
    Uint32 used;
} primaries[PRIMARY_PROBES];
static Uint32 primary_clock = 0;
static Uint32 *missing = NULL;                   // Code points no font draws, each logged once a session
static int missing_count = 0;
static int missing_capacity = 0;

// A function to open a face of a font file at a size, the face being a property in SDL3_ttf.
// Something there that is not a regular file (a pipe, say, whose read would wait for good) is never
// opened, as open_font_file() refuses it, and SDL_GetError() says why.
static TTF_Font *open_face(const char *path, float size, int face)
{
    if (fileio_not_a_file(path)) {
        SDL_SetError("%s", fileio_last_error());
        return NULL;
    }
    SDL_PropertiesID props = SDL_CreateProperties();
    if (props == 0)
        return NULL;
    SDL_SetStringProperty(props, TTF_PROP_FONT_CREATE_FILENAME_STRING, path);
    SDL_SetFloatProperty(props, TTF_PROP_FONT_CREATE_SIZE_FLOAT, size);
    SDL_SetNumberProperty(props, TTF_PROP_FONT_CREATE_FACE_NUMBER, face);
    TTF_Font *font = TTF_OpenFontWithProperties(props);
    SDL_DestroyProperties(props);
    return font;
}

// A function to close one of a set's fonts
static void close_font(TTF_Font *font)
{
    if (font == NULL)
        return;
    if (fontset_close_font != NULL)
        fontset_close_font(font);
    else
        TTF_CloseFont(font);
}

// A function to put a code point in a table of seen ones that has room for it
static void seen_insert(Uint32 *table, int capacity, Uint32 ch)
{
    int i = (int) ((ch * 2654435761u) & (Uint32) (capacity - 1));
    while (table[i] != 0)
        i = (i + 1) & (capacity - 1);
    table[i] = ch;
}

// A function to tell whether a set has looked at a code point already, and remember it if not, so the
// settings screen, which measures its rows every frame, pays one lookup per character. A table that
// cannot grow (out of memory) only costs the lookup again.
static bool seen_before(FontSet *set, Uint32 ch)
{
    if (set->seen_capacity > 0) {
        int i = (int) ((ch * 2654435761u) & (Uint32) (set->seen_capacity - 1));
        for (; set->seen[i] != 0; i = (i + 1) & (set->seen_capacity - 1)) {
            if (set->seen[i] == ch)
                return true;
        }
    }
    if (2 * (set->seen_count + 1) > set->seen_capacity) {
        int capacity = set->seen_capacity > 0 ? 2 * set->seen_capacity : 64;
        Uint32 *table = calloc((size_t) capacity, sizeof(Uint32));
        if (table == NULL)
            return false;
        for (int i = 0; i < set->seen_capacity; i++) {
            if (set->seen[i] != 0)
                seen_insert(table, capacity, set->seen[i]);
        }
        free(set->seen);
        set->seen = table;
        set->seen_capacity = capacity;
    }
    seen_insert(set->seen, set->seen_capacity, ch);
    set->seen_count++;
    return false;
}

// A function to get a primary's own font at FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE with no fallbacks: once a fallback is
// attached, TTF_FontHasGlyph() on the primary answers through it, so only this can say what the
// primary itself lacks. Kept for the session, a few files at most; never attached to anything.
static TTF_Font *primary_probe(const FontSet *set)
{
    int oldest = 0;
    primary_clock++;
    for (int i = 0; i < PRIMARY_PROBES; i++) {
        if (primaries[i].path != NULL && primaries[i].face == set->face && strcmp(primaries[i].path, set->path) == 0) {
            primaries[i].used = primary_clock;
            return primaries[i].probe;
        }
        if (primaries[i].used < primaries[oldest].used)
            oldest = i;
    }
    if (primaries[oldest].probe != NULL)
        TTF_CloseFont(primaries[oldest].probe);
    free(primaries[oldest].path);
    primaries[oldest].path = strdup(set->path);
    primaries[oldest].face = set->face;
    primaries[oldest].probe = primaries[oldest].path != NULL ? open_face(set->path, FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE, set->face) : NULL;
    primaries[oldest].used = primary_clock;
    return primaries[oldest].probe;
}

// A function to tell whether a set's primary has a glyph of its own for a code point
static bool primary_has(FontSet *set, Uint32 ch)
{
    if (set->count == 0)
        return TTF_FontHasGlyph(set->font, ch);   // No fallback yet: it answers for itself
    TTF_Font *probe = primary_probe(set);
    return probe == NULL || TTF_FontHasGlyph(probe, ch);
}

// A function to get the candidates, built the first time a character needs one, with room for what
// this session learns of each; returns how many there are (0 for none, or no memory for them)
static int candidate_list(void)
{
    if (candidates == NULL) {
        int found = 0;
        const FontCandidate *list = fontset_candidates(&found);
        CandidateState *learned = found > 0 ? calloc((size_t) found, sizeof(CandidateState)) : NULL;
        if (list != NULL && learned != NULL) {
            candidates = list;
            states = learned;
            candidate_count = found;
        }
        else
            free(learned);
    }
    return candidate_count;
}

// A function to skip a candidate for the session, saying why, once
static void reject(int rank, const char *why)
{
    states[rank].rejected = true;
    log_debug("Fonts: skipped %s: %s", candidates[rank].path, why);
    if (states[rank].probe != NULL) {
        TTF_CloseFont(states[rank].probe);
        states[rank].probe = NULL;
    }
}

// A function to tell whether a candidate has a glyph for a code point: from fontconfig's character set,
// or, for a candidate with none (Windows' and the bundled font), from its probe, opened once, when the
// walk first reaches it
static bool covers(int rank, Uint32 ch)
{
    if (candidates[rank].covers != NULL)
        return candidates[rank].covers(&candidates[rank], ch);
    CandidateState *state = &states[rank];
    if (!state->probed) {
        state->probed = true;
        state->probe = open_face(candidates[rank].path, FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE, candidates[rank].face);
        if (state->probe == NULL) {
            reject(rank, SDL_GetError());
            return false;
        }
    }
    return state->probe != NULL && TTF_FontHasGlyph(state->probe, ch);
}

// A function to tell whether a font draws any ink for a code point: a pixel of its blended glyph
// that is not transparent
static bool has_ink(TTF_Font *font, Uint32 ch)
{
    SDL_Color white = { 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF, 0xFF };
    SDL_Surface *glyph = TTF_RenderGlyph_Blended(font, ch, white);
    bool ink = false;
    for (int y = 0; glyph != NULL && y < glyph->h && !ink; y++) {
        for (int x = 0; x < glyph->w && !ink; x++) {
            Uint8 r, g, b, a;
            ink = SDL_ReadSurfacePixel(glyph, x, y, &r, &g, &b, &a) && a > 0;
        }
    }
    SDL_DestroySurface(glyph);
    return ink;
}

// A function to check, the first time a candidate is picked, that it can draw the character it was
// picked for. One that cannot be scaled (Noto Color Emoji, a CBDT bitmap font drawn 128 px tall at any
// size) or that draws no ink for it (Fedora's Noto COLRv1 emoji, at SDL_ttf 3.2.2) is skipped for the
// session. A space draws no ink by design, so it is not asked for any. False when it was skipped.
static bool vetted(int rank, Uint32 ch)
{
    CandidateState *state = &states[rank];
    if (state->vetted)
        return true;
    bool own = state->probe == NULL;   // A candidate with a charset has no probe: one is opened for this
    TTF_Font *font = own ? open_face(candidates[rank].path, FONTSET_PROBE_SIZE, candidates[rank].face) : state->probe;
    char why[256];
    bool ok = false;
    if (font == NULL)
        snprintf(why, sizeof(why), "%s", SDL_GetError());
    else if (!TTF_FontIsScalable(font))
        snprintf(why, sizeof(why), "a bitmap font cannot be scaled to the text");
    else if (!fontrules_blank(ch) && !has_ink(font, ch))
        snprintf(why, sizeof(why), "it draws nothing for U+%04X", (unsigned) ch);
    else
        ok = true;
    if (own && font != NULL)
        TTF_CloseFont(font);
    if (!ok) {
        reject(rank, why);
        return false;
    }
    state->vetted = true;
    return true;
}

// A function to make room for one more fallback; false when out of memory
static bool grow(FontSet *set)
{
    if (set->count < set->capacity)
        return true;
    int capacity = set->capacity > 0 ? 2 * set->capacity : 4;
    TTF_Font **fallbacks = realloc(set->fallbacks, (size_t) capacity * sizeof(TTF_Font *));
    if (fallbacks == NULL)
        return false;
    set->fallbacks = fallbacks;
    int *ranks = realloc(set->ranks, (size_t) capacity * sizeof(int));
    if (ranks == NULL)
        return false;
    set->ranks = ranks;
    set->capacity = capacity;
    return true;
}

// A function to attach a candidate to a set as a fallback, opened at the set's size and kept in
// candidate order: one ranking after every attached one is appended, one ranking earlier rebuilds the
// chain, as SDL_ttf takes the first fallback that has a glyph. Without this, a star that attached
// Segoe UI Symbol first would make a later emoji draw from it, in monochrome. No fallback is shared
// between sets, or made with TTF_CopyFont, which copies no fallbacks and shares one file stream.
// False when it could not be opened (it is then skipped for the session).
static bool attach(FontSet *set, int rank, Uint32 ch)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < set->count; i++) {
        if (set->ranks[i] == rank)
            return true;   // Attached already, for another character
    }
    if (!grow(set))
        return false;
    TTF_Font *fallback = open_face(candidates[rank].path, set->size, candidates[rank].face);
    if (fallback == NULL) {
        reject(rank, SDL_GetError());
        return false;
    }
    int at = fontrules_insert_at(set->ranks, set->count, rank);
    memmove(&set->fallbacks[at + 1], &set->fallbacks[at], (size_t) (set->count - at) * sizeof(TTF_Font *));
    memmove(&set->ranks[at + 1], &set->ranks[at], (size_t) (set->count - at) * sizeof(int));
    set->fallbacks[at] = fallback;
    set->ranks[at] = rank;
    set->count++;
    if (at == set->count - 1)
        TTF_AddFallbackFont(set->font, fallback);
    else {
        TTF_ClearFallbackFonts(set->font);
        for (int i = 0; i < set->count; i++)
            TTF_AddFallbackFont(set->font, set->fallbacks[i]);
    }
    char face[48];
    fontrules_face_text(candidates[rank].face, face, sizeof(face));
    log_debug("Fonts: the %i pt %s fall back to %s (%s), first for U+%04X", (int) (set->size + 0.5f), set->role,
        candidates[rank].path, face, (unsigned) ch);
    return true;
}

// A function to say, once a session for each code point, that no font draws it. Not a debug line:
// the user can install a font that has it.
static void report_missing(Uint32 ch)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < missing_count; i++) {
        if (missing[i] == ch)
            return;
    }
    if (missing_count == missing_capacity) {
        int capacity = missing_capacity > 0 ? 2 * missing_capacity : 16;
        Uint32 *grown = realloc(missing, (size_t) capacity * sizeof(Uint32));
        if (grown != NULL) {
            missing = grown;
            missing_capacity = capacity;
        }
    }
    if (missing_count < missing_capacity)
        missing[missing_count++] = ch;
    log_error("Fonts: no installed font draws U+%04X; it shows as a box", (unsigned) ch);
}

// A function to give a character the primary lacks its provider: the first candidate, in order, that
// is not skipped, has it, and draws it. A character none has draws as the primary's box.
static void find_provider(FontSet *set, Uint32 ch)
{
    int count = candidate_list();
    for (int rank = 0; rank < count; rank++) {
        if (!states[rank].rejected && covers(rank, ch) && vetted(rank, ch) && attach(set, rank, ch))
            return;
    }
    report_missing(ch);
}

// A function to open a font as a set, with no fallbacks yet; NULL, with SDL's reason, when it does not open
FontSet *fontset_open(const char *path, float size, int face, const char *role)
{
    FontSet *set = calloc(1, sizeof(FontSet));
    char *copy = strdup(path);
    TTF_Font *font = set != NULL && copy != NULL ? open_face(path, size, face) : NULL;
    if (font == NULL) {
        if (set == NULL || copy == NULL)
            SDL_OutOfMemory();
        free(copy);
        free(set);
        return NULL;
    }
    set->font = font;
    set->path = copy;
    set->face = face;
    set->size = size;
    set->role = role;
    return set;
}

// A function to attach, before a text is first measured, a provider for each character its font
// lacks. A set remembers what it has looked at, so a text it has seen costs a lookup per character.
void fontset_prepare(FontSet *set, const char *text)
{
    if (set == NULL || text == NULL)
        return;
    size_t length = strlen(text);
    Uint32 ch;
    while ((ch = SDL_StepUTF8(&text, &length)) != 0) {
        if (fontrules_skip(ch) || seen_before(set, ch) || primary_has(set, ch))
            continue;
        find_provider(set, ch);
    }
}

// A function to get a set's font, its fallbacks attached
TTF_Font *fontset_font(FontSet *set)
{
    return set != NULL ? set->font : NULL;
}

// A function to resize a set: its fallbacks first, as TTF_SetFontSize() resizes only the font it is
// given, then its font, whose resize flushes its cached glyph positions. Nothing measures between the
// calls. False, with SDL's reason, when one fails; its caller then closes the set.
bool fontset_resize(FontSet *set, float size)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < set->count; i++) {
        if (!TTF_SetFontSize(set->fallbacks[i], size))
            return false;
    }
    if (!TTF_SetFontSize(set->font, size))
        return false;
    set->size = size;
    return true;
}

// A function to close a set. The font first: its close flushes its cache of glyph positions, which
// point into its fallbacks' glyphs, and clears its fallbacks. At SDL_ttf 3.2.2 removing a fallback
// flushes nothing, so a fallback closed while its font lives leaves that cache pointing at freed
// memory for the next measure of the same text (upstream #549, fixed only after 3.2.2).
void fontset_close(FontSet *set)
{
    if (set == NULL)
        return;
    close_font(set->font);
    for (int i = 0; i < set->count; i++)
        close_font(set->fallbacks[i]);
    free(set->fallbacks);
    free(set->ranks);
    free(set->seen);
    free(set->path);
    free(set);
}

// A function to let go, before TTF_Quit and once every set is closed, of what the session kept: the
// probes, what was learned of each candidate, and the list itself, whose free calls FcFini() on Linux.
// The log says what went, as LeakSanitizer cannot see what a static still points to.
void fontset_quit(void)
{
    int probes = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < PRIMARY_PROBES; i++) {
        if (primaries[i].probe != NULL) {
            TTF_CloseFont(primaries[i].probe);
            probes++;
        }
        free(primaries[i].path);
    }
    memset(primaries, 0, sizeof(primaries));
    primary_clock = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < candidate_count; i++) {
        if (states[i].probe != NULL) {
            TTF_CloseFont(states[i].probe);
            probes++;
        }
    }
    if (candidates != NULL)
        log_debug("Fonts: let go of %i fallback candidates and %i probe fonts at quit", candidate_count, probes);
    free(states);
    states = NULL;
    candidates = NULL;
    candidate_count = 0;
    free(missing);
    missing = NULL;
    missing_count = 0;
    missing_capacity = 0;
    fontset_candidates_free();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Run each mutant; each must fail, then the unmutated control passes**

Each row is one run with filter `fontset`, `MUTANT_FILE=src/fontset.c` and this `MUTANT_SED`:

| Mutant | `MUTANT_SED` | Expected failure |
|---|---|---|
| The fallbacks close first (#549) | `/^    close_font(set->font);$/d; s/^    free(set->fallbacks);$/    close_font(set->font);\n    free(set->fallbacks);/` | `ERROR: AddressSanitizer: heap-use-after-free` in `TTF_Size_Internal`, called from `record_close` |
| The primary answers through its fallbacks | `s/^    if (set->count == 0)$/    if (1)/` | `width(fontset_font(set), ARROW) is <DejaVu's>, expected <FreeSans's>` |
| An earlier provider is appended | `s/^    if (at == set->count - 1)$/    if (1)/` | the same `ARROW` check |
| A rebuild re-adds only the new fallback | `s/^        for (int i = 0; i < set->count; i++)$/        for (int i = at; i <= at; i++)/` | `width(fontset_font(set), STAR STAR) is ..., expected ...` |
| A resize leaves the fallbacks | `s/if (!TTF_SetFontSize(set->fallbacks\[i\], size))/if (0)/` | `width(fontset_font(set), "A" STAR STAR) is ..., expected ...` in `test_resize` |
| A set forgets what it saw | `s/|| seen_before(set, ch) ||/|| (seen_before(set, ch) \&\& false) ||/` | `covers_calls is 2, expected 1` |
| Ignorables are looked up | `s/if (fontrules_skip(ch) || /if (/` | `lines_with("Fonts: skipped ") is 1, expected 0` in `test_ignorable` |
| A covered text builds the list | `s/^    set->font = font;$/    candidate_list();\n    set->font = font;/` | `lists_built is 1, expected 0` |
| A missing character is logged per set | `s/if (missing\[i\] == ch)$/if (missing[i] == ch \&\& false)/` | `lines_with("Fonts: no installed font draws U+4E2D; it shows as a box") is 2, expected 1` |
| A space is asked for ink | `s/else if (!fontrules_blank(ch) && !has_ink(font, ch))/else if (!has_ink(font, ch))/` | `lines_with("Fonts: skipped ") is 1, expected 0` in `test_blank_space` |

Each run prints `MUTANT applied to src/fontset.c:` with its diff, then `fontset ... ***Failed` (or `Subprocess aborted` for the ASan row). Then the control: Step 5's command with no mutant, `100% tests passed`. The two rejection branches in `vetted()` (`TTF_FontIsScalable`, `has_ink` on a real color font) need the image's real fonts, so their mutants run in Task 1.16a Step 7. The order inside `fontset_resize()` (fallbacks before the primary) is the spec's; nothing measures between the calls, so no check can tell it from the reverse, and no mutant is run for it. - Produces, in `src/fontcand.c`: `const FontCandidate *fontset_candidates(int *count)` (built once, logged once) and `void fontset_candidates_free(void)`.

  ```c
  int fontrules_windows_families(const char *language, const char *country, const char **out);   // All 17, in walk order
  ```

- Produces the log lines, debug, once, when the list is built: `Fonts: <N> fallback candidates from fontconfig` (Linux) and `Fonts: <N> fallback candidates in <WINDIR>\Fonts` (Windows); N counts the system's candidates, not the bundled DejaVu Sans after them.

Registry value names, as this machine's `HKLM\...\Fonts` holds them (read 2026-10-11): `Segoe UI (TrueType)` = `segoeui.ttf`, `Segoe UI Emoji (TrueType)` = `seguiemj.ttf`, `Segoe UI Symbol (TrueType)` = `seguisym.ttf`, `Yu Gothic Regular & Yu Gothic UI Semilight (TrueType)` = `YuGothR.ttc`, `Microsoft YaHei & Microsoft YaHei UI (TrueType)` = `msyh.ttc`, `Microsoft JhengHei & Microsoft JhengHei UI (TrueType)` = `msjh.ttc`, `Malgun Gothic (TrueType)` = `malgun.ttf`, `Nirmala UI & Nirmala UI Bold & ... (TrueType)` = `Nirmala.ttc`, and `Leelawadee UI`, `Ebrima`, `Gadugi`, `Myanmar Text`, `Javanese Text`, `Microsoft Himalaya`, `Mongolian Baiti`, `Sylfaen`, `Segoe UI Historic`, each `(TrueType)`. A collection's value lists its faces in order with ` & `, so the matching face's position is its face number; `Segoe UI Bold (TrueType)` and `Yu Gothic Medium & Yu Gothic UI Regular` never match `Segoe UI` or `Yu Gothic`. Every value names a bare file in the Fonts folder: Windows' own fonts are never elsewhere, so no full-path form is handled.

Create `tests/test_fontcand.c`:

```c
// fontcand.c (spec section 4, Candidates). On Linux, fontconfig's sort over a fixture fontconfig file
// whose one folder is the bundled fonts (CMake writes it), so no machine's own fonts are read; on
// Windows, the walk over the runner's own Fonts registry key. Both end with the bundled DejaVu Sans,
// which this file's find_default_font() stands in for, as it stands in for the launcher's log.
#include <stdarg.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include <SDL3_ttf/SDL_ttf.h>
#include "check.h"
#include "launcher.h"
#include "image.h"
#include "debug.h"
#include "fontset.h"
#include "fontrules.h"

#define BUNDLED FONTCAND_TEST_FONTS "/DejaVuSans.ttf"
#define LINES 16

static char logged[LINES][512];
static int logged_count = 0;

// The launcher's log, kept for the checks to read
void output_log(LogLevel log_level, const char *format, ...)
{
    (void) log_level;
    if (logged_count == LINES)
        return;
    va_list args;
    va_start(args, format);
    vsnprintf(logged[logged_count], sizeof(logged[0]), format, args);
    va_end(args);
    logged[logged_count][strcspn(logged[logged_count], "\r\n")] = '\0';
    logged_count++;
}

// The bundled font, where the launcher would find it
char *find_default_font(const char *font)
{
    CHECK_STR(font, FILENAME_FALLBACK_FONT);
    return strdup(BUNDLED);
}

#ifdef _WIN32
// A function to test the registry walk: Segoe UI first, every family found once and in fontrules'
// order for the runner's locale, each with no charset, so a probe learns its coverage
static void test_registry(void)
{
    int count = 0;
    const FontCandidate *list = fontset_candidates(&count);
    CHECK(count >= 2);   // Segoe UI at least, then DejaVu Sans
    char want[64];
    snprintf(want, sizeof(want), "Fonts: %i fallback candidates in ", count - 1);
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 1);
    CHECK(strncmp(logged[0], want, strlen(want)) == 0 && strstr(logged[0], "\\Fonts") != NULL);
    CHECK_STR(list[0].family, "Segoe UI");
    CHECK(strstr(list[0].path, "\\segoeui.ttf") != NULL);
    CHECK_INT(list[0].face, 0);
    int locales = 0;
    SDL_Locale **preferred = SDL_GetPreferredLocales(&locales);
    const char *families[FONTRULES_WINDOWS_FAMILIES];
    fontrules_windows_families(preferred != NULL && locales > 0 ? preferred[0]->language : NULL,
                               preferred != NULL && locales > 0 ? preferred[0]->country : NULL, families);
    SDL_free(preferred);
    int last = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < count - 1; i++) {
        CHECK(list[i].covers == NULL && list[i].charset == NULL);
        int at = -1;
        for (int f = 0; f < FONTRULES_WINDOWS_FAMILIES && at < 0; f++)
            at = list[i].family != NULL && strcmp(list[i].family, families[f]) == 0 ? f : -1;
        CHECK(at > last);
        last = at;
    }
    CHECK_STR(list[count - 1].path, BUNDLED);
    CHECK(list[count - 1].family == NULL && list[count - 1].covers == NULL);
    int again = 0;
    CHECK(fontset_candidates(&again) == list);   // Built once...
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 1);                  // ...and logged once
    fontset_candidates_free();
}
#else
// A function to test fontconfig's sort over the bundled fonts: all seven, each with fontconfig's
// character set and face 0, then the bundled DejaVu Sans again with none; FcFini() at the end leaves
// LeakSanitizer no fontconfig cache
static void test_fontconfig(void)
{
    int count = 0;
    const FontCandidate *list = fontset_candidates(&count);
    CHECK_INT(count, 8);
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 1);
    CHECK_STR(logged[0], "Fonts: 7 fallback candidates from fontconfig");
    int dejavu = -1;
    int open_sans = -1;
    for (int i = 0; i < count - 1; i++) {
        CHECK(list[i].covers != NULL && list[i].charset != NULL);
        CHECK_INT(list[i].face, 0);
        CHECK(list[i].covers != NULL && list[i].covers(&list[i], 'A'));
        if (strstr(list[i].path, "/DejaVuSans.ttf") != NULL)
            dejavu = i;
        if (strstr(list[i].path, "/OpenSans-Regular.ttf") != NULL)
            open_sans = i;
    }
    CHECK(dejavu >= 0 && open_sans >= 0);
    if (dejavu >= 0 && open_sans >= 0) {
        CHECK(list[dejavu].covers(&list[dejavu], 0x2605));          // DejaVu Sans has the star...
        CHECK(!list[open_sans].covers(&list[open_sans], 0x2605));   // ...and Open Sans does not
    }
    CHECK_STR(list[count - 1].path, BUNDLED);
    CHECK(list[count - 1].covers == NULL && list[count - 1].charset == NULL);
    int again = 0;
    CHECK(fontset_candidates(&again) == list);   // Built once...
    CHECK_INT(again, count);
    CHECK_INT(logged_count, 1);                  // ...and logged once
    fontset_candidates_free();
}
#endif

int main(void)
{
#ifdef _WIN32
    test_registry();
#else
    test_fontconfig();
#endif
    SDL_Quit();
    return check_report();
}
```

```cmake
target_include_directories(test_fontcand PRIVATE "${PROJECT_SOURCE_DIR}/src")
```

Append to `src/fontrules.h`, before `#endif`:

```c
#define FONTRULES_WINDOWS_FAMILIES 17

const char *fontrules_cjk_first(const char *language, const char *country);
int fontrules_windows_families(const char *language, const char *country, const char **out);
int fontrules_registry_face(const char *value_name, const char *family);
```

Append to `src/fontrules.c`:

```c
// Windows' Han and Hangul families. YaHei stays behind Yu Gothic, which lacks simplified Han.
static const char *const CJK[] = { "Yu Gothic", "Microsoft YaHei", "Microsoft JhengHei", "Malgun Gothic" };

// The families after them: Indic, Thai and Lao, African, Myanmar, Javanese, Tibetan, Mongolian,
// Armenian and Georgian, and historic scripts
static const char *const AFTER_CJK[] = {
    "Nirmala UI", "Leelawadee UI", "Ebrima", "Gadugi", "Myanmar Text", "Javanese Text", "Microsoft Himalaya",
    "Mongolian Baiti", "Sylfaen", "Segoe UI Historic"
};

// A function to compare two ASCII strings without case; NULL matches nothing
static bool same(const char *a, const char *b)
{
    if (a == NULL || b == NULL)
        return false;
    for (; *a != '\0' && *b != '\0'; a++, b++) {
        char x = (*a >= 'A' && *a <= 'Z') ? (char) (*a - 'A' + 'a') : *a;
        char y = (*b >= 'A' && *b <= 'Z') ? (char) (*b - 'A' + 'a') : *b;
        if (x != y)
            return false;
    }
    return *a == *b;
}

// A function to choose the CJK family Windows' list tries first, from the user's preferred locale
// (SDL_GetPreferredLocales' first): ja Yu Gothic, ko Malgun Gothic, zh with TW, HK or MO Microsoft
// JhengHei, anything else Microsoft YaHei
const char *fontrules_cjk_first(const char *language, const char *country)
{
    if (same(language, "ja"))
        return "Yu Gothic";
    if (same(language, "ko"))
        return "Malgun Gothic";
    if (same(language, "zh") && (same(country, "TW") || same(country, "HK") || same(country, "MO")))
        return "Microsoft JhengHei";
    return "Microsoft YaHei";
}

// A function to list Windows' fallback families in the order they are tried, FONTRULES_WINDOWS_FAMILIES
// of them: Segoe UI; Segoe UI Emoji before Symbol, so emoji draw in color; Segoe UI Symbol; the four
// CJK families, the locale's first; then the other scripts. Returns how many it wrote.
int fontrules_windows_families(const char *language, const char *country, const char **out)
{
    int n = 0;
    out[n++] = "Segoe UI";
    out[n++] = "Segoe UI Emoji";
    out[n++] = "Segoe UI Symbol";
    const char *first = fontrules_cjk_first(language, country);
    out[n++] = first;
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(CJK) / sizeof(CJK[0]); i++) {
        if (strcmp(CJK[i], first) != 0)
            out[n++] = CJK[i];
    }
    for (size_t i = 0; i < sizeof(AFTER_CJK) / sizeof(AFTER_CJK[0]); i++)
        out[n++] = AFTER_CJK[i];
    return n;
}

// A function to find a family's face in a Fonts registry value name. "Yu Gothic Regular & Yu Gothic UI
// Semilight (TrueType)" lists a collection's faces in order, and "Segoe UI (TrueType)" a file's one
// face. A face is the family when it reads the family's name alone, or with " Regular" after it, so
// Segoe UI Bold is never Segoe UI. -1 when no face is.
int fontrules_registry_face(const char *value_name, const char *family)
{
    size_t length = strlen(value_name);
    const char *paren = strrchr(value_name, '(');   // " (TrueType)" or " (OpenType)"
    if (paren != NULL && paren > value_name && paren[-1] == ' ')
        length = (size_t) (paren - 1 - value_name);
    size_t family_length = strlen(family);
    const char *start = value_name;
    const char *end = value_name + length;
    for (int face = 0; start < end; face++) {
        const char *amp = strstr(start, " & ");
        const char *stop = amp != NULL && amp < end ? amp : end;
        size_t n = (size_t) (stop - start);
        if ((n == family_length && strncmp(start, family, n) == 0)
            || (n == family_length + 8 && strncmp(start, family, family_length) == 0
                && strncmp(start + family_length, " Regular", 8) == 0))
            return face;
        start = stop == end ? end : stop + 3;
    }
    return -1;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write the candidate list**

Create `src/fontcand.c`:

```c
// The fallback candidates (spec section 4, Candidates): the fonts a character the chosen font lacks
// may come from, in the order fontset.c tries them. On Linux, fontconfig's sort for sans-serif, which
// follows the desktop's own choices, fonts the user installed among them, and carries each font's
// character set, so no font is opened to ask what it covers. On Windows, a fixed list of Windows' own
// families, found by their Fonts registry value names, never by file name, as file names drift
// between builds; each one's coverage is learned by a probe (fontset.c). The bundled DejaVu Sans comes
// last on both. Built once, the first time a character needs it, and logged once.
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <SDL3/SDL.h>
#include <SDL3_ttf/SDL_ttf.h>
#include "launcher.h"
#include "image.h"
#include "fontset.h"
#include "fontrules.h"
#include "debug.h"
#ifdef _WIN32
#include <windows.h>
#else
#include <fontconfig/fontconfig.h>
#endif

#define PATH_BYTES 2048

static FontCandidate *list = NULL;
static int count = 0;
static int capacity = 0;
static bool built = false;
#ifndef _WIN32
static FcFontSet *sorted = NULL;       // Holds the character sets the candidates point to, until they are let go
static bool fontconfig_used = false;
#endif

// A function to add a candidate; false when out of memory (it is then left out)
static bool add(const char *path, int face, const char *family, const void *charset,
                bool (*covers)(const FontCandidate *candidate, Uint32 ch))
{
    if (count == capacity) {
        int grown = capacity > 0 ? 2 * capacity : 32;
        FontCandidate *bigger = realloc(list, (size_t) grown * sizeof(FontCandidate));
        if (bigger == NULL)
            return false;
        list = bigger;
        capacity = grown;
    }
    char *copy = strdup(path);
    if (copy == NULL)
        return false;
    list[count++] = (FontCandidate) { .path = copy, .face = face, .family = family, .charset = charset, .covers = covers };
    return true;
}

#ifdef _WIN32
// A Fonts registry value: the faces its name lists ("Segoe UI (TrueType)") and the file that holds them
typedef struct {
    char name[1024];
    char file[PATH_BYTES];
} RegistryFont;

// A function to turn a UTF-16 string into UTF-8, into a buffer; false when it does not fit
static bool to_utf8(const wchar_t *wide, char *out, int size)
{
    return WideCharToMultiByte(CP_UTF8, 0, wide, -1, out, size, NULL, NULL) > 0;
}

// A function to read the machine's Fonts registry key, where Windows' own fonts are named (fontscan.c
// reads it, and the user's, for the font picker): each value's name and file. Caller frees; NULL,
// with a count of 0, when it cannot be read.
static RegistryFont *read_registry(int *found)
{
    *found = 0;
    HKEY key;
    if (RegOpenKeyExW(HKEY_LOCAL_MACHINE, L"SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Windows NT\\CurrentVersion\\Fonts", 0, KEY_READ, &key) != ERROR_SUCCESS)
        return NULL;
    RegistryFont *fonts = NULL;
    int room = 0;
    wchar_t name[512];
    wchar_t data[1024];
    for (DWORD i = 0;; i++) {
        DWORD name_size = (DWORD) (sizeof(name) / sizeof(name[0]));
        DWORD data_size = (DWORD) (sizeof(data) - sizeof(wchar_t));
        DWORD type = 0;
        LONG result = RegEnumValueW(key, i, name, &name_size, NULL, &type, (LPBYTE) data, &data_size);
        if (result == ERROR_NO_MORE_ITEMS)
            break;
        if (result != ERROR_SUCCESS || type != REG_SZ)
            continue;
        data[data_size / sizeof(wchar_t)] = L'\0';
        if (*found == room) {
            int grown = room > 0 ? 2 * room : 256;
            RegistryFont *bigger = realloc(fonts, (size_t) grown * sizeof(RegistryFont));
            if (bigger == NULL)
                break;
            fonts = bigger;
            room = grown;
        }
        if (to_utf8(name, fonts[*found].name, (int) sizeof(fonts[*found].name))
            && to_utf8(data, fonts[*found].file, (int) sizeof(fonts[*found].file)))
            (*found)++;
    }
    RegCloseKey(key);
    return fonts;
}

// A function to add Windows' own families in their fixed order, the first preferred locale choosing
// the first CJK family (fontrules_windows_families()), each found by its registry value name; its
// file is in the Fonts folder. Returns how many it added.
static int add_windows(const char *fonts_folder)
{
    int locale_count = 0;
    SDL_Locale **locales = SDL_GetPreferredLocales(&locale_count);
    const char *language = locales != NULL && locale_count > 0 ? locales[0]->language : NULL;
    const char *country = locales != NULL && locale_count > 0 ? locales[0]->country : NULL;
    const char *families[FONTRULES_WINDOWS_FAMILIES];
    int family_count = fontrules_windows_families(language, country, families);
    SDL_free(locales);   // The families are fontrules.c's own strings: nothing points into the locales
    int registered = 0;
    RegistryFont *fonts = read_registry(&registered);
    int added = 0;
    char path[PATH_BYTES];
    for (int f = 0; f < family_count; f++) {
        for (int i = 0; i < registered; i++) {
            int face = fontrules_registry_face(fonts[i].name, families[f]);
            if (face < 0)
                continue;
            snprintf(path, sizeof(path), "%s\\%s", fonts_folder, fonts[i].file);
            if (add(path, face, families[f], NULL, NULL))
                added++;
            break;
        }
    }
    free(fonts);
    return added;
}
#else
// A function to read a candidate's coverage from the character set fontconfig gave it
static bool charset_covers(const FontCandidate *candidate, Uint32 ch)
{
    return FcCharSetHasChar((const FcCharSet *) candidate->charset, ch);
}

// A function to add fontconfig's sort for sans-serif, Regular, with the user's language (which
// FcDefaultSubstitute adds), untrimmed, so a font a skipped one would have shadowed is still there.
// Each pattern is a face, or a named instance of a variable one; its index is fontconfig's
// (instance << 16) | face, which SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 hands to FT_Open_Face unchanged. A variable font's own
// pattern is left out: it opens at the default instance, which for Fedora's Noto Sans CJK VF is Thin,
// and its named instances follow it in the sort, the Regular one first. Returns how many it added.
static int add_fontconfig(void)
{
    fontconfig_used = true;
    FcPattern *pattern = FcNameParse((const FcChar8 *) "sans-serif:style=Regular");
    if (pattern == NULL)
        return 0;
    FcConfigSubstitute(NULL, pattern, FcMatchPattern);
    FcDefaultSubstitute(pattern);
    FcResult result;
    sorted = FcFontSort(NULL, pattern, FcFalse, NULL, &result);
    FcPatternDestroy(pattern);
    int added = 0;
    for (int i = 0; sorted != NULL && i < sorted->nfont; i++) {
        FcChar8 *file = NULL;
        FcCharSet *charset = NULL;
        int index = 0;
        FcBool variable = FcFalse;
        if (FcPatternGetString(sorted->fonts[i], FC_FILE, 0, &file) != FcResultMatch
            || FcPatternGetCharSet(sorted->fonts[i], FC_CHARSET, 0, &charset) != FcResultMatch)
            continue;
        FcPatternGetInteger(sorted->fonts[i], FC_INDEX, 0, &index);
        if (FcPatternGetBool(sorted->fonts[i], FC_VARIABLE, 0, &variable) == FcResultMatch && variable)
            continue;
        if (add((const char *) file, index, NULL, charset, charset_covers))
            added++;
    }
    return added;
}
#endif

// A function to get the candidates, building them the first time
const FontCandidate *fontset_candidates(int *found)
{
    if (!built) {
        built = true;
#ifdef _WIN32
        char windows[MAX_PATH + 1];
        UINT length = GetWindowsDirectoryA(windows, (UINT) sizeof(windows));
        char folder[PATH_BYTES];
        snprintf(folder, sizeof(folder), "%s\\Fonts", length > 0 && length < sizeof(windows) ? windows : "C:\\Windows");
        log_debug("Fonts: %i fallback candidates in %s", add_windows(folder), folder);
#else
        log_debug("Fonts: %i fallback candidates from fontconfig", add_fontconfig());
#endif
        // The bundled DejaVu Sans last: Greek, Cyrillic, Hebrew, Arabic, symbols and some emoji, in
        // monochrome. It has no character set, so a probe learns its coverage.
        char *bundled = find_default_font(FILENAME_FALLBACK_FONT);
        if (bundled != NULL)
            add(bundled, 0, NULL, NULL, NULL);
        free(bundled);
    }
    *found = count;
    return list;
}

// A function to let go of the candidates (fontset_quit()), and on Linux of fontconfig's caches, so the
// LeakSanitizer leg sees none: only once fontconfig was used, as nothing else in StreamFlex starts it
void fontset_candidates_free(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < count; i++)
        free(list[i].path);
    free(list);
    list = NULL;
    count = 0;
    capacity = 0;
    built = false;
#ifndef _WIN32
    if (sorted != NULL)
        FcFontSetDestroy(sorted);
    sorted = NULL;
    if (fontconfig_used)
        FcFini();
    fontconfig_used = false;
#endif
}
```

```bash
# Fallback fonts (72-fallback-fonts.sh): two fontconfig files a run names with FONTCONFIG_FILE, each
# reading one folder of its own and keeping its cache beside it. fcbox's folder holds sf-box.ttf, whose
# glyphs for 中, 游 and 戏 (U+4E2D, U+6E38, U+620F) are filled squares; fcempty's holds nothing.
for fc in fcbox fcempty; do
    mkdir -p "$TESTER_HOME/$fc/fonts" "$TESTER_HOME/$fc/cache"
    printf '<?xml version="1.0"?>\n<!DOCTYPE fontconfig SYSTEM "urn:fontconfig:fonts.dtd">\n<fontconfig>\n  <dir>%s</dir>\n  <cachedir>%s</cachedir>\n</fontconfig>\n' \
        "$TESTER_HOME/$fc/fonts" "$TESTER_HOME/$fc/cache" > "$TESTER_HOME/$fc/fonts.conf"
done
```
