Create `tests/headless/checks/72-fallback-fonts.sh`:

```bash
# Fallback fonts (spec section 4): a character the title font lacks draws from a fallback font, found by
# fontconfig, with the bundled DejaVu Sans last. FONTCONFIG_FILE names one of run.sh's fixture files, so
# the candidates are known: fcbox's one font, sf-box.ttf, draws 中 as a filled square, and fcempty has
# none. The real-font run reads the image's own fonts instead.

# A function for a +key (run_keys), with the run's NAME and PID: once the menu is drawn, count the
# title color's exact pixels inside the box they span (Color=#FF00FF, which nothing else on the screen
# has) into NAME.box, trying for up to 10 s to see a filled glyph
f72_shot() {
    local name=$1 pid=$2 shot=/tmp/f72.xwd i
    command xdotool search --name '^StreamFlex$' windowmove %@ 0 0 windowsize %@ 1920 1080 > /dev/null 2>&1
    wait_line 'Begin program loop' "$pid" || return 0
    for i in $(seq 25); do
        sleep 0.4
        { xwd -root -silent -out "$shot" && python3 "$HERE/pixels.py" "$shot" count 255,0,255 inside 255,0,255; } \
            > "$out/$name.box" 2>&1
        f72_filled "$name" && return 0
    done
}

# A function to tell whether NAME.box shows a filled glyph: at least 100 pixels, filling 90 % of their
# box. Open Sans's own box for a character it lacks is an outline 1.8 px thick at 36 pt, which leaves
# most of its box empty.
f72_filled() {
    local x0 y0 x1 y1 n
    read -r x0 y0 x1 y1 n < <(sed -n 's/^box \([0-9]*\),\([0-9]*\) to \([0-9]*\),\([0-9]*\): \([0-9]*\) pixels.*/\1 \2 \3 \4 \5/p' "$out/$1.box" 2> /dev/null)
    [ -n "${n:-}" ] && [ "$n" -ge 100 ] && [ $((n * 10)) -ge $(( (x1 - x0 + 1) * (y1 - y0 + 1) * 9 )) ]
}

# sf-box has 中: the title falls back to it, logs that once, draws it filled, and at quit the log says
# what the sets' module let go of
FONTCONFIG_FILE=$TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts.conf run_keys f72-box +f72_shot
log=$out/f72-box.log
ok=1
grep -qxF 'Fonts: 1 fallback candidates from fontconfig' "$log" \
    && [ "$(grep -cxF "Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to $TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts/sf-box.ttf (face 0), first for U+4E2D" "$log")" = 1 ] \
    && ! grep -q 'Fonts: no installed font draws' "$log" && f72_filled f72-box \
    && sed -n '/Quitting program/,$p' "$log" | grep -qE '^Fonts: let go of 2 fallback candidates and [0-9]+ probe fonts at quit$' \
    && ran_clean f72-box && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: a title's 中 falls back to the fixture's box font and draws filled (exit $(cat "$out/f72-box.code"))" $ok
grep '^Fonts:' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
sed 's/^/      /' "$out/f72-box.box" 2> /dev/null

# No font has 中: the title keeps Open Sans's hollow box, and the log says so once (not a debug line)
FONTCONFIG_FILE=$TESTER_HOME/fcempty/fonts.conf CFG=$FX/f72-box.ini run_keys f72-empty +f72_shot
log=$out/f72-empty.log
ok=1
grep -qxF 'Fonts: 0 fallback candidates from fontconfig' "$log" \
    && [ "$(grep -cxF 'Fonts: no installed font draws U+4E2D; it shows as a box' "$log")" = 1 ] \
    && ! grep -q ' fall back to ' "$log" && ! f72_filled f72-empty && ran_clean f72-empty && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: a character no font has keeps the hollow box and is logged once (exit $(cat "$out/f72-empty.code"))" $ok
grep '^Fonts:' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
sed 's/^/      /' "$out/f72-empty.box" 2> /dev/null

# On the image's own fonts: which color emoji font it has is detected, not named by distribution.
# Debian's is a CBDT bitmap font, skipped as one that cannot be scaled; Fedora's is a COLRv1 font,
# skipped as drawing nothing at SDL_ttf 3.2.2. 😀 then falls back to DejaVu Sans, in monochrome, and 中
# to Noto Sans CJK: Debian's Regular collection, face 0, and Fedora's variable one at its Regular named
# instance, 4.
run_quick f72-real
log=$out/f72-real.log
cbdt=/usr/share/fonts/truetype/noto/NotoColorEmoji.ttf
colr=/usr/share/fonts/google-noto-color-emoji-fonts/Noto-COLRv1.ttf
cjk=/usr/share/fonts/opentype/noto/NotoSansCJK-Regular.ttc
cjkvf=/usr/share/fonts/google-noto-sans-cjk-vf-fonts/NotoSansCJK-VF.ttc
skip='' han=''
if [ -f "$cbdt" ]; then skip="Fonts: skipped $cbdt: a bitmap font cannot be scaled to the text"
elif [ -f "$colr" ]; then skip="Fonts: skipped $colr: it draws nothing for U+1F579"; fi
if [ -f "$cjk" ]; then han="Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to $cjk (face 0), first for U+4E2D"
elif [ -f "$cjkvf" ]; then han="Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to $cjkvf (face 0, named instance 4), first for U+4E2D"; fi
emoji=$(grep -m1 -E '^Fonts: the 36 pt titles fall back to [^ ]*/DejaVuSans\.ttf \(face 0\), first for U\+1F600$' "$log")
ok=1
[ -n "$skip" ] && [ -n "$han" ] && [ -n "$emoji" ] && [ "$(grep -cxF "$skip" "$log")" = 1 ] \
    && precedes "$log" "$skip" "$emoji" && grep -qxF "$han" "$log" && ran_clean f72-real && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: on the image's fonts the color emoji font is skipped, 😀 falls back to DejaVu Sans and 中 to Noto Sans CJK (exit $(cat "$out/f72-real.code"))" $ok
echo "      expected: ${skip:-no color emoji font found}; ${han:-no Noto Sans CJK found}"
grep '^Fonts:' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# Shrink mode over box glyphs: each title keeps one scratch set, opened at its smaller size with its
# fallbacks and resized step by step, so every size measured carries the fallback the drawn one does.
# 90-titles-fit.sh then finds no title wider than its button in this run either.
FONTCONFIG_FILE=$TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts.conf run_quick f72-shrink
log=$out/f72-shrink.log
smaller=$(grep -E '^Fonts: the [0-9]+ pt titles fall back to .*/sf-box\.ttf \(face 0\), first for U\+' "$log" | grep -vc '^Fonts: the 78 pt ')
ok=1
grep -q "Menu 'Main': 3 x 1 grid, 556 px buttons, 78 pt titles" "$log" \
    && grep -q "Fonts: the 78 pt titles fall back to $TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts/sf-box.ttf (face 0), first for U+4E2D" "$log" \
    && [ "${smaller:-0}" -ge 1 ] && ! grep -q 'px wide, over its' "$log" && ran_clean f72-shrink && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: Shrink measures CJK titles with their fallback at every size and fits them (exit $(cat "$out/f72-shrink.code"))" $ok
grep -E '^Fonts:|px wide, over its' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'

# Shrink when every step down fails (the harness build's STREAMFLEX_TEST_FAIL_SHRINK_STEP): the scratch
# set is closed, its font before its fallback, and the title is cut at the menu's size
FONTCONFIG_FILE=$TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts.conf STREAMFLEX_TEST_FAIL_SHRINK_STEP=1 CFG=$FX/f72-shrink.ini run_quick f72-shrinkfail
log=$out/f72-shrinkfail.log
ok=1
grep -q 'Test hook: the step down to [0-9]* pt fails' "$log" && ! grep -q 'px wide, over its' "$log" \
    && ran_clean f72-shrinkfail && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: a failed step down closes the scratch set and still cuts the title to fit (exit $(cat "$out/f72-shrinkfail.code"))" $ok
grep -E 'Test hook|px wide, over its' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
```

In `src/image.h`, add `#include "fontset.h"` as its first line, and in `TextInfo` (`:11-22`) after `TTF_Font *font;`:


Change `:27` and `:43` to:

```c

FontSet *title_font(int size);
```

In `src/image.c`, replace `:56-124` (from `#define MAX_TITLE_FONTS 16` through `title_fonts_keep`'s closing brace) with:

```c
#define MAX_TITLE_FONTS 16

// Title font sets by point size, for menus whose titles scale with their buttons
static struct {
    int size;
    FontSet *set;
} title_fonts[MAX_TITLE_FONTS];
static int title_font_count = 0;

// A function to get the title font's set at a point size, opening it the first time. Each size is a
// set of its own, its fallbacks attached at that size as its titles need them; the cache keeps
// MAX_TITLE_FONTS of them and closes the oldest to make room.
FontSet *title_font(int size)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < title_font_count; i++) {
        if (title_fonts[i].size == size)
            return title_fonts[i].set;
    }
    FontSet *set = fontset_open(title_info.font_path, (float) size, title_info.font_face, "titles");
#ifdef STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS
    // Only the headless harness builds this: STREAMFLEX_TEST_FAIL_TITLE_SIZE names a size that fails
    const char *fail = getenv("STREAMFLEX_TEST_FAIL_TITLE_SIZE");
    if (fail != NULL && atoi(fail) == size && set != NULL) {
        fontset_close(set);
        set = NULL;
        SDL_SetError("the test hook failed it");
    }
#endif
    if (set == NULL) {
        log_error("Could not open the title font at %i pt\n%s", size, SDL_GetError());
        return NULL;
    }
    if (title_font_count == MAX_TITLE_FONTS) {
        fontset_close(title_fonts[0].set);
        memmove(&title_fonts[0], &title_fonts[1], (MAX_TITLE_FONTS - 1) * sizeof(title_fonts[0]));
        title_font_count--;
    }
    title_fonts[title_font_count].size = size;
    title_fonts[title_font_count].set = set;
    title_font_count++;
    return set;
}

// A function to close every cached title set
void title_fonts_free(void)
{
    for (int i = 0; i < title_font_count; i++)
        fontset_close(title_fonts[i].set);
    title_font_count = 0;
}

// A function to close the cached title sets at sizes not in a list: when settings close, the
// sizes no menu uses any more
void title_fonts_keep(const int *sizes, int count)
{
    int kept = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < title_font_count; i++) {
        bool used = false;
        for (int j = 0; j < count && !used; j++)
            used = title_fonts[i].size == sizes[j];
        if (used)
            title_fonts[kept++] = title_fonts[i];
        else {
            log_debug("Titles: closed the %i pt title font, which no menu uses now", title_fonts[i].size);
            fontset_close(title_fonts[i].set);
        }
    }
    title_font_count = kept;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Prepare every title before it is measured, and give Shrink one scratch set**

Replace `render_text` (`image.c:499-599`, as Task 1.2 left it) with the function below. Only its font lines change; the surface calls are shown as Task 1.2 ported them, and Task 1.2's are kept where they differ:

```c
// A function to render text. A title's set gets the fallbacks of every character its font lacks
// before the text is first measured; the clock's TextInfo has no set, and draws in its one font.
SDL_Surface *render_text(const char *text, TextInfo *info, SDL_Rect *rect, int *text_height)
{
    TTF_Font *output_font = NULL;
    FontSet *reduced = NULL; // Shrink mode's scratch set: opened once, then resized a step at a time
    int w, h;

    // Copy text into new buffer in case we need to manipulate it
    char *text_buffer = strdup(text);

    // Calculate size of the rendered title, through its fallbacks
    fontset_prepare(info->set, text_buffer);
    TTF_GetStringSize(info->font, text_buffer, 0, &w, &h);

    // If title is too large to fit
    if (info->oversize_mode != OVERSIZE_NONE && w > info->max_width) {

        // Truncate mode:
        if (info->oversize_mode == OVERSIZE_TRUNCATE)
            truncate_to_fit(info->font, text_buffer, info->max_width, &w, &h);

        // Shrink mode: work out the size that fits from the measured width, never going below the
        // readable minimum, then cut whatever still does not fit. The text keeps one scratch set, so
        // every size it measures carries the same fallbacks as the size it draws.
        else if (info->oversize_mode == OVERSIZE_SHRINK) {
            int size = info->font_size * info->max_width / w;
            if (size < info->min_size)
                size = info->min_size;
            if (size > 0 && size < info->font_size) {
                reduced = fontset_open(info->font_path, (float) size, info->font_face, "titles");
                fontset_prepare(reduced, text_buffer);

                // The font's own rounding can leave it a pixel or two too wide: step down to the minimum
                while (reduced != NULL) {
                    TTF_GetStringSize(fontset_font(reduced), text_buffer, 0, &w, &h);
                    if (w <= info->max_width || size <= info->min_size)
                        break;
                    bool resized = fontset_resize(reduced, (float) --size);
#ifdef STREAMFLEX_TEST_HOOKS
                    // Only the headless harness builds this: every step down fails
                    if (getenv("STREAMFLEX_TEST_FAIL_SHRINK_STEP") != NULL && resized) {
                        log_debug("Test hook: the step down to %i pt fails", size);
                        resized = false;
                    }
#endif
                    if (!resized) {
                        fontset_close(reduced);
                        reduced = NULL;
                    }
                }
            }

            // A step down that failed leaves the title at the menu's size, and w from the size the
            // scratch set had; truncate_to_fit() measures it again in the font it is drawn in
            if (w > info->max_width)
                truncate_to_fit(reduced != NULL ? fontset_font(reduced) : info->font, text_buffer, info->max_width, &w, &h);
        }
    }
    output_font = reduced != NULL ? fontset_font(reduced) : info->font;

    // Render surface
    SDL_Surface *surface = NULL;
    if (info->shadow) {
        int shadow_offset = layout_shadow_offset(h);
        SDL_Surface *foreground = TTF_RenderText_Blended(output_font, text_buffer, 0, *info->color);
        SDL_Surface *shadow = TTF_RenderText_Blended(output_font, text_buffer, 0, *info->shadow_color);
        surface = SDL_CreateSurface(foreground->w + shadow_offset, foreground->h + shadow_offset, SDL_PIXELFORMAT_ARGB8888);
        SDL_FillSurfaceRect(surface, NULL, SDL_MapSurfaceRGBA(surface, 0, 0, 0, 0));
        SDL_Rect shadow_rect = {shadow_offset, shadow_offset, shadow->w, shadow->h};
        SDL_BlitSurface(shadow, NULL, surface, &shadow_rect);
        SDL_Rect foreground_rect = {0, 0, foreground->w, foreground->h};
        SDL_BlitSurface(foreground, NULL, surface, &foreground_rect);
        SDL_DestroySurface(foreground);
        SDL_DestroySurface(shadow);
    }
    else
        surface = TTF_RenderText_Blended(output_font, text_buffer, 0, *info->color);

    // Set geometry
    rect->w = surface->w;
    rect->h = surface->h;
    if (info->oversize_mode == OVERSIZE_SHRINK && text_height != NULL)
        *text_height = h;

    // Clean up: the scratch set's font first, then its fallbacks (fontset_close())
    fontset_close(reduced);
    free(text_buffer);

    return surface;
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Open the titles as sets in `load_font`, and leave the clock one font**

Replace `load_font` (`image.c:644-691`, its comment included, as Task 1.2 left it) with:

```c
// A function to open load_font()'s font at info->font_size: a set for a role, one font without
static void open_text_font(TextInfo *info, const char *path, int face, const char *role)
{
    if (role != NULL) {
        info->set = fontset_open(path, (float) info->font_size, face, role);
        info->font = fontset_font(info->set);
    }
    else
        info->font = open_font_file(path, info->font_size, face);
}

// A function to open a text's font: the configured file and face (a relative path is also tried
// beside the executable), else the bundled font. The configured path is never changed: what was
// opened is kept in info->font_path, and a failure says so in the log. A role ("titles") opens it as
// a font set, whose fallbacks fontset_prepare() attaches; NULL (the clock) opens one font. A font the
// TextInfo still holds is closed first, so a reload leaks nothing: the caller must drop any other
// pointer to it (launcher.c's fixed_title_font), and info->set must not be one of title_font()'s
// cached sets.
int load_font(TextInfo *info, const char *configured, int face, const char *default_font, const char *role)
{
    if (info->set != NULL)
        fontset_close(info->set);   // Its font is info->font
    else if (info->font != NULL)
        TTF_CloseFont(info->font);
    info->set = NULL;
    free(info->font_path);
    info->font_path = NULL;
    info->font = NULL;
    info->font_face = 0;
    if (configured != NULL) {
        open_text_font(info, configured, face, role);
        if (info->font != NULL)
            info->font_path = strdup(configured);

        // A relative path in the config means the folder StreamFlex is in, not the one it was started
        // from (the Windows config names .\assets\fonts\...; a shortcut's "Start in" folder can be anywhere)
        else if (config.exe_path != NULL && is_relative_path(configured)) {
            char exe_font_path[MAX_PATH_CHARS + 1];
            join_paths(exe_font_path, sizeof(exe_font_path), 2, config.exe_path, configured);
            open_text_font(info, exe_font_path, face, role);
            if (info->font != NULL)
                info->font_path = strdup(exe_font_path);
        }
        if (info->font != NULL)
            info->font_face = face;
        else
            log_error("Could not open the font %s (face %i), using the default font\n%s", configured, face, SDL_GetError());
    }
    if (info->font == NULL) {
        char *default_font_path = find_default_font(default_font);
        if (default_font_path != NULL) {
            open_text_font(info, default_font_path, 0, role);
            if (info->font == NULL)
                log_error("Could not open the default font %s\n%s", default_font_path, SDL_GetError());
        }
        if (info->font == NULL) {
            free(default_font_path);
            log_fatal("Could not load default font");
            return 1;
        }
        info->font_path = default_font_path;
    }
    return 0;
}
```

Append to `tests/headless/checks/72-fallback-fonts.sh`:

```bash
# The settings screen: its fonts are sets too. The Menus list's widths are measured when settings
# open, so the CJK menu name gives the 30 pt row font (2.8 % of 1080) its fallback there
FONTCONFIG_FILE=$TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts.conf UNTIL='Settings opened over menu' run_keys f72-settings Menu
log=$out/f72-settings.log
ok=1
[ "$(grep -cxF "Fonts: the 30 pt settings rows fall back to $TESTER_HOME/fcbox/fonts/sf-box.ttf (face 0), first for U+6E38" "$log")" = 1 ] \
    && ran_clean f72-settings && ok=0
result "fallback fonts: a CJK menu name falls back on the settings screen (exit $(cat "$out/f72-settings.code"))" $ok
grep '^Fonts:' "$log" | sed 's/^/      /'
```

Append to `tests/headless/checks/90-titles-fit.sh`:

```bash

# Fallback fonts (spec section 4): no run but the fallback checks' own (f72-*) attached a fallback or
# met a character no font has, so every other check's exact pixels are drawn as before. f59-symbols is
# the one exception, by design: it chooses Dingy, a fixture font with glyphs for "DingyAa0" alone, as
# the title font, so its titles' other letters now come from fallbacks.
fell=$(grep -lE '^Fonts: (the [0-9]+ pt .* fall back to |no installed font draws )' "$out"/*.log 2> /dev/null \
    | grep -vE "/(f72-[^/]*|f59-symbols)\.log$")
ok=1
[ -z "$fell" ] && ok=0
result "no run outside the fallback checks logged a fallback font" $ok
for f in $fell; do grep -HE '^Fonts: (the [0-9]+ pt .* fall back to |no installed font draws )' "$f" | sed "s|^$out/||; s/^/      /"; done
```

In `src/settings_screen.c`, add `#include "fontset.h"` after `#include "image.h"` (`:22`), and after `:79` add:

```c
static FontSet *set_header = NULL;    // The three fonts' sets: each font above is its set's, fallbacks attached
static FontSet *set_row = NULL;
static FontSet *set_small = NULL;
```

Replace `text_width` (`:143-151`, as Task 1.2 left it) with:

```c
// A function to get the set a settings font belongs to; NULL for any other font
static FontSet *set_of(TTF_Font *font)
{
    if (font == NULL)
        return NULL;
    if (font == font_row)
        return set_row;
    if (font == font_small)
        return set_small;
    return font == font_header ? set_header : NULL;
}

// A function to measure a line of text, the fallbacks of the characters its font lacks attached first
static int text_width(TTF_Font *font, const char *text)
{
    int w = 0;
    int h = 0;
    fontset_prepare(set_of(font), text);
    if (text[0] != '\0')
        TTF_GetStringSize(font, text, 0, &w, &h);
    return w;
}
```

`cached_text`'s comment (`:153`) becomes `// A function to get a line of text as a texture, from the cache when it was drawn recently; its text is prepared, as draw_text() measures it first`.

In `draw_wrapped` (`:222-240`) and `wrapped_height` (`:1173-1185`), add as the line before each `TTF_RenderText_Blended_Wrapped(...)` call:

```c
    fontset_prepare(set_of(font), text);
```

In `open_fonts()` (`:425-432`), replace from `float height = (float) geo.screen_height;` through `return font_header != NULL && ...;` with:

```c
    float height = (float) geo.screen_height;
    set_header = fontset_open(path, (float) max_int(8, (int) (HEADER_FONT_RATIO * height)), 0, "settings headers");
    set_row = fontset_open(path, (float) max_int(8, (int) (ROW_FONT_RATIO * height)), 0, "settings rows");
    set_small = fontset_open(path, (float) max_int(8, (int) (SMALL_FONT_RATIO * height)), 0, "settings notes");
    font_header = fontset_font(set_header);
    font_row = fontset_font(set_row);
    font_small = fontset_font(set_small);
    if (font_header == NULL || font_row == NULL || font_small == NULL)
        log_error("Settings cannot open: could not open the font %s\n%s", path, SDL_GetError());
    free(bundled);
    return font_header != NULL && font_row != NULL && font_small != NULL;
}

// A function to close the screen's three font sets, each font before its fallbacks (fontset_close()).
// A set that never opened is NULL, which fontset_close() passes over.
static void close_fonts(void)
{
    fontset_close(set_header);
    fontset_close(set_row);
    fontset_close(set_small);
    set_header = NULL;
    set_row = NULL;
    set_small = NULL;
    font_header = NULL;
    font_row = NULL;
    font_small = NULL;
```

(the closing brace of `close_fonts` is `open_fonts`'s old one). In `free_screen()`, replace `:768-776` (the three `if (font_...) TTF_CloseFont(font_...);` pairs and the three `= NULL` lines) with `    close_fonts();`, after `clear_text_cache();` as now. In Task 1.6's `settings_reflow()`, replace its close of the three fonts (the same `TTF_CloseFont(font_header)`, `font_row`, `font_small` lines and their `NULL`s) with `close_fonts();`, keeping its `clear_text_cache()` before it and its `open_fonts()` after it. **Interfaces:**

Two claims have no headless half, on purpose: 15.12 (a Latin-only config opens no fallback) is proven by `90-titles-fit.sh`'s all-logs check, which has no run the register can cite, so its Linux half is `manual`; and no smoke row cites `f47-display`, `f48-sizereset`, `f72-real` or any `67-wayland.sh` run (a test hook, or a claim that turns on the desktop's own fonts or compositor).
- Produces: nothing another task consumes. qa-harness (its item 75) keys on the new ids, so none of them may change once this merges.

**Rules this task follows** (`tests/qa/smoke-test.md` header, `CONTRIBUTING.md:149`, `design/specs/2026-10-10-smoke-doc-design.md`):
- One observable claim per row. Where § 9 joins two claims in one clause ("reports charging and shows none"), they are two rows.
- A row has exactly one tag. Where § 9 names a pair no tag covers (`[Ubuntu]` `[Kubuntu]`, `[Fedora]` `[Fedora-KDE]`), the row is `[Linux]` with per-desktop steps, or one row per desktop.
- **A row's tag is part of its claim, so a tag change retires the row and adds a new one** (`CONTRIBUTING.md:149`). Spec § 9 says 5.48's tag "becomes `[Linux]`"; that is done as retire 5.48, add 5.48a `[Linux]` in its place. 1.7's claim (SDL2) stops being true, so it too is retired and replaced, by 1.7a.
- New rows take the next free number at the end of their module, or a letter suffix when they replace a retired row in place. No id is renumbered.
- The honesty rule (`automation-coverage.md:30`): a half is `streamflex-headless` only when the check asserts the row's claim itself; a desktop-specific claim (Wayland focus, a GNOME notice, the fonts a desktop ships) is never headless. Every Linux half that waits on qa-harness reads exactly `qa-harness item 75, planned`; a half no lane can run (a real GPU, a real pad, a second display) is `manual` with its reason, which never mentions qa-harness.

- [ ] **Step 2: Confirm the check and run behind every headless half**

```powershell
$checks = 'C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex/tests/headless/checks'
$want = @(('60-timing.sh','f60-icons'), ('70-chroma.sh','f70-chroma'),
          ('72-fallback-fonts.sh','f72-empty'), ('72-fallback-fonts.sh','f72-settings'),
          ('72-fallback-fonts.sh','f72-shrink'),
          ('47-screen.sh','f47-resize'), ('47-screen.sh','f47-appwait'), ('47-screen.sh','f47-settings'), ('47-screen.sh','f47-picker'),
          ('66-pad-power.sh','f66-power'), ('66-pad-power.sh','f66-right'))
foreach ($w in $want) {
    $file = Join-Path $checks $w[0]
    # The same rule check-smoke.py's check_evidence applies: a run_* call, not a comment, naming the run
    $rule = '^[^#]*\brun_(?:keys_at\s+\S+|(?!keys_at\b)[a-z_]+)\s+' + [regex]::Escape($w[1]) + '(\s|$)'
    if (-not (Test-Path $file)) { "MISSING FILE $($w[0])" }
    elseif (-not (Select-String -Path $file -Pattern $rule -Quiet)) { "MISSING RUN  $($w[1]) in $($w[0])" }
    else { "ok           $($w[0]) run $($w[1])" }
}
```

Reset the config, and start with `-d`. **Frame**, and zoom on the top right corner. | The log shows `Gamepad power: instance id <N>, wireless, on battery, 10%` and `Battery indicator: shown, critical, 10% at <X>,<Y> <W>x<H>`, and a red battery glyph is drawn at that rectangle. |

- [ ] **Step 6: `docs/configuration.md`: Transparent Backgrounds, split Linux and Windows**

Replace `docs/configuration.md:912-933`, from `## Transparent Backgrounds` through the `### Windows Implenetation` paragraph (whose CPU-load warning describes the chroma key's layered window and no longer holds), with the block below. `### Animated Backgrounds` (`:935`) and `### Custom Widgets` (`:938`) stay as they are, after it. The Linux part keeps the old chroma-key paragraphs word for word, except its first sentence and the shader bullet, which no longer need to say "Linux".

````markdown
## Transparent Backgrounds
Transparent mode lets the desktop, or a program running behind StreamFlex, show through the background. Windows and Linux do it in different ways.

### Transparent on Windows
On Windows, every pixel of the window carries its own opacity. The background is fully see-through, and icons, titles and the highlight are drawn over it as over any other background, their soft edges blending into the desktop. No key color is involved: [ChromaKeyColor](#chromakeycolor) is ignored, the settings screen shows no *See-through color* row, and no color in your icons, text or highlight can turn see-through.

- The window is one pixel row shorter than the display, and Windows treats it as fullscreen, so the taskbar never draws over icons or titles. A taskbar behind the window shows through the see-through background, as the rest of the desktop does, and the missing bottom row shows the desktop.
- A background [Overlay](#overlay) tints the desktop at its [OverlayOpacity](#overlayopacity). At 100% it hides the desktop. The screensaver's dim darkens the desktop the same way, at its dim level.
- The see-through area still belongs to StreamFlex: a mouse click there goes to StreamFlex, not to the window behind it.
- StreamFlex draws a Transparent background with Vulkan where the graphics driver offers the alpha it needs, and with Direct3D 11 otherwise. Setting the `SDL_RENDER_DRIVER` environment variable to `vulkan` or `direct3d11` chooses one of them. No other renderer can draw a transparent window, so with `SDL_RENDER_DRIVER` set to another (`direct3d12`, `direct3d` or `opengl`), StreamFlex shows the Color background instead, and its debug log says why.
- Per-pixel transparency does not work on every machine. SDL's own documentation says it "does not work on all hardware configurations", such as laptops whose display is driven by one GPU while another one renders. Where it does not work, choose another background.

### Transparent on Linux
*This needs a compositor. See the [Linux Setup Guide](https://bilbospocketses.github.io/streamflex/setup_linux#transparent-backgrounds) for details. To see whether SDL chose X11 or Wayland, and which renderer, run StreamFlex with debug logging (`-d`): the log's `Video:` line at startup names both.*

On Linux, StreamFlex makes the background transparent with the chroma key technique. This method works by setting a strategically chosen color to the background, which is removed later. In film production, this technique is often refered to as "blue screening" or "green screening".

Every pixel of the window that is exactly the chroma key color becomes transparent. StreamFlex keeps icons clear of it: as an icon loads, any opaque pixel within one step of the key in every channel is moved two steps away from it (with the default key, near-black becomes `#030303`). Text and the colors you configure are not changed, so choose a key color that your fonts, highlight and other colors do not use. The color is set with the `ChromaKeyColor` setting in the Background section. The default is `#010101`, a slight off-shade of black.

A limitation of this method is that your icons and text must be fully opqaue or fully transparent. Any semi-transparent pixels will blend with the chroma key background so that it does not produce a color match, and consequently will not be removed from the background.

StreamFlex's text rendering is anti-aliased, which gives the text a "feathered" look with semi-transparent pixels on the edges. Normally, this is desirable, but in the case of chroma keying, semi-transparent pixels will cause your chroma key background color to "bleed through" on the edges of the text. If bright blue or bright green is chosen as the chroma key, this will result in a blue or green glowing effect around the text, which is usually undesirable. This is the reason why a dark color is chosen as the default chroma key. The bleed though appears as a dark outline rather than as a bright glowing.

Some icons have shadows which are intended to provide a textured look. The shadows are usually semi-transparent, which will cause them to not render properly with the chroma key technique. You should choose icons without shadows, or manually erase the shadows from an icon if there are no other icons available for the given application. Some icons are also heavily anti-aliased, which can give a glowing or outline effect similar to the text rendering described above. Icons from the [Icon Library](icons) have smooth, partly transparent edges, so a faint outline in the chroma key color can show around them.

Another common issue is the highlight. The default highlight is semi-transparent, which will not render properly when blended with the chroma key background. There are a few ways to address with this:
- Set the `FillOpacity` setting to 0%, and use an outline-only highlight instead
- Use custom [Selected Icons](#selected-icon-overrides) in place of StreamFlex's highlight
- Use a shader to recover the highlight's transparency. See the [Linux Setup Guide](https://bilbospocketses.github.io/streamflex/setup_linux#transparent-backgrounds) for details.

Transparent backgrounds will require some effort to obtain a setup that looks good and works well. Be prepared to do a significant amount of tinkering if you wish to use this feature.
````

The notes state this figure (spec § 2). It is measured by building the same commit twice in CI, with and without HarfBuzz, on the same runner image, and comparing the two `Windows build` artifacts. Nothing from this step merges.

1. The zip with HarfBuzz: the branch head's own CI run. Its head SHA must equal the local `HEAD`:

```powershell
$repo = 'C:/Users/jscha/source/repos/streamflex'
$out = 'C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-gates/zip-size'
```

If the newest run's `headSha` is not `HEAD`, push first and wait for its `Windows` job; a zip from an older commit measures another build.

2. The zip without HarfBuzz: a throwaway branch in a worktree outside the checkout, so the shared working tree is untouched:

```powershell

```

In `C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-gates/no-harfbuzz/vcpkg.json`, remove `"harfbuzz"` from the `sdl3-ttf` entry's `features`; if it was the only feature, the entry becomes the bare string `"sdl3-ttf"`. Then read the `sdl3-ttf` overlay port's own `vcpkg.json` (Task 1.1's overlay folder): if its `default-features` names `harfbuzz`, add `"default-features": false` to the manifest's `sdl3-ttf` entry instead.

In that worktree's `.github/workflows/build.yml`, under the Windows job's `- name: Unit tests` step, add the line `        continue-on-error: true` (at the step's indentation), so the job still uploads the zip when test 5 (HarfBuzz present) fails, as it must here.

```powershell
$wt = 'C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-gates/no-harfbuzz'
git -C $wt add vcpkg.json .github/workflows/build.yml
git -C $wt commit -m "spike: the Windows zip without HarfBuzz, to measure its size (throwaway, never merged)"
git -C $wt push -u origin spike/no-harfbuzz
gh workflow run build.yml --repo bilbospocketses/streamflex --ref spike/no-harfbuzz
```

Wait for that run's `Windows` job. Expected: its `Unit tests` step reports Task 1.1's HarfBuzz test (spec § 9 test 5) **failed** and every other test passed. That failure is the control: it proves this zip has no HarfBuzz. A run where the HarfBuzz test passes measured nothing; find what still enabled the feature before going on. Then:

```powershell
gh run download <that run id> --repo bilbospocketses/streamflex -n "Windows build" -D "$out/without"
```

3. Measure both:

```powershell
Add-Type -AssemblyName System.IO.Compression.FileSystem
$sizes = @{}
foreach ($k in 'with', 'without') {
    $zip = Get-ChildItem "$out/$k/*.zip" | Select-Object -First 1
    $archive = [IO.Compression.ZipFile]::OpenRead($zip.FullName)
    $exe = $archive.Entries | Where-Object { $_.Name -eq 'streamflex.exe' } | Select-Object -First 1
    $sizes[$k] = @{ zip = $zip.Length; exe = $exe.Length }
    $archive.Dispose()
    '{0}: {1} zip {2} bytes, streamflex.exe {3} bytes' -f $k, $zip.Name, $zip.Length, $exe.Length
}
$zipMB = [math]::Round($sizes.with.zip / 1MB, 1)
$deltaMB = [math]::Round(($sizes.with.zip - $sizes.without.zip) / 1MB, 1)
$exeDeltaMB = [math]::Round(($sizes.with.exe - $sizes.without.exe) / 1MB, 1)
"zip $zipMB MB, HarfBuzz +$deltaMB MB; exe +$exeDeltaMB MB"
```

Write all four byte counts and the three rounded figures into the ledger (`progress.md`), with both run ids.

4. Remove the throwaway branch, by name:

```powershell
git -C $repo push origin --delete spike/no-harfbuzz
git -C $repo worktree remove C:/Users/jscha/ClaudeScratch/streamflex-sdl3-gates/no-harfbuzz
git -C $repo branch -D spike/no-harfbuzz
```

- [ ] **Step 9: The release notes under `[Unreleased]`**

`CHANGELOG.md:9-18` today: the heading, the SDL2 notice paragraph (`:11`), and a `### Changed` list of five items (`:13-18`). The notice stays the first paragraph (Global Constraints: v0.5.0 and the next two releases open with it). After the edit the section reads, in this order, with the five existing `### Changed` items kept word for word at the end of `### Changed`:

```markdown
## [Unreleased]

v0.4.1 is the last release built on SDL2. From v0.5.0, StreamFlex is built on SDL3 and needs Debian trixie, Raspberry Pi OS trixie or Ubuntu 25.10 (or a newer release of each), Arch Linux, or Windows. Debian bookworm, Ubuntu 24.04 LTS and older releases package no SDL3, so users there stay on v0.4.1, which stays downloadable; there is no backport.

StreamFlex now runs on SDL3 and draws with Vulkan first. Text the title font cannot draw comes from fallback fonts, Linux desktops with a current compositor run StreamFlex as a native Wayland client, the screen stays sharp at any display scale and follows a change of resolution, a gamepad low on its battery shows a small glyph, and on Windows the Transparent background is see-through pixel by pixel, with soft edges. A config written for 0.4.x reads and saves as before.

### Added
- **Vulkan first.** StreamFlex asks SDL for `vulkan,direct3d12,direct3d11,opengl` on Windows and `vulkan,opengl` on Linux and the Raspberry Pi, and SDL takes the first that works; on SDL2 it drew with Direct3D 9 on Windows and OpenGL on Linux. The debug log names the renderer (`Renderer: vulkan`) and says when it is not the first choice (`Renderer: opengl (vulkan was unavailable)`). The `SDL_RENDER_DRIVER` environment variable chooses another renderer (`Renderer: opengl (from SDL_RENDER_DRIVER)`); there is no setting for it.
- **A lost graphics device is recovered.** When the GPU is reset or lost (a driver update, a GPU reset), StreamFlex rebuilds everything it draws and keeps the menu and the settings screen as they were. When the renderer itself is lost, StreamFlex makes a new one, up to five tries 2 seconds apart while it keeps answering input; when the loss comes while a launched app runs, it waits for the app to end first.
- **Fallback fonts.** A title, menu name or settings value in a script the font lacks (Chinese, Japanese, Korean, Indic, Thai, Arabic, Hebrew, symbols and emoji) is drawn from a font that has it, instead of as boxes: on Linux from the fonts fontconfig offers, on Windows from Windows' own fonts (Segoe UI, Segoe UI Emoji, Yu Gothic, Microsoft YaHei, Malgun Gothic, Nirmala UI and others, the CJK font first chosen by the preferred language), and from the bundled DejaVu Sans last. The clock keeps its one font. A character no installed font has still shows as a box, and the log says so once, even without `-d`: `Fonts: no installed font draws U+1F579; it shows as a box`. A config whose text the font covers opens no other font. There is no setting.
- **Native Wayland.** SDL now chooses the video driver: Wayland when the compositor offers `wp_fifo_manager_v1` (GNOME 48 and later, KDE Plasma 6.4 and later), X11 otherwise. Ubuntu and Kubuntu 25.10 and later, Fedora, and Debian trixie's GNOME run StreamFlex as a native Wayland client; Debian trixie's Plasma 6.3 stays on XWayland, and the debug log says why. The `SDL_VIDEO_DRIVER` environment variable (or SDL2's `SDL_VIDEODRIVER`) still forces a driver. The Wayland app id and the X11 window class are `streamflex`, matching `streamflex.desktop`, however StreamFlex was started.
- **Sharp at any display scale.** On a scaled Wayland output StreamFlex draws at the output's native pixels, and on Windows it is per-monitor DPI aware (v2). The OS scale is logged (`Screen: 3840x2160 px, pixel density 1.00, display scale 1.50`) but not applied: a setting in pixels or points keeps meaning pixels and points, and the layout is already sized as a share of the screen.
- **A change of screen size is handled.** A resolution change, a TV switching mode, a monitor plugged in or a move to another display lays StreamFlex out again at the new size, keeping the menu, and the settings screen with its page and unsaved changes; an open picker or folder browser closes as Back would. A change while a launched app runs waits until the app ends, so a game that switches the resolution and back costs nothing. On SDL2, StreamFlex never handled a size change.
- **A low-battery glyph for gamepads.** While a connected pad runs on its battery at 40 % or below, a small battery glyph shows in the top corner opposite the clock: amber, and red at 15 % or below. It hides again above 45 %, and shows nothing for a pad that is charging, on its cable, or reports no battery. With several pads it shows the lowest. There is no setting.
- **Startup timing in the debug log** (`-d`). `Timing:` lines give the time a menu's icons and titles took, split into reading, decoding and making textures, the time of a background image, and the time from the window opening to the first menu shown.

### Changed
- **StreamFlex is built on SDL3** (SDL3 3.2.10, SDL3_image 3.2.4 and SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 or later), and the Linux packages on Debian trixie. The `.deb` depends on `libsdl3-0`, `libsdl3-image-0`, `libsdl3-ttf-0` and `libfontconfig1`, the Arch package on `sdl3`, `sdl3_image`, `sdl3_ttf` and `fontconfig`. The Windows zip is still one static executable, with no DLLs.
- **Transparent mode on Windows is per-pixel alpha.** The background is truly see-through, and the soft edges of icons, titles and the highlight blend into the desktop with no key color, so the chroma key's holes in dark art cannot occur. `ChromaKeyColor` is ignored on Windows (it is still read and saved, so a config shared with Linux keeps it), and the settings screen shows no *See-through color* row there. The window is one pixel row short of the display and marked fullscreen, so the taskbar never draws over icons or titles; a taskbar behind the window shows through the see-through background, and the missing row shows the desktop. It draws with Vulkan, or with Direct3D 11 where the driver's Vulkan offers no premultiplied alpha. Switching to or from Transparent in settings recreates the window, keeping the page and its edits. A Transparent window no renderer can draw (with `SDL_RENDER_DRIVER` set to `direct3d12`, `direct3d` or `opengl`, for example) falls back to the Color background instead of exiting. The see-through area now takes mouse clicks. Transparent on Linux is unchanged: the chroma key, with a compositor's shader on X11.
- **A background overlay now tints the desktop in Transparent mode on Windows,** at its opacity. Under the chroma key an overlay made the Transparent background fully opaque, because no pixel stayed the key color. The screensaver's dim does the same: it darkens the desktop at its dim level instead of turning it black.
- **Text is shaped with HarfBuzz on Windows too,** as every Linux build already was: Arabic joins and runs right to left, Indic scripts reorder, emoji sequences combine, and kerning applies.
- **PlayStation pads run in SDL's enhanced mode.** SDL3 switches DualShock 4 and DualSense pads to their enhanced reports, which over Bluetooth are the only ones that carry the battery, and StreamFlex keeps SDL's default. Once switched, a pad stays so until it is powered off, and a launched app that reads it through DirectInput without SDL may misread it until then.
- **The Windows manifest declares per-monitor v2 DPI awareness** (it was per-monitor v1), and the debug log says which one the process has: `DPI awareness: per-monitor v2`.
- **The debug log's `Resolution:` is the window's size in pixels,** which a fullscreen window shares with the display; on Windows in Transparent mode it is one row less.
- <the five existing items, `CHANGELOG.md:14-18`, unchanged and in their order>

### Known limits
- Linux draws emoji in monochrome, from a font such as DejaVu Sans: SDL3_ttf 3.2.2 cannot scale the color emoji fonts Linux ships (Noto Color Emoji is a bitmap font, and Fedora's COLRv1 emoji draw nothing), so StreamFlex skips them. Windows draws emoji in color, from Segoe UI Emoji.
- On Linux, a character drawn from a fallback font can sit slightly above or below the line (Noto Sans CJK measured 4 px low at 48 pt), and can make a title a few pixels taller than its font's height. The Windows build carries a fix for it (SDL_ttf issue #629); Linux uses the distribution's SDL3_ttf.
- A title mixing left-to-right and right-to-left text is not reordered: SDL_ttf has no bidirectional algorithm. Such a title is also shaped wrong when the title font itself covers the right-to-left script.
- A gamepad's battery shows only where the pad and the platform report one. On Linux that is only through SDL's HIDAPI, which needs udev rules giving the user the pad's `hidraw` node (Steam's, or `game-devices-udev`); without them, no glyph shows.
- HarfBuzz makes the Windows zip <deltaMB> MB larger; the zip is <zipMB> MB.
- Transparent on Windows may not work on every machine: SDL notes that per-pixel transparency "does not work on all hardware configurations", such as laptops whose display is driven by one GPU while another one renders. It was checked on one machine, whose display its graphics card drives.
- On Windows, a graphics driver whose Vulkan offers no premultiplied alpha for a window draws Transparent with Direct3D 11.
- The Raspberry Pi package is built, and its metadata checked, in CI; it was not checked by hand on a Pi.
```

On Linux, Transparent needs a compositor (picom on X11), and GNOME/Wayland shows no see-through at all." The same narrowing goes into `:85`'s "(b) ... making `Transparent` work without the tinkering (sub-project 4)", which gains "on Linux". Both touched lines use American spelling ("colors", not the line's current British form), per the spelling rule for touched text.
5. **No edit now:** the 3c spec (`design/specs/2026-09-29-menu-editing-design.md`) names no release, and its text-input line is re-read against SDL3 when 3c's research runs, as the todo already requires.

## Decided before the measurement

- **Which configs the timing gate judges (user, 2026-10-10).** The user's Windows machine has no StreamFlex install with a real config, which the spec assumed, so the bundle runs the shipped default (4 entries) and two heavy configs (a 24-entry home menu and a 48-entry submenu, over a 4K image or a 4K slideshow). Threshold 1 is judged on `default`; threshold 2 on `default` and the heavy configs' 24-icon `Main`; the 48-icon `Big` is information only (Task 0.5, Step 2).
- **Phase 0 runs inline** (user, 2026-10-10): the controller does Tasks 0.1-0.3 and 0.5 itself; the port (Phase 1 onward) is subagent-driven.
